U_{\tau}
linearly increases with
\tau
, and hence the commutator norm
\left\|\left[\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right),u_{L}\right]\right\|=\left\|\left[\beta H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau},U_{\tau}^{\dagger}u_{L}U_{\tau}\right]\right\|
is roughly upper-bounded
by
\Theta(\beta|L[\tau]|)=\Theta(\beta|L|\tau^{D})
.
Thus, the integral with respect to
\tau_{1}
converges as
\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{\tau-\tau_{1}}\Theta(\beta|L|\tau_{1}^{D})d\tau_{1}
, which makes
\mathcal{\chi}_{\tau,\rho,L^{\rm c}}
of order of
\beta|L|\tau^{D}

*3
*3

*3

The actual estimation of
\mathcal{\chi}_{\tau,\rho,L^{\rm c}}
is provided in Lemma
38
, which is worse than
\mathcal{O}(\beta|L|\tau^{D})
but is still
{\rm poly}(\beta,|L|,\tau)
.

Thus, the first term in the RHS of (
S.203
), i.e.,
16e^{-\tau}\left(\mathcal{D}_{C}\mathcal{\chi}_{\tau,\rho,AB}+\mathcal{D}_{A}\mathcal{\chi}_{\tau,\rho,BC}+\mathcal{D}_{AC}\mathcal{\chi}_{\tau,\rho,B}\right)
, becomes sufficiently small by choosing
\tau\gtrsim|AC|
.
However, when
|A|
or
|C|
is macroscopically large (i.e.,
|A|,|C|=\mathcal{O}(|\Lambda|)
), we cannot obtain a meaningful upper bound for the second term
\left\|H_{\rho,\tau}(A:C|B)\right\|
(see Lemma
39
below).

S.III.3.1
Proof of Lemma
16

For an arbitrary base
\{\ket{\phi_{s}}\}_{s}
, the von Neumann entropy
S(\rho)
is upper-bounded by
[
163
,
164
,
165
]

\displaystyle S(\rho)\leq-\sum_{s}p_{\phi,s}\log(p_{\phi,s}),\quad\sum_{s}p_{\phi,s}=1,

(S.204)

where
p_{\phi,s}:=\bra{\phi_{s}}\rho\ket{\phi_{s}}
.
Note that the equation is achieved iff
\{\ket{\phi_{s}}\}_{s}
are given by the eigenbase of
\rho
.

We start with the spectral decomposition of
\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}
as

\displaystyle\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}=\sum_{s}\lambda_{s}\ket{s}\bra{s},

(S.205)

where
\{\ket{s}\}
are the eigenstate states of
\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}
.
We consider

\displaystyle\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau+d\tau}=\frac{e^{-d\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\rho e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}e^{-d\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}}{{\rm tr}\left(e^{-d\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\rho e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}e^{-d\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\right)}.

(S.206)

We obtain

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\left(e^{-d\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\rho e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}e^{-d\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\right)=

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\left(e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\rho e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\right)-{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\rho e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\right)2d\tau+\mathcal{O}(d\tau^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\left(e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\rho e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\right)\left[1-2{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)d\tau\right]+\mathcal{O}(d\tau^{2}),

(S.207)

which reduces Eq. (
S.206
) to

\displaystyle\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau+d\tau}=\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}-\{\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau},{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\}d\tau+2{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}d\tau+\mathcal{O}(d\tau^{2}).

(S.208)

Using Eq. (
S.205
), we calculate

\displaystyle\bra{s}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau+d\tau}\ket{s}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{s}-2\lambda_{s}\bra{s}{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\ket{s}d\tau+2\lambda_{s}{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)d\tau+\mathcal{O}(d\tau^{2}),

(S.209)

and

\displaystyle\log\left(\bra{s}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau+d\tau}\ket{s}\right)

\displaystyle=\log\left(\lambda_{s}\right)+\log\left(1-2\bra{s}{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\ket{s}d\tau+2{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)d\tau+\mathcal{O}(d\tau^{2})\right)

\displaystyle=\log\left(\lambda_{s}\right)+2\bra{s}{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\ket{s}d\tau-2{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)d\tau+\mathcal{O}(d\tau^{2}).

(S.210)

Then, by using the inequality (
S.204
), we upper-bound
S(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau+d\tau})
as

\displaystyle S(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau+d\tau})\leq-\sum_{s}\bra{s}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau+d\tau}\ket{s}\log\left(\bra{s}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau+d\tau}\ket{s}\right)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{s}\left[\lambda_{s}-2\lambda_{s}\bra{s}{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\ket{s}d\tau+2\lambda_{s}{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)d\tau\right]\cdot\left[\log\left(\lambda_{s}\right)+2\bra{s}{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\ket{s}d\tau-2{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)d\tau\right]+\mathcal{O}(d\tau^{2})

\displaystyle=S(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau})-2d\tau\sum_{s}\lambda_{s}\left[\bra{s}{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\ket{s}-{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]+2d\tau\sum_{s}\lambda_{s}\left[\bra{s}{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\ket{s}-{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]\log(\lambda_{s})+\mathcal{O}(d\tau^{2})

\displaystyle=S(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau})+2d\tau\sum_{s}\lambda_{s}\left[\bra{s}{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\ket{s}-{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]\log(\lambda_{s})+\mathcal{O}(d\tau^{2}),

(S.211)

where we use
\sum_{s}\lambda_{s}\left[\bra{s}{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\ket{s}-{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]=0

from
\sum_{s}\lambda_{s}\bra{s}{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\ket{s}={\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\sum_{s}\lambda_{s}\ket{s}\bra{s}\right)={\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)

in the last equation.
For the second term in the RHS of (
S.211
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s}\lambda_{s}\left[\bra{s}{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\ket{s}-{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]\log(\lambda_{s})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s}\bra{s}{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\ket{s}-{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\cdot{\rm tr}\left[\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\log(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau})\right]

\displaystyle={\rm tr}\left[{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]-{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\cdot{\rm tr}\left[\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\log(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau})\right].

(S.212)

Combining (
S.211
) and (
S.212
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\frac{S(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau})}{d\tau}\right|\leq 2\left|{\rm tr}\left\{\left[{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}-{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right\}\right|.

(S.213)

The upper bound is expressed as a bipartite correlation between
\log(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau})
and
\mathcal{Q}_{L}
, which is upper-bounded by
\mathcal{O}(|L^{\rm c}|)
in general, as the entanglement Hamiltonian for
\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}
is supported on
L^{\rm c}
. If the clustering theorem holds, only the surface term around the region
L
of
\log(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau})
contributes to the upper bound (
S.213
). Unfortunately, we cannot exploit the clustering property, as high-dimensional Gibbs states may exhibit long-range correlations at thermal critical points.

To estimate the RHS of the inequality (
S.213
), we first prove

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\left\{\left[{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}-{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}O_{L^{\rm c}}\right\}=0

(S.214)

for an arbitrary operator
O_{L^{\rm c}}
that is supported on
L^{\rm c}
.
Because of
{\rm tr}_{L}\left(\cdots O_{L^{\rm c}}\right)=O_{L^{\rm c}}{\rm tr}_{L}\left(\cdots\right)
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}O_{L^{\rm c}}\right)

\displaystyle={\rm tr}_{L^{\rm c}}\left\{O_{L^{\rm c}}{\rm tr}_{L}\left[\left(1-{\mathcal{P}}_{L}\right)\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle={\rm tr}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[O_{L^{\rm c}}{\rm tr}_{L}\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}-{\mathcal{P}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}{\mathcal{P}}_{L}\right)\right]

\displaystyle={\rm tr}\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}-{\mathcal{P}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}{\mathcal{P}}_{L}\right){\rm tr}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[O_{L^{\rm c}}{\rm tr}_{L}\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]={\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right){\rm tr}\left(O_{L^{\rm c}}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right),

(S.215)

where we use
{\rm tr}_{L}\left({\mathcal{P}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}{\mathcal{P}}_{L}\right)\propto{\rm tr}_{L}\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)
in the third equation from Eq. (
S.188
) and

\left\|\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}-{\mathcal{P}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}{\mathcal{P}}_{L}\right\|_{1}={\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)
in the fourth equation.
The above equation immediately yields Eq. (
S.214
).

By using the fact that the operator
\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L}\left[\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]
is supported on
L^{\rm c}
, we obtain from Eq. (
S.214
)

\displaystyle 2\left|{\rm tr}\left\{\left[{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}-{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle=2\left|{\rm tr}\left\{\left[{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}-{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\left(\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L}\left[\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|\left[{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}-{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right\|_{1}\cdot\left\|\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L}\left[\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 4{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\left\|\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L}\left[\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 16\mathcal{D}_{L}e^{-\tau}\left\|\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L}\left[\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]\right\|,

(S.216)

where, in the last inequality, we use

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)=1-{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{P}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}{\mathcal{P}}_{L}\right)=1-\left\|{\mathcal{P}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}{\mathcal{P}}_{L}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 1-\left(\left\|\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty}\right\|_{1}-\left\|{\mathcal{P}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}{\mathcal{P}}_{L}-\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty}\right\|_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\|{\mathcal{P}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}{\mathcal{P}}_{L}-\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty}\right\|_{1}\leq 4\mathcal{D}_{L}e^{-\tau},

(S.217)

which is derived from
\left\|{\mathcal{P}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}{\mathcal{P}}_{L}-\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty}\right\|_{1}\leq\left\|\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}-\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty}\right\|_{1}\leq 4\mathcal{D}_{L}e^{-\tau}
by (
S.194
).
Note that
\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty}=\rho_{L^{\rm c}}\otimes{\mathcal{P}}_{L}={\mathcal{P}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty}{\mathcal{P}}_{L}
.
By applying the inequality (
S.216
) to (
S.213
) and integrating it with
\tau
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|S(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty})-S(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau})\right|\leq\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\left|\frac{S(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau_{1}})}{d\tau_{1}}\right|d\tau_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 16\mathcal{D}_{L}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{-\tau_{1}}\left\|\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau_{1}}\right)-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L}\left[\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau_{1}}\right)\right]\right\|d\tau_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 16\mathcal{D}_{L}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{-\tau_{1}}\sup_{u_{L}}\left\|\left[\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau_{1}}\right),u_{L}\right]\right\|d\tau_{1}.

(S.218)

To connect the upper bound (
S.218
) to
S(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty}|\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau})
, we need to estimate

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\left[\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty}\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)-\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]=\int_{\tau}^{\infty}{\rm tr}\left[\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau_{1}+d\tau_{1}}-\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau_{1}}\right)\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right].

(S.219)

By using Eq. (
S.208
), we obtain

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\left[\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau_{1}+d\tau_{1}}-\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau_{1}}\right)\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]={\rm tr}\left[\left(-\{\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau_{1}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\}+2{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau_{1}}\right)\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau_{1}}\right)\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]d\tau_{1}.

(S.220)

Using the same analyses as in the inequality (
S.216
), we have

\displaystyle\left|{\rm tr}\left[\left(-\{\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau_{1}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\}+2{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau_{1}}\right)\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau_{1}}\right)\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left|{\rm tr}\left\{\left[{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}-{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau_{1}}\right)\right]\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau_{1}}\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 16\mathcal{D}_{L}e^{-\tau_{1}}\left\|\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L}\left[\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]\right\|.

(S.221)

By applying Eq. (
S.220
) and the inequality (
S.221
) to Eq. (
S.219
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|{\rm tr}\left[\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty}\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)-\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq 16\mathcal{D}_{L}\left\|\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L}\left[\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]\right\|\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{-\tau_{1}}d\tau_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 16\mathcal{D}_{L}e^{-\tau}\sup_{u_{L}}\left\|\left[\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right),u_{L}\right]\right\|.

(S.222)

By combining the inequalities (
S.218
) and (
S.222
),
we prove the main inequality (
S.198
) from

\displaystyle S\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty}|\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)

\displaystyle={\rm tr}\left[\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty}\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty}\right)-\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty}\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]

\displaystyle={\rm tr}\left[\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty}\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty}\right)-\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]-{\rm tr}\left[\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty}\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)-\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left|S(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty})-S(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau})\right|+\left|{\rm tr}\left[\rho_{L^{\rm c},\infty}\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)-\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\log\left(\rho_{L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)\right]\right|.

(S.223)

This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
16
]

S.IV
Connection of exponential operators

In the practical use of the BP formalism or the PTP formalism for the entanglement Hamiltonian as shown in Sec.
S.III
, we have to estimate the logarithm of the product of exponential operators.
To make the problem clearer, we here consider a set of operators
\{{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N}
and calculate the logarithm of

\displaystyle\Psi_{N}:=e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}_{N}}\cdots e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}_{1}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}_{1}}\cdots e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}_{N}}\quad(N\propto 1/\epsilon),

(S.224)

where we choose
\epsilon
infinitesimally small, which makes
N
infinitely large (i.e.,
N\to\infty
).

In the BP formalism for one-dimensional systems (see Sec.
S.III.2
), we need to consider the logarithm of Eq. (
S.173
), that is,

\displaystyle\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{XY}e^{\beta\left(\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{X}+H_{L[\ell]^{\rm c}}\right)}\tilde{\Phi}_{XY}^{\dagger}\right)=\log\left[\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{1}\tilde{\phi}_{XY,\tau}d\tau}e^{\beta\left(\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{X}+H_{L[\ell]^{\rm c}}\right)}\left(\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{1}\tilde{\phi}_{XY,\tau}d\tau}\right)^{\dagger}\right].

(S.225)

Therefore, we choose

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}\to\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{X}+H_{L[\ell]^{\rm c}},\quad\{{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N}:=\{\tilde{\phi}_{XY,j/N}\}_{j=1}^{N},\quad\epsilon=\frac{1}{N}\quad\textrm{for}\quad N\to\infty.

(S.226)

where we define
\{{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N}
as the discretization of the function
\tilde{\phi}_{XY,\tau}
.

On the other hand, in the PTP formalism for high-dimensional systems (see Sec.
S.III.3
), we need to consider the logarithm of
Eq. (
S.197
) with the choice of
\rho=e^{\beta H}
:

\displaystyle\log\left({\mathcal{P}}_{L,\tau}e^{\beta H}{\mathcal{P}}_{L,\tau}\right)=\log\left(e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}e^{\beta H}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\right),

(S.227)

where we use the definition of Eq. (
S.190
), i.e.,
{\mathcal{P}}_{L,\tau}:=e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}:=1-{\mathcal{P}}_{L}
.
We thus choose

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}\to H,\quad\{{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N}:={\mathcal{Q}}_{L},\quad\epsilon=\frac{\tau}{N}\quad\textrm{for}\quad N\to\infty.

(S.228)

Our purpose here is to investigate the property of
\log(\Psi_{N})
, which is derived from the sequential estimations of the logarithm of

\displaystyle\Psi_{m+1}=e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}_{m+1}}\Psi_{m}e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}_{m+1}}=e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}_{m+1}}e^{\log(\Psi_{m})}e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}_{m+1}}.

(S.229)

For this purpose, we prove the following convenient lemma, which plays a key role in our analyses:

Lemma 18
.

For arbitrary operators in the form of

\displaystyle e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}},

(S.230)

we obtain the logarithm as

\displaystyle\log(e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}})=\beta e^{-2i\epsilon\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{A}}e^{2i\epsilon\mathcal{C}}+2\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2}),

(S.231)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}:=\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{\beta}(t)e^{i{\mathcal{A}}t}{\mathcal{B}}e^{-i{\mathcal{A}}t}dt.

(S.232)

where
g_{\beta}(t)
has been defined in the context of the belief propagation (
S.92
).

S.IV.0.1
Proof of Lemma
18

For the proof, we start from the belief propagation (
S.91
) as follows:

\displaystyle e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}+2\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}}=\hat{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}},\epsilon}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\hat{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}},\epsilon}^{\dagger},

(S.233)

where
\hat{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}},\epsilon}
is obtained from Eqs. (
S.90
) and (
S.91
)

\displaystyle\hat{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}},\epsilon}=\exp\left[\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}+\frac{2i\epsilon}{\beta}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{\beta}(t)e^{i{\mathcal{A}}t}{\mathcal{B}}e^{-i{\mathcal{A}}t}dt\right]=e^{2i\epsilon\mathcal{C}}e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2}),

(S.234)

where we use the definition of the operator
{\mathcal{C}}
.
By applying the explicit form of
\hat{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}},\epsilon}
to Eq. (
S.233
), we have

\displaystyle e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}}

\displaystyle=e^{-2i\epsilon\mathcal{C}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}+2\epsilon\beta{\mathcal{B}}}e^{2i\epsilon\mathcal{C}}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})

\displaystyle=\exp\left[\beta e^{-2i\epsilon\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{A}}e^{2i\epsilon\mathcal{C}}+2\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\right].

(S.235)

This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
18
]

Remark.

We emphasize that the same analyses cannot be applied to
e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}}
.
Usually, the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula (
S.94
) gives

\displaystyle\log(e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}})=\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\epsilon\left(\frac{\beta{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}{1-e^{-\beta{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}}{\mathcal{B}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2}).

(S.236)

Here, the Taylor expansion of the operator
\frac{{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}{1-e^{-\beta{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}}B
gives

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}{1-e^{-\beta{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}}{\mathcal{B}}=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{{\mathcal{B}}_{m}}{m!}(\beta{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}})^{m}B,

(S.237)

where
\{{\mathcal{B}}_{m}\}_{m=0}^{\infty}
are the Bernoulli numbers, which grow as
(cm)^{\mathcal{O}(m)}
with
c
a positive constant.
Unfortunately, we cannot utilize the expansion because it is not a convergent series unless
\|{\mathcal{A}}\|
and
\|{\mathcal{B}}\|
are sufficiently small.
Due to this fact, theoretical analyses of the quasi-locality estimation based on the BCH expansion are notoriously challenging.
On the other hand, if we apply the BCH formula to
\log(e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}})
, we have

\displaystyle\log(e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}})=\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\epsilon\left(\frac{\beta{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}{1-e^{-\beta{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}}{\mathcal{B}}+{\rm h.c.}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2}),

(S.238)

where we use the same equation in Eq. (
S.94
) for the derivation of the belief propagation.
By applying the spectral expressions as in Eq. (
S.96
) and (
S.97
), we can derive

\displaystyle\frac{\beta{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}{1-e^{-\beta{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}}{\mathcal{B}}+{\rm h.c.}=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{A}},t)\left(\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{\beta\omega}{\tanh(\beta\omega/2)}e^{-i\omega t}d\omega\right)dt,

(S.239)

which gives the equivalent expression to Eq. (
S.231
).
The primary difference in considering
\log(e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}})
is that we need to take
the Fourier transform of
\frac{\beta\omega}{1-e^{-\beta\omega}}
instead of
\frac{\beta\omega}{\tanh(\beta\omega/2)}
, which is not well defined.
We also show another proof of Lemma
18
in Appendix
A
using the perturbation theory for the operator logarithm.

By connecting Lemma
18
iteratively, we obtain the following corollary:

Corollary 19
.

The logarithm of the operator

\displaystyle e^{\tau{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}e^{\tau{\mathcal{B}}}

(S.240)

is expressed as

\displaystyle\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\tau}:=\log(e^{\tau{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}e^{\tau{\mathcal{B}}})=U_{\tau}(\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\hat{{\mathcal{B}}}_{\tau})U_{\tau}^{\dagger},

(S.241)

where we define

\displaystyle U_{\tau}:=\mathcal{T}e^{-i\int_{0}^{\tau}{\mathcal{C}}_{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{1}},\quad\hat{{\mathcal{B}}}_{\tau}:=2\int_{0}^{\tau}U_{\tau_{1}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{B}}U_{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{1},\quad{\mathcal{C}}_{\tau}:=\frac{2}{\beta}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{\beta}(t){\mathcal{B}}(\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\tau},t)dt.

(S.242)

Remark.
When we consider
\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{\tau}{\mathcal{B}}_{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{1}}
instead of
e^{\tau{\mathcal{B}}}
, we have the same statement, but the operators
\hat{{\mathcal{B}}}_{\tau}
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{\tau}
are replaced by

\displaystyle\hat{{\mathcal{B}}}_{\tau}:=2\int_{0}^{\tau}U_{\tau_{1}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{B}}_{\tau_{1}}U_{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{1},\quad{\mathcal{C}}_{\tau}:=\frac{2}{\beta}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{\beta}(t){\mathcal{B}}_{\tau}(\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\tau},t)dt.

(S.243)

Proof of Corollary
19
.

We assume the form of Eq. (
S.241
) for
\tau\leq x
and prove the case of
\tau=x+dx
.
Here, we denote

\displaystyle e^{\beta\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{x}}=e^{x{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}e^{x{\mathcal{B}}},\quad e^{\beta\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{x+dx}}=e^{(x+dx){\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}e^{(x+dx){\mathcal{B}}}e^{dx{\mathcal{B}}}=e^{dx{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{x}}e^{dx{\mathcal{B}}}.

(S.244)

Then, Lemma
18
gives the form of
\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{x+dx}
as

\displaystyle\beta\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{x+dx}

\displaystyle=\beta e^{-idx\mathcal{C}_{x}}\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{x}e^{idx\mathcal{C}_{x}}+2dx{\mathcal{B}}+\mathcal{O}(dx^{2})

\displaystyle=e^{-idx\mathcal{C}_{x}}\left(\beta\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{x}+2dx{\mathcal{B}}\right)e^{idx\mathcal{C}_{x}}+\mathcal{O}(dx^{2})

\displaystyle=e^{-idx\mathcal{C}_{x}}\left(U_{x}(\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\hat{{\mathcal{B}}}_{x})U_{x}^{\dagger}+2dx{\mathcal{B}}\right)e^{idx\mathcal{C}_{x}}+\mathcal{O}(dx^{2})

\displaystyle=U_{x+dx}\left(\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\hat{{\mathcal{B}}}_{x}+2dxU_{x}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{B}}U_{x}\right)U_{x+dx}^{\dagger}+\mathcal{O}(dx^{2}),

(S.245)

which yields the desired equation (
S.241
) for
\tau=x+dx
.
This completes the proof of Corollary
19
.
\square

S.IV.1
Refined error bound for approximate quantum Belief propagation

In this subsection, we consider the approximation of the belief propagation operator
\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}
in Eq. (
S.86
) as

\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}
as

\displaystyle\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}:=\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{1}\tilde{\phi}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}d\tau},

(S.246)

where
\tilde{\phi}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}
is Hermitian.
In Lemma
9
, we have proved the error bound for the approximation of

\displaystyle\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\approx\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}.

(S.247)

However, we cannot usually ensure that the logarithms of
\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}
and
\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}
are close to each other.
Using Lemma
18
, we aim to estimate the error for the approximation of

\displaystyle\log\left(\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)\approx\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right).

(S.248)

This kind of approximation is critical in estimating the conditional mutual information (see Lemma
14
).
We prove the following lemma:

Proposition 20
.

Let us define
\tilde{\phi}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}
as in Eq. (
S.246
) such that
\|\phi_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}-\tilde{\phi}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\|\leq\delta\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|/2
for
\forall\tau
.
Then, the norm difference between
\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}
and
\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}
is upper-bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|\log\left(\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|\leq 3\mathcal{N}_{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}}}(\beta\nu_{1}+1)e^{\beta\nu_{2}}\delta,

(S.249)

where we define
\mathcal{N}_{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}}}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}}}:=\max(4\pi,\beta\left\|{\mathcal{A}}\right\|+\beta\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|),

(S.250)

and choose
\nu_{1}
and
\nu_{2}
as

\displaystyle\nu_{1}=4\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\log\left(\mathcal{N}_{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}}}\right),\quad\nu_{2}=\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\left(14\log\left(\mathcal{N}_{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}}}\right)+1\right).

(S.251)

Remark.

From the leading term in the inequality (
S.249
), we can upper-bound

\displaystyle\left\|\log\left(\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\log\left(\beta\left\|{\mathcal{A}}\right\|\right)}\delta

(S.252)

by using the
\Theta(1)
notation in Eq. (
S.14
).
Compared with the upper bound in (
S.108
), the additional logarithmic term
\log\left(\beta\left\|{\mathcal{A}}\right\|\right)
appears in the exponential.
In applying the proposition to many-body systems, the norm
\left\|{\mathcal{A}}\right\|
becomes as large as the system size
|\Lambda|
, which makes the upper bound meaningless.
To avoid it, we need to use

\displaystyle\left\|\log\left(\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|\leq\left\|\log\left(\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\left\|\log\left(\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)-\left[\log\left(\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)\right]\right\|,

(S.253)

where
{\mathcal{A}}_{L}
consists of interaction terms that act on the subsets
L\subset\Lambda
[see Eq. (
S.8
)].

For the first term, we apply Proposition
20
, which now yields
\mathcal{N}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{L},{\mathcal{B}}}=\mathcal{O}(\beta|L|)
.
More precisely, for
\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}
, we can no longer ensure
\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}=e^{\beta\left({\mathcal{A}}_{L}+{\mathcal{B}}\right)}
. Hence, we need to consider

\displaystyle e^{\beta\left({\mathcal{A}}_{L}+{\mathcal{B}}\right)}=\Phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\Phi^{{}^{\prime}\dagger}_{{\mathcal{B}}},

(S.254)

and decompose as

\displaystyle\left\|\log\left(\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\log\left(\Phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\Phi^{{}^{\prime}\dagger}_{{\mathcal{B}}}\right)-\log\left(\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|+\left\|\log\left(\Phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\Phi^{{}^{\prime}\dagger}_{{\mathcal{B}}}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|,

(S.255)

where each of the above terms can be treated by Proposition
20
.
Regarding the second term in the RHS of the above inequality (
S.253
), it is generally uncontrollable by the choice of
L
.
If we assume the quasi-locality of the operators
{\mathcal{A}}
(and
{\mathcal{B}}
), we can ensure that those terms exponentially decay with the size of
L
(see Theorem
2
and Lemma
41
).

S.IV.1.1
Proof of Proposition
20

For the proof, we adopt the ansatz of

\displaystyle\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\tilde{\Phi}^{\dagger}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right):=u_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left(\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right)u_{\tau},

(S.256)

with

\displaystyle\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}:=\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{\tau}\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}d\tau},\quad\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}:=\frac{\tilde{\phi}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau/\beta}}{\beta}\quad(0\leq\tau\leq\beta),

(S.257)

where
u_{\tau}
is appropriately chosen in an iterative way [see Eq. (
S.267
) below].
Note that
\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}},\beta}=\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{\beta}\tilde{\phi}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau/\beta}(d\tau/\beta)}=\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{1}\tilde{\phi}_{{\mathcal{B}},x}dx}=\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}
.
We now aim to estimate the norms of
\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|
and
\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|
in the form of

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|\leq Q(\tau)\delta,\quad\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|\leq Q(\tau)\delta,\quad Q(\tau):=(\tau\nu_{1}+1)e^{\tau\nu_{2}},

(S.258)

where we choose
\nu_{1}
and
\nu_{2}
appropriately, and it will be eventually set as in Eq. (
S.251
).
For the proof of the above inequalities, it is enough to prove

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|}{d\tau}\right|\leq\frac{\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}Q(\tau)}{2}\delta,\quad\left|\frac{d\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|}{d\tau}\right|\leq\frac{\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}Q(\tau)}{2}\delta,

(S.259)

since
\delta\int_{0}^{\tau}\left[\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}Q(\tau_{1})\right]d\tau_{1}\leq\tau\nu_{1}\delta+(\tau\nu_{1}+1)\delta\int_{0}^{\tau}\nu_{2}e^{\tau_{1}\nu_{2}}d\tau\leq 2Q(\tau)\delta
.
Under the inequality (
S.258
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\log\left(\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\beta\left({\mathcal{A}}+{\mathcal{B}}\right)-u_{\beta}^{\dagger}\left(\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\beta{\mathcal{B}}+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\beta}\right)u_{\beta}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|u_{\beta}-1\right\|\cdot\beta\left(\left\|{\mathcal{A}}\right\|+\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\right)+\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\beta}\right\|\leq 3\mathcal{N}_{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}}}Q(\beta)\delta.

(S.260)

In the following, we aim to prove the inequality (
S.258
).
First of all, for
\tau=0
, we have
\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|=\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|=0
, and the inequalities in (
S.258
) are trivially satisfied.
We then assume the inequalities (
S.258
) up to a fixed
\tau
and consider the case of
\tau+d\tau
:

\displaystyle\log\left[e^{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}d\tau}e^{u_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left(\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right)u_{\tau}}e^{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}d\tau}\right]=u_{\tau+d\tau}^{\dagger}\left(\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}+d\tau{\mathcal{B}}+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau+d\tau}\right)u_{\tau+d\tau},

(S.261)

where the unitary operator
u_{\tau+d\tau}
is defined as in Eq. (
S.267
) using
u_{\tau}

We now need to prove the inequality (
S.259
).
We estimate the upper bounds of
\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau+d\tau}\right\|
and
\left\|u_{\tau+d\tau}-1\right\|
in terms of
\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|
and
\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|
, respectively.
By using Lemma
18
, we obtain

\displaystyle\log\left[e^{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}d\tau}e^{u_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left(\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right)u_{\tau}}e^{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}d\tau}\right]=e^{-2i\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}u_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left(\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right)u_{\tau}e^{2i\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}+2d\tau\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau},

(S.262)

where we have ignored the terms of order of
\mathcal{O}(d\tau^{2})
and define
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}
as in Eq. (
S.231
):

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}:=\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{\beta}(t)\cdot\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\left[u_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right)u_{\tau},t\right]dt,

(S.263)

where we have defined
\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\left[u_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right)u_{\tau},t\right]=e^{itu_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right)u_{\tau}}\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}e^{-itu_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right)u_{\tau}}
.

Because the following equation exactly holds,

\displaystyle\log\left[e^{\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}d\tau}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}d\tau}\right]=\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}+d\tau{\mathcal{B}},

(S.264)

we have

\displaystyle e^{-2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}\left(\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}\right)e^{2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}+2d\tau\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}=\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}+d\tau{\mathcal{B}},

(S.265)

where
\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}
are defined in the same ways as in Eqs. (
S.257
) and (
S.263
), respectively.
By applying Eq. (
S.264
) to (
S.262
), we have

\displaystyle e^{-2i\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}u_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left(\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right)u_{\tau}e^{2i\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}+2d\tau\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-2i\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}u_{\tau}^{\dagger}e^{2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}\left(e^{-2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}\left(\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right)e^{2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}\right)e^{-2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}u_{\tau}e^{2i\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}+2d\tau\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-2i\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}u_{\tau}^{\dagger}e^{2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}\left(\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}+d\tau{\mathcal{B}}-2d\tau\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}+e^{-2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}e^{2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}\right)e^{-2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}u_{\tau}e^{2i\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}+2d\tau\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{\tau+d\tau}^{\dagger}\left(\beta{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}+d\tau{\mathcal{B}}-2d\tau\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}+e^{-2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}e^{2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}+2d\tau u_{\tau+d\tau}\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}u_{\tau+d\tau}^{\dagger}\right)u_{\tau+d\tau},

(S.266)

where we define

\displaystyle u_{\tau+d\tau}:=e^{-2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}u_{\tau}e^{2i\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}.

(S.267)

Using the notation of
u_{\tau+d\tau}
, the above equation gives
\Delta A_{\tau+d\tau}
as

\displaystyle\Delta A_{\tau+d\tau}=e^{-2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}e^{2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}+2d\tau\left(u_{\tau+d\tau}\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}u_{\tau+d\tau}^{\dagger}-\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right),

(S.268)

whose upper bound is given by

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta A_{\tau+d\tau}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|+2d\tau\left\|\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}-\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|+2d\tau\left\|u_{\tau+d\tau}\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}u_{\tau+d\tau}^{\dagger}-\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|+2d\tau\left\|\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}-\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|+4d\tau\left\|\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|\cdot\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|,

(S.269)

where we use
u_{\tau+d\tau}d\tau=u_{\tau}d\tau+\mathcal{O}(d\tau^{2})
.
We therefore obtain using the assumption (
S.258
)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\left\|\Delta A_{\tau+d\tau}\right\|-\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|}{d\tau}\right|\leq\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\left(\delta+2\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|\right)\leq\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\left[1+2Q(\tau)\right]\delta,

(S.270)

where we use
\left\|\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|\leq\left\|\phi_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau/\beta}\right\|/\beta\leq\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|/2
from Eq. (
S.257
)
and the inequality (
S.109
) and
\left\|\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}-\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|\leq\delta\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|/2
from the initial condition.

We then estimate
\left\|u_{\tau+d\tau}-1\right\|
as

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}u_{\tau}e^{2i\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}-1\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|e^{2i\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}\left(u_{\tau}-1\right)e^{-2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}+e^{2i\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}e^{-2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}-1\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|+\left\|e^{2i\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}e^{-2i\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}d\tau}-1\right\|\leq\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|+2d\tau\left\|\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}-\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}\right\|,

(S.271)

which gives

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|u_{\tau+d\tau}-1\right\|-\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|}{d\tau}\leq 2\left\|\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}-\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}\right\|.

(S.272)

To estimate the RHS of the above inequality, we prove the lemma as follows:

Lemma 21
.

For an arbitrary
\tau
, we prove the upper bound of

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}-\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}\right\|\leq\frac{\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|}{\pi}\log\left(\mathcal{N}_{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}}}\right)\left(2\delta+10\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|\right)+\frac{1}{4}\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\cdot\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|.

(S.273)

We show the proof in Sec.
S.IV.1.2
.

By applying the inequality (
S.273
) to (
S.272
) with the use the assumption (
S.258
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|u_{\tau+d\tau}-1\right\|-\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|}{d\tau}\leq\frac{2\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|}{\pi}\log\left(\mathcal{N}_{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}}}\right)\left(2+10Q(\tau)\right)\delta+\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|Q(\tau)\delta

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\leq 2\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\log\left(\mathcal{N}_{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}}}\right)\delta+\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\cdot\left[7\log\left(\mathcal{N}_{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\right]Q(\tau)\delta.

(S.274)

Therefore, the inequalities (
S.270
) and (
S.274
) reduce to the form of (
S.259
) under the choices in Eq. (
S.251
), that is,

\nu_{1}=4\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\log\left(\mathcal{N}_{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}}}\right)
and
\nu_{2}=\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\left(14\log\left(\mathcal{N}_{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}}}\right)+1\right)
.
We thus prove the inequality (
S.258
), which also proves the inequality (
S.260
).
This completes the proof of Proposition
20
.
\square

S.IV.1.2
Proof of Lemma
21

For the norm of
\left\|\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime},\tau}-\mathcal{C}_{\phi^{\prime},\tau}\right\|
, we have to treat the integral with the filter function
g_{\beta}(t)
, which diverges to infinity as
1/|t|
.
So, as in Eq. (
S.310
) below, we need to decompose the time evolution to
|t|\leq\Delta_{t}
and
|t|>\Delta_{t}
, where
\Delta_{t}
is chosen appropriately afterward.
We, in the following, treat the two integrals:

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{|t|>\Delta_{t}}g_{\beta}(t)\left\{\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\left[u_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right)u_{\tau},t\right]-\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\left({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta,t\right)\right\}dt\right\|

(S.275)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{|t|\leq\Delta_{t}}tg_{\beta}(t)\int_{0}^{1}\left\{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\left[u_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right)u_{\tau},\lambda t\right]-\psi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\left({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta,\lambda t\right)\right\}d\lambda dt\right\|,

(S.276)

where we denote
\psi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}={\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta}\left(\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right)
and

\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}={\rm ad}_{u_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right)u_{\tau}}\left(\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right)
.

Before analyzing (
S.275
) and (
S.276
),
we obtain the following upper bound for arbitrary operators
\tilde{O}
and
O
:

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{O}\left[u_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right)u_{\tau},t\right]-O\left({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta,t\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\tilde{O}-O\right\|+\left(4\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|+2t\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right\|\right)\cdot\left\|O\right\|,

(S.277)

which is derived from the following two inequalities:

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{O}\left[u_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right)u_{\tau},t\right]-O\left[u_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right)u_{\tau},t\right]\right\|\leq\left\|\tilde{O}-O\right\|.

(S.278)

and

\displaystyle\left\|O\left[u_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right)u_{\tau},t\right]-O\left({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta,t\right)\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|u_{\tau}^{\dagger}e^{i({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta)t}u_{\tau}Ou_{\tau}^{\dagger}e^{-i({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta)t}u_{\tau}-e^{i({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta)t}Oe^{-i({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta)t}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(4\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|+2\left\|e^{i({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta)t}-e^{i({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta)t}\right\|\right)\cdot\left\|O\right\|\leq\left(4\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|+2|t|\cdot\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right\|\right)\cdot\left\|O\right\|,

(S.279)

where we use

\displaystyle e^{i({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta)t}=e^{i({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta)t}\mathcal{T}e^{i\beta^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta,-x)dx}.

(S.280)

We apply the inequality (
S.277
) to (
S.275
) and (
S.276
) to prove the inequality (
S.273
).

Using the inequality (
S.358
) in Lemma
26
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{|t|>\Delta_{t}}|g_{\beta}(t)|dt\leq\frac{2\beta}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\beta}{2\pi\Delta_{t}}\right),\quad\int_{|t|>\Delta_{t}}|tg_{\beta}(t)|dt\leq 2\left(\frac{\beta}{2\pi}\right)^{2}\zeta(2)=\frac{\beta^{2}}{12}\quad\textrm{for}\quad\Delta_{t}\leq\frac{\beta}{4\pi},

(S.281)

and hence, we obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{t_>_delta_t_phi_g_beta}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{|t|>\Delta_{t}}|g_{\beta}(t)|\left[\left\|\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}-\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|+\left(4\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|+2|t|\cdot\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right\|\right)\cdot\left\|\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|\right]dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\beta}{2\pi\Delta_{t}}\right)\left(\left\|\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}-\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|+4\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|\cdot\left\|\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|\right)+\frac{1}{6}\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|\cdot\left\|\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\beta}{2\pi\Delta_{t}}\right)\left(\delta+4\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|\right)+\frac{\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|}{12}\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|,

(S.282)

where in the last inequality we use
\left\|\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|\leq\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|/2
and
\left\|\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}-\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|\leq\delta\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|/2
as in the inequality (
S.270
).

In the same way, using the inequality (
S.359
) in Lemma
26
,
we have

\displaystyle\int_{|t|\leq\Delta_{t}}|tg_{\beta}(t)|dt\leq\frac{\beta}{\pi}\Delta_{t},\quad\int_{|t|\leq\Delta_{t}}|t^{2}g_{\beta}(t)|dt\leq\frac{\beta}{2\pi}\Delta_{t}^{2},

(S.283)

which yields

\displaystyle\eqref{t_le_delta_t_phi_g_beta}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{|t|\leq\Delta_{t}}|tg_{\beta}(t)|\int_{0}^{1}\left[\left\|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}-\psi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|+\left(4\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|+2|\lambda t|\cdot\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right\|\right)\cdot\left\|\psi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|\right]dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{t}}{\pi}\left(\left\|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}-\psi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|+4\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|\cdot\left\|\psi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|\right)+\frac{\Delta_{t}^{2}}{\pi\beta}\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|\cdot\left\|\psi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|.

(S.284)

To estimate the norm of
\psi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}-\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}
, we calculate as

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}-\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}

\displaystyle={\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta}\left(\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right)-{\rm ad}_{u_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right)u_{\tau}}\left(\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right)

\displaystyle={\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta}\left(\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}-\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right)+{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta}\left(\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right)-{\rm ad}_{u_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right)u_{\tau}}\left(\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right),

(S.285)

which yields

\displaystyle\left\|\psi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}-\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta\right\|\cdot\left\|\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}-\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|+2\left\|\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|\cdot\left\|{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta-u_{\tau}^{\dagger}\left({\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta+\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right)u_{\tau}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\left\|{\mathcal{A}}\right\|+\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\right)\cdot\left\|\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}-\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|+2\left\|\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|\cdot\left[2\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|\cdot\left(\left\|{\mathcal{A}}\right\|+\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\right)+\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\left(\left\|{\mathcal{A}}\right\|+\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\right)\left(\delta+2\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|\right)+\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\cdot\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\beta\right\|,

(S.286)

where we use
\left\|{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta\right\|\leq\left\|{\mathcal{A}}\right\|+\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|
from
\tau\leq\beta
.
Using a similar analysis, we also obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\psi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta}\left(\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right)\right\|\leq 2\left\|{\mathcal{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{B}}/\beta\right\|\cdot\left\|\phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}\right\|\leq\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\left(\left\|{\mathcal{A}}\right\|+\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\right).

(S.287)

By applying the upper bounds (
S.286
) and (
S.287
) to the inequality (
S.284
), we finally obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{t_le_delta_t_phi_g_beta}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{t}}{\pi}\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\left(\left\|{\mathcal{A}}\right\|+\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\right)\left(\delta+6\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|+\frac{\Delta_{t}}{\beta}\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|\right)+\frac{\Delta_{t}}{\pi\beta}\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\cdot\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|.

(S.288)

We now choose
\Delta_{t}=\beta/\mathcal{N}_{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}}}=\min\left[\beta/4\pi,(\left\|{\mathcal{A}}\right\|+\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|)^{-1}\right]
, which satisfies the condition (
S.357
).
By combining the inequalities (
S.282
) and (
S.288
) with the above choice of
\Delta_{t}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{t_>_delta_t_phi_g_beta}+\eqref{t_le_delta_t_phi_g_beta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\beta}{2\pi\Delta_{t}}\right)\left(\delta+4\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|\right)+\frac{\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|}{12}\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\Delta_{t}}{\pi}\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\left(\left\|{\mathcal{A}}\right\|+\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\right)\left(\delta+6\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|+\frac{\Delta_{t}}{\beta}\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|\right)+\frac{\Delta_{t}}{\pi\beta}\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\cdot\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}}}}{2\pi}\right)\left(\delta+4\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|\right)+\frac{\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|}{12}\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|+\frac{1}{\pi}\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\left(\delta+6\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|+\frac{2\Delta_{t}}{\beta}\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|}{\pi}\log\left(\mathcal{N}_{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}}}\right)\left(2\delta+10\left\|u_{\tau}-1\right\|\right)+\left(\frac{1}{12}+\frac{1}{2\pi}\right)\left\|{\mathcal{B}}\right\|\cdot\left\|\Delta{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}\right\|,

(S.289)

which reduces to the main inequality (
S.273
), where we use
1/12+1/(2\pi)\leq 1/4
.
This completes the proof of Lemma
21
.
\square

S.V
Quasi-locality of the entanglement Hamiltonian

In this section, we estimate quasi-locality after the connection of exponential operators as in Eq. (
S.224
) using Lemma
18
(or Corollary
19
).
In general, the forms of
\{\Psi_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N}
are extremely complicated.
To see this point, we first consider

\displaystyle\Psi_{1}=e^{\epsilon\beta{\mathcal{B}}_{1}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}e^{\epsilon\beta{\mathcal{B}}_{1}}=\exp\left(\beta e^{-2i\epsilon{\mathcal{C}}_{1}}{\mathcal{A}}e^{2i\epsilon{\mathcal{C}}_{1}}+2\epsilon\beta{\mathcal{B}}_{1}\right),

(S.290)

where
{\mathcal{C}}_{1}
is a quasi-local operator, which is defined by Eq. (
S.231
).
Second, we consider

\displaystyle\Psi_{2}

\displaystyle=e^{\epsilon\beta{\mathcal{B}}_{2}}e^{\beta e^{-2i\epsilon{\mathcal{C}}_{1}}Ae^{2i\epsilon{\mathcal{C}}_{1}}+2\epsilon\beta{\mathcal{B}}_{1}}e^{\epsilon\beta{\mathcal{B}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\beta e^{-2i\epsilon{\mathcal{C}}_{2}}e^{-2i\epsilon{\mathcal{C}}_{1}}Ae^{2i\epsilon{\mathcal{C}}_{1}}e^{2i\epsilon{\mathcal{C}}_{2}}+2\beta\epsilon e^{-2i\epsilon{\mathcal{C}}_{2}}B_{1}e^{2i\epsilon{\mathcal{C}}_{2}}+2\beta\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}_{2}\right),

(S.291)

where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{2}:=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{\beta}(t)e^{i\left(e^{-2i\epsilon{\mathcal{C}}_{1}}Ae^{2i\epsilon{\mathcal{C}}_{1}}+2\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}_{1}\right)t}B_{2}e^{-i\left(e^{-2i\epsilon{\mathcal{C}}_{1}}Ae^{2i\epsilon{\mathcal{C}}_{1}}+2\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}_{1}\right)t}dt.

(S.292)

Thus, the operator
{\mathcal{C}}_{2}
includes the double exponential operators.
In general,
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
is characterized by an exponential tower of operators; in the limit of
N\to\infty
, the number of tower layers becomes infinite.
This point makes analyses for the logarithm of
\Psi_{N}
quite challenging.

In the following, we show our main mathematical theorems to estimate the quasi-locality after the connection of exponential operators.
As the basic setup, we let
H_{0}
be an arbitrary Hamiltonian with short-range interaction with the condition (
S.7
).
Then, we start with the logarithm of

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\tau}=\log\left(e^{\tau V}e^{\beta H_{0}}e^{\tau V}\right),

(S.293)

Corollary
19
gives the form of

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\tau}=U_{\tau}(\beta H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau})U_{\tau}^{\dagger},

(S.294)

where

\displaystyle U_{\tau}=\mathcal{T}e^{-i\int_{0}^{\tau}{\mathcal{C}}_{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{1}},\quad\hat{V}_{\tau}=2\int_{0}^{\tau}U_{\tau_{1}}^{\dagger}VU_{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{1},\quad{\mathcal{C}}_{\tau}=\frac{2}{\beta}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{\beta}(t)V(\hat{H}_{\tau},t)dt.

(S.295)

We aim to prove the following statement on the quasi-locality of the unitary operator
U_{\tau}
:

Subtheorem 1
.

Let
V
be quasi-local around the subset
\mathfrak{L}
(
\mathfrak{L}\subset\Lambda
) in the sense of

\displaystyle\left\|[V,u_{i}]\right\|\leq\left\|V\right\|Q(0,\ell),\quad\ell=d_{i,\mathfrak{L}},

(S.296)

where
u_{i}
is an arbitrary unitary operator defined on the site
i
that is separated from
\mathfrak{L}
by distance
\ell
.
Then, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|\leq Q(\tau,r)\quad\textrm{for}\quad\forall\tau>0

(S.297)

with

\displaystyle Q(\tau,r)=e^{\kappa_{0}\tau+\kappa_{1}\tau\log(r+\tau+e)-\kappa_{\beta}r},

(S.298)

where we define
\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{\beta}
as

\displaystyle\kappa_{0}=\Theta(\beta^{D})\left\|V\right\|\log(\beta\left\|V\right\|\cdot|\mathfrak{L}|),\quad\kappa_{1}=\Theta(\beta^{D})\left\|V\right\|,

(S.299)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\beta}:=\min\left(\frac{\pi\mu}{2v\beta},\frac{\mu}{4}\right).

(S.300)

Remark.
In Eq. (
S.293
), we consider the case where the operator
V
does not depend on
\tau
, but the proof technique can be straightforwardly generalized to the logarithm of

\displaystyle e^{V_{N}\tau/N}\cdots e^{V_{2}\tau/N}e^{V_{1}\tau/N}e^{\beta H_{0}}e^{V_{1}\tau/N}e^{V_{2}\tau/N}\cdots e^{V_{N}\tau/N},

(S.301)

where
N
is arbitrarily large.
We also note that from the inequality
\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|\leq 2
, we can replace
Q(\tau,r)
in (
S.297
) as

\displaystyle Q(\tau,r)\to\min\left[2,Q(\tau,r)\right],

(S.302)

but we omit the
\min(\cdots)
notation.

S.V.1
Proof of Subtheorem
1

For the proof, we use the inductive method.
For
\tau=0
, we trivially obtain
U_{\tau}=\hat{1}
, and hence
\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|=0
.
We assume the inequality (
S.298
) up to a certain
\tau
and prove the case of
\tau+d\tau
with
d\tau
infinitesimally small.
We have

\displaystyle\left\|[U_{\tau+d\tau},u_{i}]\right\|=\left\|[e^{-i{\mathcal{C}}_{\tau}d\tau}U_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|\leq\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|+d\tau\left\|[{\mathcal{C}}_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|,

(S.303)

which yields
(d/d\tau)\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|\leq\left\|[{\mathcal{C}}_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|
.
Then, our task is to prove

\displaystyle\left\|[{\mathcal{C}}_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d}{d\tau}e^{\kappa_{0}\tau+\kappa_{1}\tau\log(r+\tau+e)-\kappa_{\beta}r}=Q(\tau,r)\left[\kappa_{0}+\kappa_{1}\log(r+\tau+e)+\frac{\kappa_{1}\tau}{r+\tau+e}\right]

(S.304)

under the assumption of (
S.297
) for
[0,\tau]
.
The inequality (
S.304
) and
\left\|[U_{0},u_{i}]\right\|=0
yields the desired inequality of

\displaystyle\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|=\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{d}{d\tau_{1}}\left\|[U_{\tau_{1}},u_{i}]\right\|d\tau_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\|[{\mathcal{C}}_{\tau_{1}},u_{i}]\right\|d\tau_{1}\leq\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{d}{d\tau_{1}}e^{\kappa_{0}\tau_{1}+\kappa_{1}\tau_{1}\log(r+\tau_{1}+e)-\kappa_{\beta}r}d\tau_{1}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\kappa_{0}\tau+\kappa_{1}\tau\log(r+\tau+e)-\kappa_{\beta}r}=Q(\tau,r).

(S.305)

In proving the inequality (
S.304
), it is enough to prove

\displaystyle\left\|[{\mathcal{C}}_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|\leq\left[\kappa_{0}+\kappa_{1}\log(r+\tau+e)\right]Q(\tau,r)

(S.306)

under the choices of
\kappa_{0}
and
\kappa_{1}
as in Eq. (
S.299
) and (
S.300
), respectively.

In the following, the primary task is to estimate the norm of

\displaystyle\left[{\mathcal{C}}_{\tau},u_{i}\right]=\frac{2}{\beta}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{\beta}(t)\left[V(\hat{H}_{\tau},t),u_{i}\right]dt.

(S.307)

In the above equation, we need to consider the integral, which includes the function
g_{\beta}(t)
.
A challenging problem that may occur is that the integral

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|g_{\beta}(t)|dt

(S.308)

is divergent since
|g_{\beta}(t)|\propto 1/|t|
for
t\ll 1
[see also Eq. (
S.92
)].
To avoid the divergence, we utilize the following equation as in Ref.
[
26
, (D. 83) and (D.84)]
:

\displaystyle O(H,t)=O+\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\lambda}O(H,\lambda t)d\lambda=O+it\int_{0}^{1}{\rm ad}_{H}[O(H,\lambda t)]d\lambda,

(S.309)

which yields the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{\beta}(t)V(\hat{H}_{\tau},t)dt

\displaystyle=\int_{|t|>\delta t}g_{\beta}(t)V(\hat{H}_{\tau},t)dt+\int_{|t|\leq\delta t}g_{\beta}(t)\left(V+it\int_{0}^{1}{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}[V(\hat{H}_{\tau},\lambda t)]d\lambda\right)dt

\displaystyle=\int_{|t|>\delta t}g_{\beta}(t)V(\hat{H}_{\tau},t)dt+i\int_{|t|\leq\delta t}tg_{\beta}(t)\int_{0}^{1}{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}[V(\hat{H}_{\tau},\lambda t)]d\lambda dt,

(S.310)

where in the second equation, we use
\int_{|t|\leq\delta t}g_{\beta}(t)dt=0
since
g_{\beta}(t)
is an odd function, and the parameter
\delta t
is appropriately chosen afterward so that it can satisfy

\displaystyle\delta t\leq\frac{\beta}{4\pi}.

(S.311)

To analyze the commutator norm of
\left\|[{\mathcal{C}}_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|
in (
S.304
), we prove the following general statement:

Lemma 22
.

For arbitrary local unitary operator
u_{i}
and operator
O
, we have the following upper bounds:

\displaystyle\left\|[O(\hat{H}_{\tau},t),u_{i}]\right\|\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\left[O,u_{i}(H_{0},-t)\right]\right\|

\displaystyle+2\left\|O\right\|\left(\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|+\left\|\left[U_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-t)\right]\right\|+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\left[\hat{V}_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-t_{1})\right]\right\|dt_{1}\right).

(S.312)

Proof of Lemma
22
.

Using the explicit form of
\hat{H}_{\tau}
in Eq. (
S.294
), we start from

\displaystyle O(\hat{H}_{\tau},t)=e^{i\hat{H}_{\tau}t}Oe^{-i\hat{H}_{\tau}t}=U_{\tau}e^{i\left(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau}\right)t}U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau}e^{-i\left(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau}\right)t}U_{\tau}^{\dagger},

(S.313)

which immediately yields

\displaystyle\left\|\left[O(\hat{H}_{\tau},t),u_{i}\right]\right\|\leq 2\left\|O\right\|\cdot\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|+\left\|\left[e^{i\left(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau}\right)t}U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau}e^{-i\left(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau}\right)t},u_{i}\right]\right\|.

(S.314)

The second term is upper-bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|\left[e^{i\left(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau}\right)t}U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau}e^{-i\left(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau}\right)t},u_{i}\right]\right\|=\left\|\left[U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau},e^{-i\left(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau}\right)t}u_{i}e^{i\left(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau}\right)t}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\left[U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau},e^{-iH_{0}t}\tilde{U}_{\tau}u_{i}\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\dagger}e^{iH_{0}t}\right]\right\|=\left\|\left[U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau},e^{-iH_{0}t}\left([\tilde{U}_{\tau},u_{i}]\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\dagger}+u_{i}\right)e^{iH_{0}t}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|O\right\|\cdot\left\|[\tilde{U}_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|+\left\|\left[U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-t)\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|O\right\|\cdot\left\|[\tilde{U}_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|+2\left\|O\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[U_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-t)\right]\right\|+\left\|\left[O,u_{i}(H_{0},-t)\right]\right\|,

(S.315)

where
\tilde{U}_{\tau}:=e^{iH_{0}t}e^{-i\left(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau}\right)t}
.
Finally, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left[\tilde{U}_{\tau},u_{i}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\left[\mathcal{T}e^{-\int_{0}^{t}e^{iH_{0}t_{1}}\hat{V}_{\tau}e^{-iH_{0}t_{1}}dt_{1}},u_{i}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\left[\hat{V}_{\tau},e^{-iH_{0}t_{1}}u_{i}e^{iH_{0}t_{1}}\right]\right\|dt_{1}=\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\left[\hat{V}_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-t_{1})\right]\right\|dt_{1},

(S.316)

which reduces the inequality (
S.315
) to

\displaystyle\left\|\left[e^{i\left(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau}\right)t}U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau}e^{-i\left(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau}\right)t},u_{i}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left[O,u_{i}(H_{0},-t)\right]\right\|+2\left\|O\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[U_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-t)\right]\right\|+2\left\|O\right\|\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\left[\hat{V}_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-t_{1})\right]\right\|dt_{1}.

(S.317)

Combining the above inequality with the inequality (
S.314
), we obtain the main inequality (
S.312
).
This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
22
]

Using Lemma
22
in Eq. (
S.310
) with
O=V
or
O={\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}(V)}
, the norm of Eq. (
S.307
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\beta}\left\|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{\beta}(t)\left[V(\hat{H}_{\tau},t),u_{i}\right]dt\right\|,

(S.318)

is upper-bounded by using the summation of the following three terms:

\displaystyle\int_{|t|>\delta t}dt|g_{\beta}(t)|\Bigl(\left\|\left[V,u_{i}(H_{0},-t)\right]\right\|+2\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|+2\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[U_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-t)\right]\right\|\Bigr),

(S.319)

\displaystyle\int_{|t|\leq\delta t}dt\int_{0}^{1}d\lambda|tg_{\beta}(t)|\Bigl(\left\|\left[{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(V),u_{i}(H_{0},-\lambda t)\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+2\left\|{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(V)\right\|\cdot\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|+2\left\|{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(V)\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[U_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-\lambda t)\right]\right\|\Bigr),

(S.320)

\displaystyle 2\left\|V\right\|\int_{|t|>\delta t}dt|g_{\beta}(t)|\int_{0}^{t}dt_{1}\left\|\left[\hat{V}_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-t_{1})\right]\right\|+2\left\|{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(V)\right\|\int_{|t|\leq\delta t}dt|tg_{\beta}(t)|\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\lambda t}dt_{1}\left\|\left[\hat{V}_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-t_{1})\right]\right\|d\lambda.

(S.321)

In analyzing the above terms, we have to treat the norms like
\left\|\left[U_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-t)\right]\right\|
.
For this purpose, based on Lemma
5
, we prove the following proposition (see Sec.
S.V.2
for the proof):

Proposition 23
.

Let us adopt the same setup as in Lemma
5
.
We also choose
\mathcal{F}(\ell)
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\ell)=\exp\left(K_{\ell}-\kappa_{\beta}\ell\right),

(S.322)

where
K_{\ell}
monotonically increases with
\ell
.
Then, we consider two kinds of functions as

\displaystyle g_{1}(t)=\begin{cases}|g_{\beta}(t)|&\quad\textrm{for}\quad|t|\geq\delta t,\\
0&\quad\textrm{for}\quad|t|<\delta t,\end{cases}\quad\quad g_{2}(t)=\begin{cases}0&\quad\textrm{for}\quad|t|\geq\delta t,\\
|tg_{\beta}(t)|&\quad\textrm{for}\quad|t|<\delta t.\end{cases}

(S.323)

We then obtain

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{1}(t)\left\|[O,u_{i}(H_{0},t)]\right\|dt\leq\mathfrak{g}_{1}\mathcal{F}(\ell),\and\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{2}(t)\left\|[O,u_{i}(H_{0},\lambda t)]\right\|dt\leq\mathfrak{g}_{2}\delta t\mathcal{F}(\ell),

(S.324)

where
0\leq\lambda\leq 1
, and
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{2}
are defined as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{1}:=\frac{4\beta}{\pi}\left(1+\gamma(\Delta\ell)^{D}e^{\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\right)\log\left(\frac{\beta}{2\pi\delta t}\right)+\frac{8\gamma(4D\Delta\ell)^{D}\beta}{\pi}e^{2\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}+\frac{\gamma Cv\beta^{2}}{3}\left(\frac{8D^{2}}{e\mu}\right)^{D},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{2}:=\frac{2\beta}{\pi}\left[1+2^{D}D!\gamma Cv(1+4/\mu)^{D}\delta t\right],

(S.325)

where
\Delta\ell
is an arbitrary integer that satisfies

\displaystyle\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell\geq 1.

(S.326)

Remark.

By choosing
\Delta\ell
such that
\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell=\mathcal{O}(1)
[e.g.,
\Delta\ell=\lceil 1/\kappa_{\beta}\rceil=\mathcal{O}(\beta)
], we have
\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell\leq 2
, and hence

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{1}\leq\Theta(\beta^{D+1})\log(\beta/\delta t),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{2}\leq\Theta(\beta),

(S.327)

using the
\Theta
notation in Eq. (
S.14
), where we use
\kappa_{\beta}=\mathcal{O}(\beta)
and
\delta t=\mathcal{O}(1)
as has been given in (
S.121
) and (
S.311
) [see also Eq. (
S.348
)], respectively.

By using Proposition
23
, we are able to upper-bound (
S.319
)
under the conditions of

\displaystyle\left\|[V,u_{i}]\right\|\leq\left\|V\right\|Q(0,r),\quad\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|\leq Q(\tau,r),

(S.328)

where
Q(\tau,r)
in Eq. (
S.298
) can be expressed by the form of Eq. (
S.322
).
We then obtain

\displaystyle\int_{|t|>\delta t}dt|g_{\beta}(t)|\cdot\left\|\left[V,u_{i}(H_{0},-t)\right]\right\|\leq\mathfrak{g}_{1}\left\|V\right\|Q(0,r)\leq\mathfrak{g}_{1}\left\|V\right\|Q(\tau,r),

\displaystyle\int_{|t|>\delta t}dt|g_{\beta}(t)|\cdot 2\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|\leq 2\left\|V\right\|Q(\tau,r)\cdot\frac{2\beta}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\beta}{2\pi\delta t}\right)\leq\mathfrak{g}_{1}\left\|V\right\|Q(\tau,r),

\displaystyle\int_{|t|>\delta t}dt|g_{\beta}(t)|\cdot 2\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[U_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-t)\right]\right\|\leq 2\mathfrak{g}_{1}\left\|V\right\|Q(\tau,r),

(S.329)

where, in the second inequality, we use the inequality (
S.358
) in Lemma
26
below.
We therefore obtain

\displaystyle\textrm{Eq.~\eqref{norm_V_tau_t_u_i_com_1}}\leq 4\mathfrak{g}_{1}\left\|V\right\|Q(\tau,r).

(S.330)

To apply Proposition
23
to the terms of (
S.320
),
we need to consider the quasi-locality of
{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(V)
, which can be estimated by the following general statement (see Sec.
S.V.3
for the proof):

Proposition 24

(
Analysis of
{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(O)
).

Let
O
be an arbitrary quasi-local operator around
\mathfrak{L}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\left[O,u_{i}\right]\right\|\leq\mathcal{N}_{O}\check{Q}(\tau,r)\quad{\rm with}\quad\mathcal{N}_{O}\geq\left\|O\right\|,

(S.331)

with

\displaystyle\check{Q}(\tau,r):=\min\left(2,e^{\check{\kappa}_{0}\tau+\check{\kappa}_{1}\tau\log(r+\tau+e)-\check{\kappa}_{\beta}r}\right)\geq Q(\tau,r),

(S.332)

where
d_{i,\mathfrak{L}}=r
and
u_{i}
is an arbitrary local unitary operator on the site
i
.
Note that the condition has been imposed for
Q(\tau,r)
as in (
S.302
).
Under the condition (
S.298
),
{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(V)
is quasi-local in the sense that

\displaystyle\left\|\left[{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(O),u_{i}\right]\right\|\leq\mathcal{N}_{O}g_{\tau,r}\check{Q}(\tau,r)

(S.333)

with

\displaystyle g_{\tau,r}:=24\left\|V\right\|\tau+2^{2D+6}\gamma^{2}|\mathfrak{L}|^{2}\bar{J}_{0}\left(\check{C}_{2,\tau}^{2D}+r^{2D}+8\gamma|\mathfrak{L}|\check{C}_{2,\tau}^{2D}\check{C}_{3,\tau}^{3D}\right)\Bigr],

(S.334)

where we define
\check{C}_{2,\tau}
and
\check{C}_{3,\tau}
as in Eq. (
S.379
), i.e.,
\check{C}_{\nu,\tau}:=\frac{\tau+e}{2}+\frac{32}{\check{\kappa}_{\beta}^{2}}\left(\nu^{2}D^{2}+\check{\kappa}_{1}^{2}\tau^{2}+\frac{\check{\kappa}_{\beta}\check{\kappa}_{0}\tau}{8}\right)
for
\nu>0
.
Also, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(O)\right\|\leq\mathcal{N}_{O}g^{\prime}_{\tau},\quad g^{\prime}_{\tau}:=2\tau\left\|V\right\|+2^{2D+5}\gamma^{2}|\mathfrak{L}|^{2}\check{C}_{2,\tau}^{2D}\bar{J}_{0}\left(1+2\gamma|\mathfrak{L}|\check{C}_{3,\tau}^{3D}\right).

(S.335)

Remark.

In applying the proposition to the case of
O=V
, we can choose as
\check{Q}(\tau,r)=Q(\tau,r)
and
\mathcal{N}_{V}=\left\|V\right\|
since
\left\|\left[V,u_{i}\right]\right\|\leq\left\|V\right\|Q(0,r)\leq\left\|V\right\|Q(\tau,r)
.
In this application, we should replace
\check{C}_{\nu,\tau}
in
g_{\tau,r}
and
g^{\prime}_{\tau}
with
C_{\nu,\tau}
:

\displaystyle C_{\nu,\tau}:=\frac{\tau+e}{2}+\frac{32}{\kappa_{\beta}^{2}}\left(\nu^{2}D^{2}+\kappa_{1}^{2}\tau^{2}+\frac{\kappa_{\beta}\kappa_{0}\tau}{8}\right)\quad(\nu\in\mathbb{N}).

(S.336)

By using the
\Theta
notation in Eq. (
S.14
), we obtain

\displaystyle g_{\tau,r}\leq\Theta(\left\|V\right\|,|\mathfrak{L}|^{3},r^{2D},\beta^{10D},\kappa_{0}^{5D},\kappa_{1}^{10D},\tau^{10D}),

\displaystyle g^{\prime}_{\tau}\leq\Theta(\left\|V\right\|,|\mathfrak{L}|^{3},\beta^{10D},\kappa_{0}^{5D},\kappa_{1}^{10D},\tau^{10D}).

(S.337)

As shown in the following section (Sec.
S.V.1.1
), for our purpose, it is enough to ensure that
g_{\tau,r}
and
g^{\prime}_{\tau}
are upper-bounded by finite-degree polynomials of
\{\left\|V\right\|,|\mathfrak{L}|,r,\beta,\kappa_{0},\kappa_{1},\tau
}.

By applying Propositions
23
and
24
to Eq. (
S.320
),
we have

\displaystyle\int_{|t|\leq\delta t}dt\int_{0}^{1}d\lambda|tg_{\beta}(t)|\cdot\left\|\left[{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(V),u_{i}(H_{0},-\lambda t)\right]\right\|\leq g_{\tau,r}\left\|V\right\|\mathfrak{g}_{2}\delta tQ(\tau,r),

\displaystyle\int_{|t|\leq\delta t}dt\int_{0}^{1}d\lambda|tg_{\beta}(t)|\cdot 2\left\|{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(V)\right\|\cdot\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|\leq 2g^{\prime}_{\tau}\left\|V\right\|Q(\tau,r)\int_{|t|\leq\delta t}dt\int_{0}^{1}d\lambda|tg_{\beta}(t)|\leq\frac{2\beta g^{\prime}_{\tau}}{\pi}\delta t\left\|V\right\|Q(\tau,r),

\displaystyle\int_{|t|\leq\delta t}dt\int_{0}^{1}d\lambda|tg_{\beta}(t)|\cdot 2\left\|{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(V)\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[U_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-\lambda t)\right]\right\|\leq 2g^{\prime}_{\tau}\left\|V\right\|\cdot\mathfrak{g}_{2}\delta tQ(\tau,r),

(S.338)

where we use the inequality (
S.359
) in the second inequality.
We thus upper-bound Eq. (
S.320
) as follows:

\displaystyle\textrm{Eq.~\eqref{norm_V_tau_t_u_i_com_2}}\leq\left(\mathfrak{g}_{2}g_{\tau,r}+\frac{2\beta}{\pi}g^{\prime}_{\tau}+2\mathfrak{g}_{2}g^{\prime}_{\tau}\right)\left\|V\right\|\delta tQ(\tau,r)\leq\left(g_{\tau,r}+3g^{\prime}_{\tau}\right)\left\|V\right\|\mathfrak{g}_{2}\delta tQ(\tau,r),

(S.339)

where we use
\frac{2\beta}{\pi}\leq\mathfrak{g}_{2}
from the definition (
S.325
).

Finally, to analyse the commutator
\left[\hat{V}_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-t_{1})\right]
in (
S.321
), we use the relation

\displaystyle\left\|\left[\hat{V}_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-t_{1})\right]\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\left[\int_{0}^{\tau}U_{\tau_{1}}VU_{\tau_{1}}^{\dagger}d\tau_{1},u_{i}(H_{0},-t_{1})\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\tau}\left(2\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[U_{\tau_{1}},u_{i}(H_{0},-t_{1})\right]\right\|+\left\|[V,u_{i}(H_{0},-t_{1})\right\|\right)d\tau_{1},

(S.340)

Then, to estimate Eq. (
S.321
), we prove the following proposition using Corollary
6
.
(see Sec.
S.V.4
for the proof):

Proposition 25
.

Let us adopt the same setup as in Proposition
23
.
We also choose
\mathcal{F}(\ell)
as in Eq. (
S.322
).
Then, we have

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{1}(t)\int_{0}^{t}\left\|[O,u_{i}(H_{0},t_{1})]\right\|dt_{1}dt\leq\mathfrak{g}_{3}\mathcal{F}(\ell),

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{2}(t)\int_{0}^{\lambda t}\left\|[O,u_{i}(H_{0},t_{1})]\right\|dt_{1}dt\leq\mathfrak{g}_{4}\delta t^{2}\mathcal{F}(\ell),

(S.341)

with

\mathfrak{g}_{3}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{4}
defined as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{3}:=\frac{\beta^{2}}{6}\left[1+\gamma(\Delta\ell)^{D}e^{\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\right]+2\gamma(4D\Delta\ell)^{D}e^{2\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\left(\frac{16D\beta\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}{\pi}+\frac{\beta^{2}}{6}\right)+\frac{4\gamma Cv\beta^{3}}{\pi^{3}}\left(\frac{8D^{2}}{e\mu}\right)^{D}\leq\Theta(\beta^{D+2}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{4}=\frac{\beta}{\pi}\left[1+\frac{2}{3}\cdot 2^{D}D!Cv(1+4/\mu)^{D}\delta t\right]\leq\mathfrak{g}_{2},

(S.342)

respectively, where
g_{1}(t)
and
g_{2}(t)
have been defined in Eq. (
S.323
).
Note that the inequality for
\mathfrak{g}_{3}
can be derived by choosing
\Delta\ell=\lceil 1/\kappa_{\beta}\rceil
, for example.

By applying Proposition
25
to the integral of Eq. (
S.340
), the first term in Eq. (
S.321
) is upper-bounded by

\displaystyle 2\left\|V\right\|\int_{|t|>\delta t}dt|g_{\beta}(t)|\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\left[\hat{V}_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-t_{1})\right]\right\|dt_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|V\right\|\int_{0}^{\tau}d\tau_{1}\int_{|t|>\delta t}dt|g_{\beta}(t)|\int_{0}^{t}\left(2\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[U_{\tau_{1}},u_{i}(H_{0},-t_{1})\right]\right\|+\left\|[V,u_{i}(H_{0},-t_{1})\right\|\right)dt_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathfrak{g}_{3}\left\|V\right\|^{2}\int_{0}^{\tau}d\tau_{1}\left[2Q(\tau_{1},r)+Q(0,r)\right]\leq 6\mathfrak{g}_{3}\left\|V\right\|^{2}\int_{0}^{\tau}d\tau_{1}Q(\tau_{1},r),

(S.343)

where we use
Q(0,r)\leq Q(\tau,r)
.
Also, the second term in Eq. (
S.321
) is upper-bounded by

\displaystyle 2\left\|{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(V)\right\|\int_{|t|\leq\delta t}dt|tg_{\beta}(t)|\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\lambda t}dt_{1}\left\|\left[\hat{V}_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-t_{1})\right]\right\|d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq 2g^{\prime}_{\tau}\left\|V\right\|\int_{|t|\leq\delta t}dt|tg_{\beta}(t)|\int_{0}^{t}dt_{1}\left\|\left[\hat{V}_{\tau},u_{i}(H_{0},-t_{1})\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2g^{\prime}_{\tau}\left\|V\right\|\int_{0}^{\tau}d\tau_{1}\int_{|t|\leq\delta t}dt|tg_{\beta}(t)|\int_{0}^{t}\left(2\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[U_{\tau_{1}},u_{i}(H_{0},-t_{1})\right]\right\|+\left\|[V,u_{i}(H_{0},-t_{1})\right\|\right)dt_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 6\mathfrak{g}_{4}g^{\prime}_{\tau}\delta t^{2}\left\|V\right\|^{2}\int_{0}^{\tau}d\tau_{1}Q(\tau_{1},r),

(S.344)

where we use the inequality (
S.335
) to derive
\left\|{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(V)\right\|\leq g^{\prime}_{\tau}\left\|V\right\|
.

From the condition (
S.298
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau}d\tau_{1}Q(\tau_{1},r)

\displaystyle=e^{-\kappa_{\beta}r}\int_{0}^{\tau}d\tau_{1}e^{\kappa_{0}\tau_{1}+\kappa_{1}\tau_{1}\log(r+\tau_{1}+e)}\leq e^{-\kappa_{\beta}r+\kappa_{1}\tau\log(r+\tau+e)}\int_{0}^{\tau}d\tau_{1}e^{\kappa_{0}\tau_{1}}

\displaystyle=e^{-\kappa_{\beta}r+\kappa_{1}\tau\log(r+\tau+e)}\frac{e^{\kappa_{0}\tau}-1}{\kappa_{0}}\leq\frac{Q(\tau,r)}{\kappa_{0}}.

(S.345)

By combining the inequalities (
S.343
) and (
S.344
) with (
S.345
), we obtain

\displaystyle\textrm{Eq.~\eqref{norm_V_tau_t_u_i_com_3}}\leq\frac{6\left\|V\right\|^{2}}{\kappa_{0}}Q(\tau,r)\left(\mathfrak{g}_{3}+\mathfrak{g}_{4}g^{\prime}_{\tau}\delta t^{2}\right).

(S.346)

S.V.1.1
Completing the proof of Subtheorem
1

Collecting (
S.330
), (
S.339
) and (
S.346
) together,
we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|[{\mathcal{C}}_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\beta}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|g_{\beta}(t)|\cdot\left\|[V(\hat{H}_{\tau},t),u_{i}]\right\|dt

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\left\|V\right\|}{\beta}\left[4\mathfrak{g}_{1}+\left(g_{\tau,r}+3g^{\prime}_{\tau}\right)\mathfrak{g}_{2}\delta t+\frac{6\left\|V\right\|}{\kappa_{0}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{3}+\mathfrak{g}_{4}g^{\prime}_{\tau}\delta t^{2}\right)\right]Q(\tau,r),

(S.347)

We now choose
\delta t
as

\displaystyle\delta t=\frac{1}{g_{\tau,r}+3g^{\prime}_{\tau}}.

(S.348)

Because of (
S.348
) and

\displaystyle g_{\tau,r}\leq\Theta(\left\|V\right\|,|\mathfrak{L}|^{3},r^{2D},\beta^{10D},\kappa_{0}^{5D},\kappa_{1}^{10D},\tau^{10D}),\quad g^{\prime}_{\tau}\leq\Theta(\left\|V\right\|,|\mathfrak{L}|^{3},\beta^{10D},\kappa_{0}^{5D},\kappa_{1}^{10D},\tau^{10D}),

(S.349)

we have

\displaystyle\log(\delta t^{-1})\leq\Theta(1)\left[\log(r+\tau+e)+\log(\kappa_{0}\kappa_{1}\beta\left\|V\right\|\cdot|\mathfrak{L}|)\right].

(S.350)

Then, the inequality (
S.347
) imposes the following condition for
\kappa_{0}
and
\kappa_{1}
from (
S.306
):

\displaystyle\frac{2\left\|V\right\|}{\beta}\left[4\mathfrak{g}_{1}+\mathfrak{g}_{2}+\frac{6\left\|V\right\|}{\kappa_{0}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{3}+\frac{\mathfrak{g}_{4}}{9g^{\prime}_{\tau}}\right)\right]\leq\kappa_{0}+\kappa_{1}\log(r+\tau+e).

(S.351)

To determine
\kappa_{0}
and
\kappa_{1}
, we first recall

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{1}\leq\Theta(\beta^{D+1})\log(\beta/\delta t),\quad\mathfrak{g}_{4}\leq\mathfrak{g}_{2}\leq\Theta(\beta),\quad\mathfrak{g}_{3}\leq\Theta(\beta^{D+2})

(S.352)

from the definitions of
\{\mathfrak{g}_{1},\mathfrak{g}_{2},\mathfrak{g}_{3},\mathfrak{g}_{4}\}
in (
S.327
) and (
S.342
).
We thus obtain
