\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\tau,t}(O)+4\left\|O\right\|\left(|t|\tau\left\|V\right\|\mathcal{E}(0,r)+2t\left\|V\right\|\int_{0}^{\tau}\mathcal{E}(\tau_{1},r)d\tau_{1}+\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{|t|}\varepsilon_{\tau_{1},x}(V)dx\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\tau,t}(O)+4\left\|O\right\|\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{|t|}\varepsilon_{\tau_{1},x}(V)dx+4|t|\cdot\left\|O\right\|\cdot\left\|V\right\|\left(\tau\mathcal{E}(0,r)+2\int_{0}^{\tau}\mathcal{E}(\tau_{1},r)d\tau_{1}\right).

(S.498)

Proof of Lemma
33
.

We first note that the following equations hold

\displaystyle e^{i\left(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau}\right)t}=\mathcal{T}e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\hat{V}_{\tau}(H_{0},x)dx}e^{iH_{0}t},\quad e^{i\left(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}+\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right)t}=\mathcal{T}e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},x)dx}e^{iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}.

(S.499)

Moreover, using the above equation, we consider the following decomposition of
e^{i\left(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau}\right)t}
:

\displaystyle e^{i\left(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau}\right)t}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathcal{T}e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\hat{V}_{\tau}(H_{0},x)dx}-e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\hat{V}_{\tau}(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},x)dx}+e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\hat{V}_{\tau}(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},x)dx}-\mathcal{T}e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},x)dx}\right)e^{iH_{0}t}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{T}e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},x)dx}e^{iH_{0}t}

\displaystyle=:\Delta U_{\tau}+e^{i\left(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}+\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right)t}e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}e^{iH_{0}t}.

(S.500)

From the above equation, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|e^{i\left(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau}\right)t}U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau}e^{-i\left(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau}\right)t}-e^{i\left(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}+\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right)t}e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}e^{iH_{0}t}U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau}e^{-iH_{0}t}e^{iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}e^{-i\left(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}+\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right)t}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|O\right\|\cdot\left\|\Delta U_{\tau}\right\|.

(S.501)

Then, using the definition of (
S.497
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|e^{i\left(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}+\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right)t}e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}e^{iH_{0}t}U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau}e^{-iH_{0}t}e^{iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}e^{-i\left(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}+\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right)t}-e^{i\left(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}+\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right)t}U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau}e^{-i\left(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}+\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right)t}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|e^{i\left(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}+\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right)t}e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}\left(e^{iH_{0}t}U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau}e^{-iH_{0}t}-e^{iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau}e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}\right)e^{iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}e^{-i\left(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}+\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right)t}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|e^{iH_{0}t}U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau}e^{-iH_{0}t}-e^{iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau}e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}\right\|=\varepsilon_{\tau,t}(O).

(S.502)

By combining the two upper bounds of (
S.501
) and (
S.502
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|e^{i\left(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau}\right)t}U_{\tau}^{\dagger}VU_{\tau}e^{-i\left(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau}\right)t}-e^{i\left(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}+\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right)t}U_{\tau}^{\dagger}VU_{\tau}e^{-i\left(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}+\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right)t}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|O\right\|\cdot\left\|\Delta U_{\tau}\right\|+\varepsilon_{\tau,t}(O).

(S.503)

Finally, we estimate the upper bound of the norm
\left\|\Delta U_{\tau}\right\|
. We begin with the inequality of

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta U_{\tau}\right\|\leq\int_{0}^{|t|}\left\|\hat{V}_{\tau}(H_{0},x)-\hat{V}_{\tau}(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},x)\right\|dx+|t|\cdot\left\|\hat{V}_{\tau}-\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|,

(S.504)

where we use the inequality (
S.114
).
For the estimation of the RHS of (
S.504
), we first upper-bound the norm
\left\|\hat{V}_{\tau}-\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|
as

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{V}_{\tau}-\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|=2\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\|U_{\tau_{1}}^{\dagger}VU_{\tau_{1}}-U_{\tau_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\dagger}V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}U_{\tau_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|d\tau_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\tau\left\|V-V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|+2\int_{0}^{\tau}\left(\left\|U_{\tau_{1}}^{\dagger}VU_{\tau_{1}}-U_{\tau_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\dagger}VU_{\tau_{1}}\right\|+\left\|U_{\tau_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\dagger}VU_{\tau_{1}}-U_{\tau_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\dagger}VU_{\tau_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|\right)d\tau_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\tau\left\|V-V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|+4\left\|V\right\|\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\|U_{\tau_{1}}-U_{\tau_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|d\tau_{1}\leq 2\tau\left\|V\right\|\mathcal{E}(0,r)+4\left\|V\right\|\int_{0}^{\tau}\mathcal{E}(\tau_{1},r)d\tau_{1},

(S.505)

where the forms of
\hat{V}_{\tau}
and
\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}
have been defined as in Eq. (
S.295
).
In the same way, the error between time evolutions of
\hat{V}_{\tau}(H_{0},x)
and
\hat{V}_{\tau}(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},x)
is upper-bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{|t|}\left\|\hat{V}_{\tau}(H_{0},x)-\hat{V}_{\tau}(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},x)\right\|dx

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{|t|}\left\|e^{iH_{0}x}U_{\tau_{1}}^{\dagger}VU_{\tau_{1}}e^{-iH_{0}x}-e^{iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}x}U_{\tau_{1}}^{\dagger}VU_{\tau_{1}}e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}x}\right\|dx=2\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{|t|}\varepsilon_{\tau_{1},x}(V)dx,

(S.506)

where we use the definition (
S.497
) for
\varepsilon_{\tau_{1},x}(V)
.
By applying the above inequalities to (
S.503
), we obtain the main inequality (
S.498
).
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
33
]

Then, we have to estimate the function
\varepsilon_{\tau,t}(O)
in Eq. (
S.497
).
For this purpose, we prove the following statement:

Lemma 34
.

The parameter
\varepsilon_{\tau,t}(O)
is upper-bounded by

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\tau,t}(O)\leq 8\left\|O\right\|\cdot\left\|U_{\tau}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(U_{\tau})\right\|+4\left\|O-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(O)\right\|+6\left\|O\right\|\cdot\left\|U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}}(U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t})\right\|

(S.507)

with

\displaystyle U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}:=e^{-iH_{0}t}e^{i(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}+H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}})t}=e^{-iH_{0}t}e^{iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}e^{iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}t},

(S.508)

where we define the subset
\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}
as (see also Fig.
12
)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}:=\mathfrak{L}[r/2].

(S.509)

Figure 12:
Schematic picture of the definitions of
\mathfrak{L}
,
\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}
and
\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}
.

Proof of Lemma
34
.

We first consider an arbitrary operator
O^{\prime}
and decompose the time evolved operator
O(H_{0},t)
using the definition (
S.508
) as

\displaystyle O^{\prime}(H_{0},t)

\displaystyle=e^{iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}e^{iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}t}U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}^{\dagger}O^{\prime}U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}t}e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t},

(S.510)

which yields

\displaystyle\left\|O^{\prime}(H_{0},t)-O^{\prime}(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},t)\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|e^{iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}e^{iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}t}U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}^{\dagger}\left[O^{\prime},U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}\right]e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}t}e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}+e^{iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}e^{iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}t}O^{\prime}e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}t}e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}-e^{iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}O^{\prime}e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left[O^{\prime},U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}\right]\right\|+\left\|e^{iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}e^{iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}t}\left[O^{\prime},e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}t}\right]e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}t}\right\|\leq\left\|\left[O^{\prime},U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}\right]\right\|+\left\|\left[O^{\prime},e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}t}\right]\right\|.

(S.511)

By applying the above inequality to
O^{\prime}=U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau}
, we upper-bound

\varepsilon_{\tau,t}(O)
as

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\tau,t}(O)

\displaystyle=\left\|\left[U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau}\right](H_{0},t)-\left[U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau}\right](H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},t)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left[U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau},U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}\right]\right\|+\left\|\left[U_{\tau}^{\dagger}OU_{\tau},e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}t}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|O\right\|\left(\left\|\left[U_{\tau},U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}\right]\right\|+\left\|\left[U_{\tau},e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}t}\right]\right\|\right)+\left\|\left[O,U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}\right]\right\|+\left\|\left[O,e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{\rm c}}}t}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|O\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[U_{\tau},U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}\right]\right\|+4\left\|O\right\|\cdot\left\|U_{\tau}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{\rm c}}}(U_{\tau})\right\|+\left\|\left[O,U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}\right]\right\|+2\left\|O-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{\rm c}}}(O)\right\|,

(S.512)

where we use
\left[\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{\rm c}}}(U_{\tau}),e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}t}\right]=\left[\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{\rm c}}}(O),e^{-iH_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}t}\right]=0

since
\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{\rm c}}}(U_{\tau})
and
\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{\rm c}}}(O)
are supported on the sebset
\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}
(
\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}\cap\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}=\emptyset
as in Fig.
12
).
To estimate the norm of
\left\|\left[U_{\tau},U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}\right]\right\|
, we approximate
U_{\tau}
and
U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}

onto the subsets
\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}
, respectively.
We then obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\left[U_{\tau},U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left[U_{\tau}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(U_{\tau}),U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}\right]\right\|+\left\|\left[\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(U_{\tau}),U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|U_{\tau}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(U_{\tau})\right\|+\left\|\left[\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(U_{\tau}),U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|U_{\tau}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(U_{\tau})\right\|+\left\|\left[\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(U_{\tau}),U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}}(U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t})\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|U_{\tau}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(U_{\tau})\right\|+2\left\|U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}}(U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t})\right\|,

(S.513)

where in the last inequality we use
\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(O_{1})\leq\left\|O_{1}\right\|
for an arbitrary
O_{1}
.
We can derive a similar inequality for
\left\|\left[O,U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}\right]\right\|
.
By applying the upper bound (
S.513
) to the inequality (
S.512
), we prove the main inequality (
S.507
) as follows:

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\tau,t}

\displaystyle\leq 4\left\|O\right\|\cdot\left\|U_{\tau}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(U_{\tau})\right\|+4\left\|O\right\|\cdot\left\|U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}}(U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t})\right\|+4\left\|O\right\|\cdot\left\|U_{\tau}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{\rm c}}}(U_{\tau})\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+2\left\|O-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(O)\right\|+2\left\|O\right\|\cdot\left\|U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}}(U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t})\right\|+2\left\|O-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{\rm c}}}(O)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 8\left\|O\right\|\cdot\left\|U_{\tau}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(U_{\tau})\right\|+4\left\|O-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(O)\right\|+6\left\|O\right\|\cdot\left\|U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}}(U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t})\right\|.

(S.514)

This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
34
]

From Lemma
34
, we aim to estimate the terms in the RHS of (
S.507
) to derive the upper bound of
\varepsilon_{\tau,t}(O)
.
We first estimate the norm of
\left\|U_{\tau}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(U_{\tau})\right\|
and
\left\|O-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(O)\right\|
.
Using the inequality (
S.43
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|U_{\tau}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(U_{\tau})\right\|\leq\sum_{i\in\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}\sup_{u_{i}}\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|\leq\sum_{i\in\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}Q(d_{i,\mathfrak{L}},\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=r/2+1}^{\infty}\sum_{i\in\partial\mathfrak{L}[s]}Q(s,\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\gamma|\mathfrak{L}|\sum_{s=r/2+1}^{\infty}s^{D-1}Q(s,\tau),

(S.515)

where we use the fact that
\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}=\mathfrak{L}[r/2]^{\rm c}
from
\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{L}[r/2]
as in Eq. (
S.509
).
Note that
|\partial\mathfrak{L}[s]|\leq s^{D-1}|\partial\mathfrak{L}|\leq s^{D-1}|\mathfrak{L}|
using the
\gamma
in the inequality (
S.5
).
By employing the same analyses as the derivation of (
S.387
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{s>r/2}s^{D-1}Q(\tau,s)\leq 3\cdot 2^{D}\left[C_{1,\tau}^{D}+(r/2)^{D}\right]Q(\tau,r/2)\leq 2^{D+2}\left(C_{1,\tau}^{D}+r^{D}\right)Q(\tau,r/2).

(S.516)

By combining the inequalities (
S.515
) and (
S.516
), the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\left\|U_{\tau}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(U_{\tau})\right\|\leq 2^{D+2}\gamma|\mathfrak{L}|\left(C_{1,\tau}^{D}+r^{D}\right)Q(\tau,r/2).

(S.517)

Following the above analyses, we can derive the same inequality for
\left\|O-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(O)\right\|
using the assumption of (
S.449
):

\displaystyle\left\|O-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm c}}}(O)\right\|\leq 2^{D+2}\gamma\left\|O\right\|\cdot|\mathfrak{L}|\left(\check{C}_{1,\tau}^{D}+r^{D}\right)\check{Q}(\tau,r/2),

(S.518)

where we adopt a similar definition to
\check{C}_{1,\tau}
in Eq. (
S.379
) for
\check{C}_{\nu,\tau}
, i.e.,
\check{C}_{\nu,\tau}:=\frac{\tau+e}{2}+32\left(\nu^{2}D^{2}+\check{\kappa}_{1}^{2}\tau^{2}+\check{\kappa}_{\beta}\check{\kappa}_{0}\tau/8\right)/\kappa_{\beta}^{2}
.

We second estimate the norm of
\left\|U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}}(U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t})\right\|
.
From Eq. (
S.508
), we have

\displaystyle U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}:=e^{-iH_{0}t}e^{i(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}+H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}})t}=\mathcal{T}e^{-i\int_{0}^{t}\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}(H_{0},-x)dx},

(S.519)

\displaystyle\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}=\sum_{Z:Z\cap\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}\neq\emptyset,\ Z\cap\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}\neq\emptyset}h_{Z},

(S.520)

where the second notation comes from the definition of Eq. (
S.9
).
From the inequality (
S.49
), we can derive

\displaystyle\left\|U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}}(U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t})\right\|\leq\sup_{U_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}}}\left\|\left[U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t},U_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}}\right]\right\|\leq\int_{0}^{|t|}\sup_{U_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}}}\left\|\left[\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}(H_{0},-x),U_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}}\right]\right\|dx.

(S.521)

We then decompose
\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}
as
\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(1)}+\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(2)}
with

\displaystyle\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(1)}:=\sum_{Z:Z\cap\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}[-r/4]\neq\emptyset,\ Z\cap\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}\neq\emptyset}h_{Z},\quad\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(2)}:=\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}-\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(1)},

(S.522)

where
\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(2)}
is supported on
\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}[-r/4]^{\rm c}=\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}[r/4]^{\rm c}
from the definition.
By applying Lemma
1
to the norms of
\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(1)}
and
\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(2)}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(1)}\right\|\leq|\partial(\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}[-r/4])|\mathcal{J}_{r/4}\leq\tilde{J}_{0}|\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}|e^{-\mu r/8},\quad\left\|\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(2)}\right\|\leq|\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}|\mathcal{J}_{0}=\tilde{J}_{0}|\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}|,

(S.523)

which reduces the inequality (
S.521
) to

\displaystyle\left\|U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}}(U_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}},t})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2|t|\tilde{J}_{0}|\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}|e^{-\mu r/8}+\int_{0}^{|t|}\sup_{U_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}}}\left\|\left[h^{(2)}_{\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}(H_{0},-x),U_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}}\right]\right\|dx

\displaystyle\leq 2|t|\tilde{J}_{0}|\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}|e^{-\mu r/8}+\int_{0}^{|t|}\tilde{J}_{0}|\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}|\cdot\min\left[C|\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}|\left(e^{v|t|}-1\right)e^{-\mu r/4},1\right]dx

\displaystyle\leq\gamma|\mathfrak{L}|r^{D}\tilde{J}_{0}|t|\left[2e^{-\mu r/8}+\min\left(C\gamma|\mathfrak{L}|r^{D}e^{v|t|-\mu r/4},1\right)\right],

(S.524)

where we use
|\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}|=|\partial(\mathfrak{L}[r/2])|\leq\gamma|\mathfrak{L}|r^{D}
apply the Lieb–Robinson bound (
S.51
) in the second inequality.

By combining the inequalities (
S.517
), (
S.518
) and (
S.524
) with (
S.507
)
in Lemma
34
, we obtain

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\tau,t}(O)

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\left\|O\right\|\cdot|\mathfrak{L}|\Bigl\{2^{D+5}\left(C_{1,\tau}^{D}+r^{D}\right)Q(\tau,r/2)+2^{D+4}\left(\check{C}_{1,\tau}^{D}+r^{D}\right)\check{Q}(\tau,r/2)

\displaystyle\quad+6r^{D}\tilde{J}_{0}|t|\left[2e^{-\mu r/8}+\min\left(C\gamma|\mathfrak{L}|r^{D}e^{v|t|-\mu r/4},1\right)\right]\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq 6\gamma\left\|O\right\|\cdot|\mathfrak{L}|\Bigl\{2^{D+3}\left(\check{C}_{1,\tau}^{D}+r^{D}\right)\check{Q}(\tau,r/2)+\tilde{J}_{0}|t|r^{D}\left[2e^{-\mu r/8}+\min\left(C\gamma|\mathfrak{L}|r^{D}e^{v|t|-\mu r/4},1\right)\right]\Bigr\},

(S.525)

where we use
Q(\tau,r/2)\leq\check{Q}(\tau,r/2)
from the assumption.

By applying the inequality (
S.525
),
we upper-bound the terms in (
S.498
) that include
\epsilon_{\tau,t}
as

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\tau,t}(O)+4\left\|O\right\|\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{|t|}\varepsilon_{\tau_{1},x}(V)dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 6\gamma\left\|O\right\|\cdot|\mathfrak{L}|\left(4|t|\tau\left\|V\right\|+1\right)\Bigl\{2^{D+3}\left(\check{C}_{1,\tau}^{D}+r^{D}\right)\check{Q}(\tau,r/2)+\tilde{J}_{0}|t|r^{D}\left[2e^{-\mu r/8}+\min\left(C\gamma|\mathfrak{L}|r^{D}e^{v|t|-\mu r/4},1\right)\right]\Bigr\},

where, in considering the integrals of
\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{|t|}\varepsilon_{\tau_{1},x}(V)dx
, we use the property that the RHS of (
S.525
) monotonically increases with
\tau
and
|t|
.
By combining the above inequality with (
S.498
) in Lemma
33
,
we prove the main inequality (
S.451
).
This completes the proof of Proposition
30
.
\square

S.VI.3
Proof of Proposition
32

The basic proof relies on the proof of Proposition
24
.
We start from the simple upper bound of

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(V)-{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}}(V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left[\hat{H}_{\tau},V-V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right]\right\|+\left\|\left[\hat{H}_{\tau}-\hat{H}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right]\right\|.

(S.526)

First, from the expressions of
\hat{H}_{\tau}=U_{\tau}(H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau})U_{\tau}^{\dagger}
and
\hat{H}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}=U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}+\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}})U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\dagger}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left[\hat{H}_{\tau}-\hat{H}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right]\right\|=\left\|\left[\hat{H}_{\tau}-U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}U_{\tau}^{\dagger}U_{\tau}(H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}+\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}})U_{\tau}^{\dagger}U_{\tau}U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\dagger},V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\hat{H}_{\tau}-U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}U_{\tau}^{\dagger}\hat{H}_{\tau}U_{\tau}U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\dagger}\right\|+2\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\hat{V}_{\tau}-\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|+\left\|\left[U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\left(H_{0}-H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right)U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\dagger},V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[U_{\tau}U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\dagger},\hat{H}_{\tau}\right]\right\|+12\tau\left\|V\right\|^{2}\mathcal{E}(\tau,r)+\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},\widehat{H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}}\right]\right\|+\left\|\left[\widehat{H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}},V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right]\right\|,

(S.527)

where we use the definition (
S.8
) for
\widehat{H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}}
and the similar inequality to (
S.436
) in deriving

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{V}_{\tau}-\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|\leq 2\tau\left\|V-V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|+2\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\|\left[V,U_{\tau_{1}}U_{\tau_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\dagger}\right]\right\|d\tau_{1}\leq 2\tau\left\|V\right\|\left[\mathcal{E}(0,r)+2\mathcal{E}(\tau,r)\right]\leq 6\tau\left\|V\right\|\mathcal{E}(\tau,r).

(S.528)

Here, we use the definition of
\widehat{H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}}=H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}+\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}
and define the similar decomposition to Eq. (
S.522
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}=\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(1)}+\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(2)},\quad\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(1)}:=\sum_{Z:Z\cap\mathfrak{L}[r/2]\neq\emptyset,\ Z\cap\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}\neq\emptyset}h_{Z},\quad\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(2)}:=\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}-\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(1)},

(S.529)

where
h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(2)}
is supported on the subset
\mathfrak{L}[r/2]^{\rm c}
.
Using the inequality (
S.37
) in Lemma
2
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},\widehat{H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}}\right]\right\|+\left\|\left[\widehat{H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\rm c}}},V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right]\right\|=\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right]\right\|+\left\|\left[\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(1)}\right]\right\|+\left\|\left[\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(1)},V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right]\right\|+\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(2)}\right]\right\|+\left\|\left[\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(2)},V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 4\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(1)}\right\|+\left\|\partial h_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{(2)}\right\|\sum_{i\in\mathfrak{L}[r/2]^{\rm c}\setminus\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\sup_{u_{i}}\left(\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},u_{i}\right]\right\|+\left\|\left[V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},u_{i}\right]\right\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4\left\|V\right\|\tilde{J}_{0}|\partial\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}|\left[e^{-\mu r/4}+|\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}|\frac{Q(\tau,r/2)+Q(0,r/2)}{4}\right]\leq 6\left\|V\right\|\tilde{J}_{0}|\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}|^{2}Q(\tau,r/2),

(S.530)

where we use the similar inequality to (
S.523
) and the upper bounds of

\left\|\left[U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},u_{i}\right]\right\|\leq Q(\tau,d_{i,\mathfrak{L}})
and
\left\|\left[V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},u_{i}\right]\right\|\leq\left\|V\right\|Q(0,d_{i,\mathfrak{L}})
.

By applying the inequalities (
S.527
) and (
S.530
) to (
S.526
),
We obtain the upper bound of

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(V)-{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}}(V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left[\hat{H}_{\tau},V-V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right]\right\|+2\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[U_{\tau}U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\dagger},\hat{H}_{\tau}\right]\right\|+6\left\|V\right\|\left[\tau\mathcal{E}(\tau,r)+\tilde{J}_{0}|\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}|^{2}Q(\tau,r/2)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left[\Delta V,\hat{H}_{\tau}\right]\right\|+2\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[\Delta U_{\tau},\hat{H}_{\tau}\right]\right\|+6\left\|V\right\|\left(2\tau\left\|V\right\|+\tilde{J}_{0}|\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}|^{2}\right)\mathcal{E}(\tau,r),

(S.531)

where we define
\Delta V:=V-V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}
and
\Delta U_{\tau}:=U_{\tau}U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\dagger}-1
with
\left\|\Delta U_{\tau}\right\|\leq\left\|U_{\tau}-U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|
.

For the norm of
\Delta V
and
\Delta U_{\tau}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\left[V-V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},u_{i}\right]\right\|\leq 2\min\left(\left\|V-V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|,\left\|V\right\|Q(0,\ell)\right)\leq 2\left\|V\right\|\min\left[\mathcal{E}(\tau,r),Q(\tau,\ell)\right],

(S.532)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\left[U_{\tau}-U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},u_{i}\right]\right\|\leq 2\min\left(\left\|U_{\tau}-U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|,Q(\tau,\ell)\right)\leq 2\min\left[\mathcal{E}(\tau,r),Q(\tau,\ell)\right],

(S.533)

where
d_{i,\mathfrak{L}}=\ell
and we use the ansatz (
S.433
),
Q(0,\ell)\leq Q(\tau,\ell)
for the first upper bound and Subtheorem
1
for
U_{\tau}
and
U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}
in the second upper bound.
We can ensure that the commutators of
[U_{\tau},u_{i}]
and
[U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}},u_{i}]
satisfy the same upper bound from Subtheorem
1
.

We then estimate the norms of
\left\|\left[\Delta V,\hat{H}_{\tau}\right]\right\|
and
\left\|\left[\Delta U_{\tau},\hat{H}_{\tau}\right]\right\|
.
Using the inequality (
S.413
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(\Delta V)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\tau\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\Delta V\right\|+\left\|H_{0},\Delta V\right\|+\left\|\Delta V\right\|\cdot\left\|[U_{\tau},H_{0}]\right\|.

(S.534)

For the third term in the above inequality, we have already obtained the upper bound in (
S.417
) as

\displaystyle\left\|[U_{\tau},H_{0}]\right\|\leq 4\gamma^{3}|\mathfrak{L}|^{3}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}r^{D-1}\tilde{Q}(\tau,r)\leq 4\gamma^{3}|\mathfrak{L}|^{3}\cdot 2^{2D+4}C_{2,\tau}^{2D}C_{3,\tau}^{3D}\bar{J}_{0},

(S.535)

where we can let
\check{Q}(\tau,r)
be
Q(\tau,r)
in (
S.417
) because the estimation of
\left\|[U_{\tau},H_{0}]\right\|
does not depend on the choice of
O
in Proposition
24
.
By using
\left\|\Delta V\right\|\leq\left\|V\right\|\mathcal{E}(0,r)\leq\left\|V\right\|\mathcal{E}(\tau,r)
, we reduce the inequality (
S.534
) to

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(\Delta V)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|V\right\|\mathcal{E}(\tau,r)\left(\tau\left\|V\right\|+2^{2D+5}\gamma^{3}|\mathfrak{L}|^{3}C_{2,\tau}^{2D}C_{3,\tau}^{3D}\bar{J}_{0}\right)+\left\|H_{0},\Delta V\right\|.

(S.536)

Thus, the remaining task is refining the estimation of
\left\|H_{0},\Delta V\right\|
.

For this purpose, we adopt the decomposition of
\Delta V
as

\displaystyle\Delta V=\Delta\tilde{V}_{\mathfrak{L}[r^{\ast}]}+\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}\left(\Delta\tilde{V}_{\mathfrak{L}[r^{\ast}+s]}-\Delta\tilde{V}_{\mathfrak{L}[r^{\ast}+s-1]}\right),\quad\Delta\tilde{V}_{\mathfrak{L}[r^{\ast}+s]}:=\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{\mathfrak{L}[r^{\ast}+s]^{\rm c}}(\Delta V),

(S.537)

where
r^{\ast}
is chosen appropriately afterward.
We then obtain a similar inequality to (
S.398
) as

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta\tilde{V}_{\mathfrak{L}[r^{\ast}+s]}-\Delta\tilde{V}_{\mathfrak{L}[r^{\ast}+s-1]}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\mathfrak{L}[r^{\ast}+s]\setminus\mathfrak{L}[r^{\ast}+s-1]}\left\|[\Delta V,u_{i}]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2|\partial(\mathfrak{L}[r^{\ast}+s])|\cdot\left\|V\right\|\min\left[\mathcal{E}(\tau,r),Q(\tau,r^{\ast}+s)\right].

(S.538)

From the above inequality, we have a similar inequality to (
S.415
) as follows:

\displaystyle\left\|[H_{0},\Delta V]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{Z:Z\cap\mathfrak{L}[r^{\ast}]\neq\emptyset}\left\|h_{Z}\right\|\cdot\left\|\Delta\tilde{V}_{\mathfrak{L}[r^{\ast}]}\right\|+2\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}\sum_{Z:Z\cap\mathfrak{L}[r^{\ast}+s]\neq\emptyset}\left\|h_{Z}\right\|\cdot\left\|\Delta\tilde{V}_{\mathfrak{L}[r^{\ast}+s]}-\Delta\tilde{V}_{\mathfrak{L}[r^{\ast}+s-1]}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\bar{J}_{0}|\mathfrak{L}[r^{\ast}]|\cdot\left\|\Delta V\right\|+4\left\|V\right\|\sum_{s=0}^{\infty}|\mathfrak{L}[r^{\ast}+s]|\cdot|\partial(\mathfrak{L}[r^{\ast}+s])|\bar{J}_{0}\min\left[\mathcal{E}(\tau,r),Q(\tau,r^{\ast}+s)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\bar{J}_{0}\left\|V\right\|r^{\ast D}|\mathfrak{L}|\mathcal{E}(\tau,r)+4\bar{J}_{0}\left\|V\right\|\gamma|\mathfrak{L}|^{2}\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}(r^{\ast}+s)^{2D-1}\min\left[\mathcal{E}(\tau,r),Q(\tau,r^{\ast}+s)\right].

(S.539)

We can prove a similar lemma to Lemma
28
:

Lemma 35
.

Let
r^{\ast}
be the minimum number such that

\displaystyle\min\left[\mathcal{E}(\tau,r),Q(\tau,r^{\ast})\right]=Q(\tau,r^{\ast})\longrightarrow r^{\ast}=\Theta(\tau,r),

(S.540)

where we use the
\Theta
notation in Eq. (
S.14
).
Then, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}(r^{\ast}+s)^{2D-1}\min\left[\left\|V-V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|,Q(\tau,r^{\ast}+s)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2D+2}\left(C_{2,\tau}^{2D}+r^{\ast 2D}\right)Q(\tau,r^{\ast})

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2D+2}\left(C_{2,\tau}^{2D}+r^{\ast 2D}\right)\mathcal{E}(\tau,r),

(S.541)

where we use
Q(\tau,r^{\ast})\leq\mathcal{E}(\tau,r)
from Eq. (
S.540
) and adopt the definition of
C_{\nu,\tau}
in Eq. (
S.336
).

By applying Lemma
35
to the inequality (
S.539
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|[H_{0},\Delta V]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\bar{J}_{0}\left\|V\right\|\cdot|\mathfrak{L}|\mathcal{E}(\tau,r)\left[r^{\ast D}+2\gamma|\mathfrak{L}|\cdot 2^{2D+2}\left(C_{2,\tau}^{2D}+r^{\ast 2D}\right)\right],

(S.542)

which reduces the inequality (
S.536
) to

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(\Delta V)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|V\right\|\mathcal{E}(\tau,r)\left[\tau\left\|V\right\|+2^{2D+5}\gamma^{3}|\mathfrak{L}|^{3}C_{2,\tau}^{2D}C_{3,\tau}^{3D}\bar{J}_{0}+\bar{J}_{0}|\mathfrak{L}|r^{\ast D}+2^{2D+3}\bar{J}_{0}\gamma|\mathfrak{L}|^{2}\left(C_{2,\tau}^{2D}+r^{\ast 2D}\right)\right].

(S.543)

The same analyses yield

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(\Delta U_{\tau})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathcal{E}(\tau,r)\left[\tau\left\|V\right\|+2^{2D+5}\gamma^{3}|\mathfrak{L}|^{3}C_{2,\tau}^{2D}C_{3,\tau}^{3D}\bar{J}_{0}+\bar{J}_{0}|\mathfrak{L}|r^{\ast D}+2^{2D+3}\bar{J}_{0}\gamma|\mathfrak{L}|^{2}\left(C_{2,\tau}^{2D}+r^{\ast 2D}\right)\right].

(S.544)

By applying the above two inequalities (
S.542
) and (
S.544
) to
(
S.531
), we can derive the main inequality as

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(V)-{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}}(V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}})\right\|\leq\left\|\left[\Delta V,\hat{H}_{\tau}\right]\right\|+2\left\|V\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[\Delta U_{\tau},\hat{H}_{\tau}\right]\right\|+6\left\|V\right\|\left(2\tau\left\|V\right\|+\tilde{J}_{0}|\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}|^{2}\right)\mathcal{E}(\tau,r)

\displaystyle\leq 6\left\|V\right\|\mathcal{E}(\tau,r)\left[3\tau\left\|V\right\|+\tilde{J}_{0}|\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}|^{2}+\bar{J}_{0}|\mathfrak{L}|r^{\ast D}+2^{2D+3}\bar{J}_{0}\gamma|\mathfrak{L}|^{2}\left(C_{2,\tau}^{2D}+r^{\ast 2D}+4\gamma^{2}|\mathfrak{L}|C_{2,\tau}^{2D}C_{3,\tau}^{3D}\right)\right].

(S.545)

This completes the proof of Proposition
32
.
\square

S.VI.3.1
Proof of Lemma
35

From the definition of
r^{\ast}
in Eq. (
S.540
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}(r^{\ast}+s)^{2D-1}\min\left[\left\|V-V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|,Q(\tau,r^{\ast}+s)\right]=\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}(r^{\ast}+s)^{2D-1}Q(\tau,r^{\ast}+s)=\sum_{s>r^{\ast}}s^{2D-1}Q(\tau,s).

(S.546)

We next estimate an upper bound for
\sum_{s>r^{\ast}}s^{2D-1}Q(\tau,s)
.
The upper bound can be derived by the same calculations for the derivation of (
S.387
), which gives

\displaystyle\sum_{s>r^{\ast}}s^{2D-1}Q(\tau,s)\leq 3\cdot 2^{2D}(s_{0}^{\ast 2D}+r^{\ast 2D})Q(\tau,r^{\ast}),

(S.547)

where
s_{0}^{\ast}
is defined by

\displaystyle s^{2D-1}Q(\tau,s)\leq e^{-\kappa_{\beta}s/2}.

(S.548)

for
s\geq s_{0}^{\ast}
. Note that Lemma
27
with
\nu=2D-1
gives

\displaystyle s_{0}^{\ast}\leq\frac{\tau+e}{2}+\frac{32}{\kappa_{\beta}^{2}}\left[(2D-1)^{2}+\kappa_{1}^{2}\tau^{2}+\frac{\kappa_{\beta}\kappa_{0}\tau}{8}\right]\leq C_{2,\tau}.

(S.549)

By applying (
S.549
) to the inequality (
S.547
), we prove the main inequality (
S.541
). This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
35
]

S.VII
Improved bound for 1D cases

In Theorem
2
, the norm error between the unitary operator
U_{\tau}
and
U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}
is given by (
S.435
):

\displaystyle\left\|U_{\tau}-U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|\leq\exp\left[\Theta(\tau\beta^{D})\left\|V\right\|\log(\beta\left\|V\right\|\cdot|\mathfrak{L}|r\tau)-\kappa_{\beta}r/2\right],

(S.550)

which yields the behaviour of the RHS as
e^{\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\tau\beta^{D}\left\|V\right\|)-\Theta(r/\beta)}
from
\kappa_{\beta}\propto 1/\beta
.
Therefore, when we apply the above inequality to the BP formalism in one-dimensional systems as in (
S.225
), i.e.,

\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{XY}e^{\beta\left(\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{X}+H_{L[\ell]^{\rm c}}\right)}\tilde{\Phi}_{XY}^{\dagger}\right)=\log\left[\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{1}\tilde{\phi}_{XY,\tau}d\tau}e^{\beta\left(\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{X}+H_{L[\ell]^{\rm c}}\right)}\left(\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{1}\tilde{\phi}_{XY,\tau}d\tau}\right)^{\dagger}\right],

we have
\left\|V\right\|=\mathcal{O}(\beta)
from
\left\|\tilde{\phi}_{XY,\tau}\right\|\leq\beta\left\|\partial h_{L[\ell]}\right\|=\mathcal{O}(\beta)
[see also Eq. (
S.168
)] and
\tau=1
in (
S.550
), and hence, the RHS reduces to
e^{\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\beta^{2})-\mathcal{O}(r/\beta)}
.
This leads to the correlation length of the CMI in the form of
\mathcal{O}(\beta^{3})
.
Note that the operator
V
is now quasi-local around the surface region of
X
(or
Y
), and hence we can take
|\mathfrak{L}|=|\partial X|=1
.

In the present section, we aim to improve the dependence of
\beta
by adopting a different ansatz in Subtheorem
1
.
In detail, we prove the RHS as in
e^{\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\beta)-\mathcal{O}(r/\beta)}
, which is expected to be optimal since it gives qualitatively the same behavior as for the quasi-locality of the belief propagation operator [see the inequality (
S.135
)].
We prove the following theorem:

Theorem 3
.

Let us adopt the same setup as in Theorem
2
.
Then, we prove

\displaystyle\left\|U_{\tau}-U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|\leq\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(\tau,r)=\left[1+2\tau\mathcal{K}_{2}\right]e^{\kappa_{0}^{(1)}\tau+\kappa_{1}^{(1)}\tau\log(r+\tau+e)-\kappa_{\beta}r/2},

(S.551)

where
\mathcal{K}_{2}
has been defined in Eq. (
S.434
) and
\kappa_{0}^{(1)}
and
\kappa_{1}^{(1)}
are defined in similar ways to Eq. (
S.299
):

\displaystyle\kappa_{0}^{(1)}=\Theta(1)\left\|V\right\|\log(\beta\left\|V\right\|\cdot|\mathfrak{L}|),\quad\kappa_{1}^{(1)}=\Theta(1)\left\|V\right\|.

(S.552)

Remark.
Under the choice as in (
S.225
) for the BP formalism,
we have
V\to\tilde{\phi}_{XY,\tau}
(
\left\|V\right\|=\mathcal{O}(\beta)
),
\tau\to 1
and
|\mathfrak{L}|=|\partial X|=\mathcal{O}(1)
.
We thus reduce the upper bound (
S.551
) to

\displaystyle\left\|U_{\tau=1}-U_{\tau=1,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta r)-\kappa_{\beta}r/2},

(S.553)

where we let
\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}=\partial{X}[r]
.
We, therefore, obtain the desired upper bound.

Using the same analyses for the derivation of (
S.437
), we can obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{XY}e^{\beta\left(\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{X}+H_{L[\ell]^{\rm c}}\right)}\tilde{\Phi}_{XY}^{\dagger}\right)-U_{\tau=1,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\left[\beta\left(\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{X}+H_{L[\ell]^{\rm c}}\right)+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\tau=1,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right]U_{\tau=1,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\dagger}\right\|\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta r)-\kappa_{\beta}r/2},

(S.554)

where we use Eq. (
S.243
), which define
\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\tau=1,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}
as

\displaystyle\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\tau=1,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}:=2\int_{0}^{1}U_{\tau_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\phi}_{XY,\tau_{1}}U_{\tau_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}d\tau_{1}.

(S.555)

S.VII.1
Proof of Theorem
3

We follow the same proof techniques for Subtheorem
1
and Theorem
2
.
We start with the improvement of Subtheorem
1
.
In the improvement, we adopt a different ansatz to characterize the quasi-locality of the unitary operator
U_{\tau}
so that we can utilize Lemma
7
.
For this purpose, let
u_{X}
be an arbitrary unitary operator defined on the subset
X
separated from the subset
\mathfrak{L}
by distance
r
.
We aim to prove an upper bound of the commutator norm in the form of

\displaystyle\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{X}]\right\|\leq Q^{(1)}(\tau,r)=e^{\kappa_{0}^{(1)}\tau+\kappa_{1}^{(1)}\tau\log(r+\tau+e)-\kappa_{\beta}r}\quad(d_{X,\mathfrak{L}}=r).

(S.556)

Almost all the proof techniques are applied to the ansatz (
S.556
).
The differences arise from the estimations of the parameters
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{3}
in Eqs. (
S.325
) and (
S.341
), respectively.
For the modifications of Propositions
23
and
25
, we utilize Lemma
7
instead of Lemma
5
.
We then prove the following proposition:

Proposition 36
.

Let us adopt the same setup as in Propositions
23
and
25
.
We also choose
\mathcal{F}(\ell)
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\ell)=\exp\left(K_{\ell}-\kappa_{\beta}\ell\right),

(S.557)

where
K_{\ell}
monotonically increases with
\ell
.
We then obtain

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{1}(t)\left\|[O,u_{X}(H_{0},t)]\right\|dt\leq\mathfrak{g}_{1}^{(1)}\mathcal{F}(\ell),\and

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{1}(t)\int_{0}^{t}\left\|[O,u_{X}(H_{0},t_{1})]\right\|dt_{1}dt\leq\mathfrak{g}_{3}^{(1)}\mathcal{F}(\ell),

(S.558)

with
g_{1}(t)
defined in Eq. (
S.323
), i.e.,

\displaystyle g_{1}(t)=\begin{cases}|g_{\beta}(t)|&\quad\textrm{for}\quad|t|\geq\delta t,\\
0&\quad\textrm{for}\quad|t|<\delta t,\end{cases}

(S.559)

where
\mathfrak{g}_{1}^{(1)}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{3}^{(1)}
are defined as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{1}^{(1)}:=\beta\left[\frac{41C|\partial\mathfrak{L}|}{\pi v}+\frac{4\left(1+4e^{2\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\right)}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\beta}{2\pi\delta t}\right)\right],

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{3}^{(1)}:=\beta^{2}\left[\frac{1}{6}+\frac{703C|\partial\mathfrak{L}|}{\pi v^{2}\beta}+2e^{\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\left(\frac{3\mu\Delta\ell}{\pi v\beta}+\frac{1}{6}+\frac{1}{6}e^{\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\right)\right].

(S.560)

Here, the parameter
\Delta\ell
can be arbitrarily chosen such that
\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell\geq 1
(or
1/(\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell)\leq 1
).

On the estimations of
g_{\tau,r}
,
g^{\prime}_{\tau}
,
\mathfrak{g}_{2}
, and
\mathfrak{g}_{4}
in Propositions
23
,
24
, and
25
, we use the previous loose estimate based on the ansatz for
\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|
.
The upper bound for
\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{X}]\right\|
is trivially applied to the commutator with a local unitary operator
u_{i}
, i.e.,
\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|\leq Q^{(1)}(\tau,r)
for
d_{i,\mathfrak{L}}=r
.
On the other hand, from the local commutator bound
\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{i}]\right\|
, one can upper-bound
\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{X}]\right\|
using the following general lemma:

Lemma 37
.

We adopt a similar setup to Proposition
23
.
Let
O
be an arbitrary operator with
\left\|[O,u_{i}]\right\|\leq\mathcal{F}(\ell)=e^{K_{\ell}-\kappa_{\beta}\ell}
for
\forall i\in\Lambda
with
\ell=d_{i,\mathfrak{L}}
, we obtain the upper bound of

\displaystyle\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{X}]\right\|\leq 2^{D+2}\gamma|\mathfrak{L}|\left(\ell^{D}+{s^{\ast}}^{D}\right)\mathcal{F}(\ell),

(S.561)

with
s^{\ast}
the solution of the following equation

\displaystyle s^{\ast}=\frac{2}{\kappa_{\beta}}\left[K_{s^{\ast}}+(D-1)\log(s^{\ast})\right],

(S.562)

where
\mathcal{F}(\ell)
monotonically decreases with
\ell
.

Remark.
If
K_{\ell}\leq\Theta[\log(\ell)]+{\rm poly}(\left\|V\right\|,\beta,|\mathfrak{L}|,\tau)
, the solution of Eq. (
S.562
) gives

\displaystyle s^{\ast}={\rm poly}(\left\|V\right\|,\beta,|\mathfrak{L}|,\tau),

(S.563)

and hence, we can obtain

\displaystyle\left\|[U_{\tau},u_{X}]\right\|\leq 2^{D+2}\gamma|\mathfrak{L}|\left(\ell^{D}+{s^{\ast}}^{D}\right)\mathcal{F}(\ell)\leq{\rm poly}(\left\|V\right\|,\beta,|\mathfrak{L}|,\tau)\mathcal{F}(\ell).

(S.564)

The norm increases by
{\rm poly}(\left\|V\right\|,\beta,|\mathfrak{L}|,\tau)
can be cancelled by

Proof of Lemma
37
.

First, using the inequality (
S.37
) in Lemma
2
, we can derive

\displaystyle\left\|[O,u_{X}]\right\|\leq\sum_{i\in X}\sup_{u_{i}}\left\|[O,u_{i}]\right\|\leq\sum_{i\in X}\mathcal{F}(d_{i,\mathfrak{L}})\leq\sum_{i\in\mathfrak{L}[\ell]^{\rm c}}\mathcal{F}(d_{i,\mathfrak{L}}).

(S.565)

We then obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathfrak{L}[\ell]^{\rm c}}\mathcal{F}(d_{i,\mathfrak{L}})\leq\sum_{j\in\mathfrak{L}}\sum_{i:d_{i,j}>\ell}\mathcal{F}(d_{i,j})\leq\sum_{j\in\mathfrak{L}}\sum_{q=1}^{\infty}\partial(j[\ell+q])\mathcal{F}(\ell+q)\leq|\mathfrak{L}|\gamma\sum_{s>\ell}s^{D-1}\mathcal{F}(s).

(S.566)

We now rely on similar analyses to the proof of Lemma
28
.
We define
s^{\ast}
as the constant such that

\displaystyle(s^{\ast})^{D-1}\mathcal{F}(s^{\ast})=(s^{\ast})^{D-1}e^{K_{s^{\ast}}-\kappa_{\beta}s^{\ast}}=e^{-\kappa_{\beta}s^{\ast}/2},

(S.567)

which reduces to the equation (
S.562
).
In the case where
\ell\geq s^{\ast}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s>\ell}s^{D-1}\mathcal{F}(s)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell<s\leq 2\ell}s^{D-1}\mathcal{F}(s)+\sum_{s>2\ell}s^{D-1}\mathcal{F}(s)

\displaystyle\leq(2\ell)^{D}\mathcal{F}(\ell)+\sum_{s>2\ell}e^{-\kappa_{\beta}\ell/2}=(2\ell)^{D}\mathcal{F}(\ell)+\frac{e^{-K_{\ell}}}{1-e^{-\kappa_{\beta}}}\mathcal{F}(\ell).

(S.568)

In the case where
\ell<s^{\ast}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s>\ell}s^{D-1}\mathcal{F}(s)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell<s\leq s^{\ast}}s^{D-1}\mathcal{F}(s)+\sum_{s>s^{\ast}}s^{D-1}\mathcal{F}(s)

\displaystyle\leq(s^{\ast})^{D}\mathcal{F}(\ell)+\sum_{s>s^{\ast}}e^{-\kappa_{\beta}\ell/2}\leq(s^{\ast})^{D}\mathcal{F}(\ell)+(2s^{\ast})^{D}\mathcal{F}(s^{\ast})+\frac{e^{-K_{s^{\ast}}}}{1-e^{-\kappa_{\beta}}}\mathcal{F}(s^{\ast})

\displaystyle\leq\left(2(2s^{\ast})^{D}+\frac{e^{-K_{s^{\ast}}}}{1-e^{-\kappa_{\beta}}}\right)\mathcal{F}(\ell)

(S.569)

By combining the above two upper bounds, the following inequality always holds:

\displaystyle\sum_{s>\ell}s^{D-1}\mathcal{F}(s)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{D+2}\left(\ell^{D}+{s^{\ast}}^{D}\right)\mathcal{F}(\ell).

(S.570)

By applying the inequalities (
S.566
) and (
S.570
) to
(
S.565
), we prove the main inequality (
S.561
).
This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
37
]

By using Lemma
37
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{2}(t)\left\|[O,u_{X}(H_{0},\lambda t)]\right\|dt

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in X}\sup_{u_{i}}\int_{|t|\leq\delta t}|tg_{\beta}(t)|\cdot\left\|[O,u_{i}(H_{0},\lambda t)]\right\|dt

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{g}_{2}\delta t\sum_{i\in X}\mathcal{F}(d_{i,\mathfrak{L}})\leq\mathfrak{g}_{2}\delta\tilde{t}\mathcal{F}(\ell),

(S.571)

where we let
\delta\tilde{t}:=2^{D+2}\gamma|\mathfrak{L}|\left(\ell^{D}+{s^{\ast}}^{D}\right)\delta t\leq{\rm poly}(\left\|V\right\|,\beta,|\mathfrak{L}|,\tau)\delta t
.
Therefore, by replacing the
\delta t
by
\delta\tilde{t}
, we can obtain the same upper bound for the summation including
|tg_{\beta}(t)|
, i.e.,
in the inequalities (
S.339
) and (
S.346
).
Here, we can obtain the same estimations for
\mathfrak{g}_{2}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{4}
in Propositions
23
and
25
as
\mathfrak{g}_{2},\mathfrak{g}_{4}\leq\Theta(\beta)
.

The remaining part of the proof is the same, and we can replace the inequality (
S.351
) with

\displaystyle\frac{2\left\|V\right\|}{\beta}\left[4\mathfrak{g}_{1}^{(1)}+\mathfrak{g}_{2}+\frac{6}{\kappa_{0}^{(1)}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{3}^{(1)}\left\|V\right\|+\frac{\mathfrak{g}_{4}}{9g^{\prime}_{\tau}}\right)\right]\leq\kappa_{0}^{(1)}+\kappa_{1}^{(1)}\log(r+\tau+e),

(S.572)

where we adopt the same choice for
\delta\tilde{t}
as in Eq. (
S.348
), i.e.,
\delta\tilde{t}^{-1}={\rm poly}(\left\|V\right\|,|\mathfrak{L}|,\beta,\kappa_{0}^{(1)},\kappa_{1}^{(1)},\tau)
, which also implies
\delta t={\rm poly}(\left\|V\right\|,|\mathfrak{L}|,\beta,\kappa_{0}^{(1)},\kappa_{1}^{(1)},\tau)
.
Moreover, using Eq. (
S.560
) in Proposition
36
,
we can replace the inequality (
S.352
) by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{1}^{(1)}\leq\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta/\delta t),\quad\mathfrak{g}_{4}\leq\mathfrak{g}_{2}\leq\Theta(\beta),\quad\mathfrak{g}_{3}^{(1)}\leq\Theta(\beta^{2}).

(S.573)

Therefore, the choices of (
S.552
) satisfy the inequality (
S.572
).
We thus achieve the improvement of Subtheorem
1
to the form of (
S.556
) in one-dimensional systems.

Finally, we will prove the main statement (
S.551
), which improves Theorem
2
.
On this point, we do not need any modification.
To see it, we start with the final conditions for
\{\mathcal{K}_{0},\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2}\}
in the form of (
S.495
).
Now, we denote the parameters by
\{\mathcal{K}_{0}^{(1)},\mathcal{K}_{1}^{(1)},\mathcal{K}_{2}\}
in one-dimensional systems, which should satisfy

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{0}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\max\Bigl[\kappa_{0}^{(1)},\Theta(\left\|V\right\|)\log(\beta\left\|V\right\|\cdot|\mathfrak{L}|)\Bigr],\quad\mathcal{K}_{1}^{(1)}=\max\Bigl[\kappa_{1}^{(1)},\Theta(\left\|V\right\|)\Bigr],

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{2}

\displaystyle=\max\left[\Theta(\left\|V\right\|)\left(\log(\beta\left\|V\right\|\cdot|\mathfrak{L}|)+\log(r+\tau+e)+\beta\left\|V\right\|\right),\Theta\left(\left\|V\right\|^{2D+3},|\mathfrak{L}|^{3},\beta^{2D^{2}+2D+2},\tau^{2D+2},r^{2D+3}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\Theta\left(\left\|V\right\|^{2D+3},|\mathfrak{L}|^{3},\beta^{2D^{2}+2D+2},\tau^{2D+2},r^{2D+3}\right).

(S.574)

By applying the forms of
\kappa_{0}^{(1)}
and
\kappa_{1}^{(1)}
, i.e.,
\kappa_{0}^{(1)}=\Theta(1)\left\|V\right\|\log(\beta\left\|V\right\|\cdot|\mathfrak{L}|)
and
\kappa_{1}^{(1)}=\Theta(1)\left\|V\right\|
, we ensure that the choices
\mathcal{K}_{0}^{(1)}=\kappa_{0}^{(1)}
and
\mathcal{K}_{1}^{(1)}=\kappa_{1}^{(1)}
satisfy the above conditions.
Therefore, we prove the main inequality (
S.551
)
This completes the proof of Theorem
3
.
\square

S.VII.2
Proof of Proposition
36

The proof strategy is the same as those for Propositions
23
and
25
.
First of all, because of the condition
\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell\geq 1
(or
1/(\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell)\leq 1
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\pi\mu}{2v\beta}\Delta\ell\geq 1\and\frac{\mu\Delta\ell}{4}\geq 1.

(S.575)

We recall that
\kappa_{\beta}
has been defined as
\kappa_{\beta}:=\min\left(\frac{\pi\mu}{2v\beta},\frac{\mu}{4}\right)
in Eq. (
S.121
).

We start from the former part in (
S.560
).
Using the inequality (
S.358
) in Lemma
26
, the parameters
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
in (
S.59
) immediately given by

\displaystyle f_{1}=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{1}(t)dt\leq\frac{2\beta}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\beta}{2\pi\delta t}\right),

\displaystyle f_{2}=\int_{|t|\leq t_{0}}|t|g_{1}(t)dt\leq\frac{\beta}{2\pi}t_{0}^{2}\leq\frac{\beta}{2\pi}\left(\frac{\mu s\Delta\ell}{2v}\right)^{2},

(S.576)

where, in the second inequality, we use the inequality (
S.359
) by replacing
\delta t
with
t_{0}
.
Note that
t_{0}
has been defined as
t_{0}=[\mu/(2v)]\Delta\ell(s-2)
, where we can arbitrarily control the value of
\Delta\ell
.
For
f_{t_{0}}(s)
, we can utilize the same inequality as (
S.364
):

\displaystyle f_{t_{0}}(s)\leq\frac{\beta}{\pi}\log\left(e+\frac{e\beta}{2\pi t_{0}}\right)e^{-2\pi t_{0}/\beta}\leq\frac{\beta}{\pi}\log\left(e+\frac{e}{2}\right)e^{-2\kappa_{\beta}(s-2)\Delta\ell}\leq\frac{2\beta}{\pi}e^{-2\kappa_{\beta}(s-2)\Delta\ell}

(S.577)

for
s\geq 3
,
where we use
2\pi t_{0}/\beta=[\pi\mu/(v\beta)](s-2)\Delta\ell\geq 2\kappa_{\beta}(s-2)\Delta\ell\geq 2(s-2)
.
For
s\leq 2
, we can simply obtain
f_{t_{0}}(s)=f_{1}
since
t_{0}\leq 0
.

Then, by applying the upper bounds for
f_{2}
and
f_{t_{0}}(s)
to (
S.77
), we first obtain

\displaystyle 2\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{F}(\ell-s\Delta\ell)C|\partial(\mathfrak{L}[\ell])|f_{2}ve^{-\mu s\Delta\ell/2}\leq 4Cv|\partial\mathfrak{L}|\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}e^{K_{\ell-s\Delta\ell}-\kappa_{\beta}(\ell-s\Delta\ell)}\cdot\frac{\beta}{2\pi}\left(\frac{\mu\Delta\ell}{2v}\right)^{2}s^{2}e^{-\mu s\Delta\ell/2}

\displaystyle\leq 4Cv|\partial\mathfrak{L}|\frac{\beta}{2\pi}\left(\frac{\mu\Delta\ell}{2v}\right)^{2}\mathcal{F}(\ell)\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}s^{2}e^{\kappa_{\beta}s\Delta\ell-\mu s\Delta\ell/2}\leq 4Cv|\partial\mathfrak{L}|\frac{\beta}{2\pi}\left(\frac{\mu\Delta\ell}{2v}\right)^{2}\mathcal{F}(\ell)\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}s^{2}e^{-\mu s\Delta\ell/4}

\displaystyle\leq 4Cv|\partial\mathfrak{L}|\frac{\beta}{2\pi}\left(\frac{\mu\Delta\ell}{2v}\right)^{2}\mathcal{F}(\ell)e^{-\mu\Delta\ell/4}\left(1+2^{2}\cdot 2!\left[\max\left(1,\frac{4}{\mu\Delta\ell}\right)\right]^{3}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2Cv|\partial\mathfrak{L}|\beta}{\pi}\mathcal{F}(\ell)e^{-\mu\Delta\ell/4}\left(\frac{\mu\Delta\ell}{2v}\right)^{2}\left(1+2^{3}\right)=\frac{41C|\partial\mathfrak{L}|\beta}{\pi v}\mathcal{F}(\ell),

(S.578)

where we use
|\partial(\mathfrak{L}[\ell])|\leq 2|\partial\mathfrak{L}|
in one-dimensional systems in the first inequality,
[
166
, (S.11) of Lemma 1 therein]
in the fourth inequality, and the following inequality for the last inequality:

\displaystyle e^{-\mu\Delta\ell/4}(\mu\Delta\ell)^{2}\leq 8.66146\cdots\leq 9,

(S.579)

which is derived under the condition of
\mu\Delta\ell\geq 4
in (
S.575
).
In the same way, we secondly obtain

\displaystyle 2\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{F}(\ell-s\Delta\ell)f_{t_{0}}(s)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathcal{F}(\ell)\left[f_{1}e^{\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\left(1+e^{\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\right)+\sum_{s=3}^{\infty}e^{\kappa_{\beta}s\Delta\ell}\cdot\frac{2\beta}{\pi}e^{-2\kappa_{\beta}(s-2)\Delta\ell}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathcal{F}(\ell)\left[2e^{2\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\cdot\frac{2\beta}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\beta}{2\pi\delta t}\right)+\frac{2\beta}{\pi}\frac{e^{\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}}{1-e^{-\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16\beta}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\beta}{2\pi\delta t}\right)e^{2\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\mathcal{F}(\ell),

(S.580)

where we use
\left(1-e^{-\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\right)^{-1}\leq 2
from
\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell\geq 1
.
Therefore, by combining the inequalities (
S.576
), (
S.578
)
and (
S.580
), we obtain

\displaystyle 2f_{1}\mathcal{F}(\ell)+2\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{F}(\ell-s\Delta\ell)\left[C|\partial(\mathfrak{L}[\ell])|f_{2}ve^{-\mu s\Delta\ell/2}+f_{t_{0}}(s)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\beta\mathcal{F}(\ell)\left[\frac{41C|\partial\mathfrak{L}|}{\pi v}+\frac{4\left(1+4e^{2\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\right)}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\beta}{2\pi\delta t}\right)\right].

(S.581)

We thus prove the first main inequality (
S.558
) with
\mathfrak{g}_{1}^{(1)}
given by Eq. (
S.560
) from (
S.77
) in Lemma
7
.

We next proceed to the latter part. The proof itself is quite similar to the proof for the former part.
For the proof, from Corollary
6
,
we use Lemma
7
by replacing
f(t)=|tg_{1}(t)|
, where

has been defined in the same way as in Eq. (
S.323
).
Therefore, the parameters
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
in (

) are upper-bounded using (

as follows:

\displaystyle f_{1}=\int_{|t|>\delta t}|tg_{\beta}(t)|dt\leq\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi^{2}}\zeta(2)=\frac{\beta^{2}}{12},

\displaystyle f_{2}=\int_{|t|\leq t_{0}}|t^{2}g_{\beta}(t)|dt\leq\frac{\beta t_{0}^{3}}{3\pi}=\frac{\beta}{3\pi}\left(\frac{\mu s\Delta\ell}{2v}\right)^{3},

(S.582)

where we use the inequality (
S.359
) in the second inequality.
Then, by following the same analyses as (
S.421
) and (
S.577
), we can obtain

\displaystyle f_{t_{0}}(s):=\int_{|t|>t_{0}}|tg_{\beta}(t)|dt

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta t_{0}}{\pi}\log\left(e+\frac{e\beta}{2\pi t_{0}}\right)e^{-2\pi t_{0}/\beta}+\frac{\beta^{2}}{12}e^{-2\pi t_{0}/\beta}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{\mu\beta}{\pi v}(s-2)\Delta\ell+\frac{\beta^{2}}{12}\right]e^{-2\kappa_{\beta}(s-2)\Delta\ell}

(S.583)

for
s\geq 3
, and we have
f_{t_{0}}(s)\leq f_{1}
for

.

Then, from the above
f_{2}
and
f_{t_{0}}(s)
, we upper-bound the second term in the RHS of (
S.77
).
Following a similar analysis to (
S.578
), we obtain

\displaystyle 2\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{F}(\ell-s\Delta\ell)C|\partial(\mathfrak{L}[\ell])|f_{2}ve^{-\mu s\Delta\ell/2}\leq 4Cv|\partial\mathfrak{L}|\frac{\beta}{3\pi}\left(\frac{\mu\Delta\ell}{2v}\right)^{3}\mathcal{F}(\ell)\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}s^{3}e^{\kappa_{\beta}s\Delta\ell-\mu s\Delta\ell/2}

\displaystyle\leq 4Cv|\partial\mathfrak{L}|\frac{\beta}{3\pi}\left(\frac{\mu\Delta\ell}{2v}\right)^{3}\mathcal{F}(\ell)e^{-\mu\Delta\ell/4}\left(1+2^{3}\cdot 3!\left[\max\left(1,\frac{4}{\mu\Delta\ell}\right)\right]^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4Cv|\partial\mathfrak{L}|\frac{\beta}{3\pi}\left(\frac{\mu\Delta\ell}{2v}\right)^{3}\mathcal{F}(\ell)e^{-\mu\Delta\ell/4}\left(1+2^{3}\cdot 3!\right)\leq\frac{703C|\partial\mathfrak{L}|\beta}{\pi v^{2}}\mathcal{F}(\ell),

(S.584)

where, in the last inequality, we use

\displaystyle e^{-\mu\Delta\ell/4}(\mu\Delta\ell)^{3}\leq 86.0321\cdots

(S.585)

from

as in (

).
Also, by replacing the function

) by the form of (

), we have the following inequality:

\displaystyle 2\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{F}(\ell-s\Delta\ell)f_{t_{0}}(s)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathcal{F}(\ell)\left\{f_{1}e^{\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\left(1+e^{\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\right)+\sum_{s=3}^{\infty}e^{\kappa_{\beta}s\Delta\ell}\cdot\left[\frac{\mu\beta}{\pi v}(s-2)\Delta\ell+\frac{\beta^{2}}{12}\right]e^{-2\kappa_{\beta}(s-2)\Delta\ell}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\beta^{2}\mathcal{F}(\ell)e^{\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\left(\frac{3\mu\Delta\ell}{\pi v\beta}+\frac{1}{6}+\frac{1}{6}e^{\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\right),

(S.586)

where we use the following inequality:

\displaystyle\sum_{s=3}^{\infty}e^{\kappa_{\beta}s\Delta\ell}\cdot\left[\frac{\mu\beta}{\pi v}(s-2)\Delta\ell+\frac{\beta^{2}}{12}\right]e^{-2\kappa_{\beta}(s-2)\Delta\ell}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu\beta\Delta\ell}{\pi v}e^{2\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}se^{-\kappa_{\beta}s\Delta\ell}+\frac{\beta^{2}}{12}e^{4\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\sum_{s=3}^{\infty}e^{-\kappa_{\beta}s\Delta\ell}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu\beta\Delta\ell}{\pi v}e^{\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\left(1+2\left[\max\left(1,\frac{1}{\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\right)\right]^{2}\right)+\frac{\beta^{2}}{12}\frac{e^{\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}}{1-e^{-\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}}\leq\left(\frac{3\mu\beta\Delta\ell}{\pi v}+\frac{\beta^{2}}{6}\right)e^{\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}.

(S.587)

Note that in the above inequality, we use
1/(\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell)\leq 1
(
\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell\geq 1
).
By using the above inequalities (
S.584
) and (
S.586
) with
f_{1}\leq\beta^{2}/12
, we obtain

\displaystyle 2f_{1}\mathcal{F}(\ell)+2\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{F}(\ell-s\Delta\ell)\left[C|\partial(\mathfrak{L}[\ell])|f_{2}ve^{-\mu s\Delta\ell/2}+f_{t_{0}}(s)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\beta^{2}\mathcal{F}(\ell)\left[\frac{1}{6}+\frac{703C|\partial\mathfrak{L}|}{\pi v^{2}\beta}+2e^{\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\left(\frac{3\mu\Delta\ell}{\pi v\beta}+\frac{1}{6}+\frac{1}{6}e^{\kappa_{\beta}\Delta\ell}\right)\right].

(S.588)

Therefore, by combining the inequalities (
S.576
), (
S.578
)
and (
S.580
), we obtain the second inequality in (
S.558
) with
\mathfrak{g}_{3}^{(1)}
provided by Eq. (
S.560
).
This completes the proof of Proposition
36
.
\square

S.VIII
Clustering theorem for conditional mutual information at arbitrary temperatures

S.VIII.1
High-dimensional systems

We now have all the ingredients to prove the clustering theorem for conditional mutual information.
We begin with the general dimensional cases, which can be treated by the PTP formalism in Sec.
S.III.3
.
We prove the following theorem on the decay of the conditional mutual information:

Theorem 4
.

Using
\Theta
notation in Eq. (
S.14
), we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:C|B)\leq\mathcal{D}_{AC}e^{-R/\Theta\left[\beta^{D+1}\log\left(R\right)\right]+\Theta(1)\log(R)}

(S.589)

for an arbitrary tripartition of the total system
\Lambda=A\sqcup B\sqcup C
,
where
R=d_{A,C}
, and
\mathcal{D}_{AC}
has been defined as the Hilbert space dimension on the subset
AC
.

Remark.
From the upper bound, the conditional mutual information decays beyond the distance
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left[\beta^{D+1}\log(\mathcal{D}_{AC})\right]
, and the asymptotic decay is given by
e^{-R/\log(R)}
.
Therefore, when
|A|
and
|C|
is order of
1
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:C|B)\leq e^{-R/\Theta\left[\beta^{D+1}\log\left(R\right)\right]}.

(S.590)

This gives the pairwise Markov structure in general quantum Gibbs states, while we cannot ensure the local and the global Markov structures, which require
\max(|A|,|C|)=\mathcal{O}(|\Lambda|)
.

The RHS of the inequality (
S.589
) has the dependence of
e^{\Theta\left(|AC|\right)}
, which originates from the coefficient
\mathcal{D}_{AC}=e^{\Theta(|AC|)}
.
This condition is necessary so that the approximate PTP operator
P_{\tau}
has a sufficiently small error to ensure the exact partial trace.
In the proof, it appears in the inequality (
S.592
).
As will be proven in the subsequent section
S.VIII.2
, we can resolve this point in one-dimensional cases.

S.VIII.1.1
Proof of Theorem
4

By applying Lemma
16
and Corollary
17
to the quantum Gibbs state
\rho_{\beta}
, we upper-bound the conditional mutual information (see also Fig.
13
).
For the convenience of readers, we show the statement again:

Lemma
16
and Corollary
17
(restatement).

Let
\rho_{\beta,L^{\rm c},\tau}
be defined as

\displaystyle\rho_{\beta,L^{\rm c},\tau}:=\frac{{\mathcal{P}}_{L,\tau}\rho_{\beta}{\mathcal{P}}_{L,\tau}}{{\rm tr}\left({\mathcal{P}}_{L,\tau}\rho_{\beta}{\mathcal{P}}_{L,\tau}\right)}=\frac{e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\rho_{\beta}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}}{{\rm tr}\left(e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\rho_{\beta}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\right)}.

(S.591)

Then, for an arbitrary tripartition of
\Lambda=A\sqcup B\sqcup C
, the quantum conditional mutual information
{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:C|B)
is upper-bounded as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:C|B)\leq 8e^{-\tau}\mathcal{D}_{AC}\left(\mathcal{\chi}_{\tau,\rho_{\beta},AB}+\mathcal{\chi}_{\tau,\rho_{\beta},BC}+\mathcal{\chi}_{\tau,\rho_{\beta},B}\right)+\left\|H_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)\right\|,

(S.592)

where we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{\chi}_{\tau,\rho_{\beta},L^{\rm c}}:=\sup_{u_{L}}\left\|\left[\log\left(\rho_{\beta,L^{\rm c},\tau}\right),u_{L}\right]\right\|+\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{\tau-\tau_{1}}\sup_{u_{L}}\left\|\left[\log\left(\rho_{\beta,L^{\rm c},\tau_{1}}\right),u_{L}\right]\right\|d\tau_{1}.

(S.593)

and

\displaystyle H_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B):=-\log(\rho_{\beta,AB,\tau})-\log(\rho_{\beta,BC,\tau})+\log(\rho_{\beta,ABC,\tau})+\log(\rho_{\beta,B,\tau}).

(S.594)

To estimate the RHS of the inequality (
S.592
), we begin with upper-bounding
\mathcal{\chi}_{\tau,\rho_{\beta},L^{\rm c}}
.
We prove the following lemma:

Lemma 38
.

Using the inequality (
S.335
) with
O\to u_{L}
and
V\to{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}
(
\left\|{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}\right\|=1
) in Proposition
24
, we can upper-bound the parameter
\mathcal{\chi}_{\tau,\rho_{\beta},L^{\rm c}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{\chi}_{\tau,\rho_{\beta},L^{\rm c}}\leq\Theta(\beta^{10D},\tau^{10D+1},|L|^{3}),

(S.595)

where we use the
\Theta
notation in Eq. (
S.14
).

Proof of Lemma
38
.

We first note that

\displaystyle\log\left(\rho_{\beta,L^{\rm c},\tau}\right)=\log\left(\frac{e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\rho_{\beta}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}}{{\rm tr}\left(e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\rho_{\beta}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\right)}\right)=\log\left(e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}e^{\beta H}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\right)-\log\left[Z_{\beta}{\rm tr}\left(e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\rho_{\beta}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\right)\right].

(S.596)

The first term characterizes the effective Hamiltonian
\hat{H}_{\tau}
in Subtheorem
1
.
Because the operator
u_{L}
is strictly localized on the subset
L
, we can apply Proposition
24
to

{\rm ad}_{\hat{H}_{\tau}}(u_{L})
.
We then obtain the upper bound as in (
S.335
):

\displaystyle\sup_{u_{L}}\left\|\left[\log\left(\rho_{\beta,L^{\rm c},\tau_{1}}\right),u_{L}\right]\right\|\leq g^{\prime}_{\tau_{1}},

(S.597)

where
g^{\prime}_{\tau_{1}}=2\tau_{1}+2^{2D+5}\gamma^{2}|L|^{2}C_{2,\tau_{1}}^{2D}\bar{J}_{0}\left(1+8\gamma|L|C_{3,\tau_{1}}^{3D}\right)
, and we adopt the definition (
S.336
) for
C_{\nu,\tau}
, i.e.,
C_{\nu,\tau_{1}}:=\frac{\tau_{1}+e}{2}+\frac{32}{\kappa_{\beta}^{2}}\left(\nu^{2}D^{2}+\kappa_{1}^{2}\tau_{1}^{2}+\frac{\kappa_{\beta}\kappa_{0}\tau_{1}}{8}\right)
.
Using the upper bound in Ref.
[
167
]
for the incomplete Gamma function, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{\tau-\tau_{1}}\tau_{1}^{m-1}d\tau_{1}\leq\frac{\left(\tau+\Gamma(m+1)^{1/(m-1)}\right)^{m}-\tau^{m}}{m\Gamma(m+1)^{1/(m-1)}}\leq\frac{\left(\tau+m+1\right)^{m}}{2m}

(S.598)

for
m\geq 2
, where
\Gamma(m)
is the Gamma function. For
m=1
, we can trivially obtain
\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{\tau-\tau_{1}}d\tau_{1}=1
.
Here,
g^{\prime}_{\tau_{1}}
is described by a
(10D)
th order polynomial with respect to
\tau
.
We therefore obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{\tau-\tau_{1}}g^{\prime}_{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{1}=\Theta(\beta^{10D},\tau^{10D+1},|L|^{3}),

(S.599)

where we use (
S.598
) with
m\leq 10D+1=\mathcal{O}(1)
.
This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
38
]

Figure 13:
Clustering theorem using the PTP formalism. We consider the approximate PTP operator
e^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{A}}
and
e^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{C}}
for the subsets
A
and
C
, respectively.
Using Theorem
2
, we can ensure that the effects of the PTP operators of
e^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{A}}
and
e^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{C}}
are approximately localized around
A
and
C
.
We thus consider the approximations onto
\tilde{A}=A[R/3]
and
\tilde{C}=C[R/3]
and utilize Lemma
16
to derive the upper bound for the conditional mutual information.

By applying Lemma
38
to (
S.592
), we have

\displaystyle 8e^{-\tau}\mathcal{D}_{AC}\left(\mathcal{\chi}_{\tau,\rho_{\beta},AB}+\mathcal{\chi}_{\tau,\rho_{\beta},BC}+\mathcal{\chi}_{\tau,\rho_{\beta},B}\right)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\tau}\mathcal{D}_{AC}\Theta(\beta^{10D},\tau^{10D+1},|AC|^{3})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{D}_{AC}e^{-\tau/2+\Theta(1)\log(\beta|AC|)},

(S.600)

where we use
\Theta(\tau^{10D+1})e^{-\tau}\leq\Theta(1)e^{-\tau/2}
.
Then, the remaining task is to estimate the norm of
H_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)
in Eq. (
S.594
).
We now prove the following lemma, which is derived from Theorem
2

Lemma 39
.

The norm of the operator
H_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)
in Eq. (
S.594
) is upper-bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|H_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 6e^{-\tau}+e^{\Theta(\tau\beta^{D})\log\left(\beta|AC|R\tau\right)-\kappa_{\beta}R/6}+\Theta(|AC|,R^{2D},\beta)e^{-4\kappa_{\beta}R/3}

\displaystyle\leq 6e^{-\tau}+e^{\Theta(\tau\beta^{D})\log\left(\beta|AC|R\tau\right)-\kappa_{\beta}R/6}.

(S.601)

Proof of Lemma
39
.

We first define
H^{\prime}_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)
by slightly modifying the definition (
S.594
) as

\displaystyle H^{\prime}_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B):=-\log(e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{C}}\rho_{\beta}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{C}})-\log(e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{A}}\rho_{\beta}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{A}})+\log(\rho_{\beta})+\log(e^{-\tau({\mathcal{Q}}_{A}+{\mathcal{Q}}_{C})}\rho_{\beta}e^{-\tau({\mathcal{Q}}_{A}+{\mathcal{Q}}_{C})})

\displaystyle=-\log(e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{C}}e^{\beta H}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{C}})-\log(e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{A}}e^{\beta H}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{A}})+\beta H+\log(e^{-\tau({\mathcal{Q}}_{A}+{\mathcal{Q}}_{C})}e^{\beta H}e^{-\tau({\mathcal{Q}}_{A}+{\mathcal{Q}}_{C})}),

(S.602)

where the definition removes the normalization factors in Eq. (
S.591
).
Note that
{\mathcal{Q}}_{(ABC)^{\rm c}}={\mathcal{Q}}_{\emptyset}=\hat{0}
.
Using the inequality (
S.191
) in Lemma
15
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\rho_{\beta}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}-{\mathcal{P}}_{L}\rho_{\beta}{\mathcal{P}}_{L}\right\|_{1}\leq 2e^{-\tau}\longrightarrow{\rm tr}\left(e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\rho_{\beta}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{L}}\right)\leq 1+2e^{-\tau}

(S.603)

for an arbitrary
L\subseteq\Lambda
.
Therefore, from the inequality of
\log(1+x)\leq x
for
x\geq 0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|H^{\prime}_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)-H_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)\right\|\leq 6e^{-\tau}.

(S.604)

In the following, we aim to estimate the upper bound for
\left\|H^{\prime}_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)\right\|
instead of
\left\|H_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)\right\|
relying on Theorem
2
.

For the convenience of readers, we first restate Theorem
2
with the use of the inequalities (
S.435
) and (
S.437
):

Theorem
2
(restatement).

Let
\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}
be an extended subset from
\mathfrak{L}
by a distance
r
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}:=\mathfrak{L}[r].

(S.605)

Then, we construct
\hat{H}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}
using the subset Hamiltonian
H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}
on
\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}\subset\Lambda
as follows:

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}=\log\left(e^{\tau V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}}e^{\beta H_{0,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}}e^{\tau V_{\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}}\right).

(S.606)

\displaystyle\left\|U_{\tau}-U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}\right\|\leq e^{\Theta(\tau\beta^{D})\left\|V\right\|\log(\beta\left\|V\right\|\cdot|\mathfrak{L}|r\tau)-\kappa_{\beta}r/2},

(S.607)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\log\left(e^{\tau V}e^{\beta H_{0}}e^{\tau V}\right)-U_{\tau,\tilde{L}}(\beta H_{0}+\hat{V}_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}})U_{\tau,\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\dagger}\right\|\leq e^{\Theta(\tau\beta^{D})\left\|V\right\|\log(\beta\left\|V\right\|\cdot|\mathfrak{L}|r\tau)-\kappa_{\beta}r/2}.

(S.608)

To utilize the theorem with
H_{0}=H
and
V=-{\mathcal{Q}}_{A}
(
\mathfrak{L}=A
),
V=-{\mathcal{Q}}_{C}
(
\mathfrak{L}=C
),
V=-{\mathcal{Q}}_{A}-{\mathcal{Q}}_{C}
(
\mathfrak{L}=A\sqcup C
), we first define
U_{\tau,\tilde{A}}
and
U_{\tau,\tilde{C}}
by the following effective Hamiltonian

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\tau,\tilde{A}}=\log\left(e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{A}}e^{\beta H_{\tilde{A}}}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{A}}\right)=U_{\tau,\tilde{A}}\left[\beta H_{\tilde{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\tilde{A}}\right]U_{\tau,\tilde{A}}^{\dagger},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\tilde{A}}=-2\int_{0}^{\tau}U_{\tau_{1},\tilde{A}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{Q}}_{A}U_{\tau_{1},\tilde{A}}

(S.609)

and

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\tau,\tilde{C}}=\log\left(e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{C}}e^{\beta H_{\tilde{C}}}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{C}}\right)=U_{\tau,\tilde{C}}\left[\beta H_{\tilde{C}}+\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\tilde{C}}\right]U_{\tau,\tilde{C}}^{\dagger},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\tilde{C}}=-2\int_{0}^{\tau}U_{\tau_{1},\tilde{C}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{Q}}_{C}U_{\tau_{1},\tilde{C}},

(S.610)

where we define
\tilde{A}=A[R/3]
and
\tilde{C}=C[R/3]
and use the expression in Corollary
19
.
Then, from the inequality (
S.608
) in Theorem
2
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\log\left(e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{A}}e^{\beta H}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{A}}\right)-U_{\tau,\tilde{A}}\left[\beta H+\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\tilde{A}}\right]U_{\tau,\tilde{A}}^{\dagger}\right\|\leq e^{\Theta(\tau\beta^{D})\log(\beta\cdot|A|R\tau)-\kappa_{\beta}R/6},

\displaystyle\left\|\log\left(e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{C}}e^{\beta H}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{C}}\right)-U_{\tau,\tilde{C}}\left[\beta H+\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\tilde{C}}\right]U_{\tau,\tilde{C}}^{\dagger}\right\|\leq e^{\Theta(\tau\beta^{D})\log(\beta\cdot|C|R\tau)-\kappa_{\beta}R/6},

\displaystyle\left\|\log\left(e^{-\tau({\mathcal{Q}}_{A}+{\mathcal{Q}}_{C})}e^{\beta H}e^{-\tau({\mathcal{Q}}_{A}+{\mathcal{Q}}_{C})}\right)-U_{\tau,\tilde{A}}U_{\tau,\tilde{C}}\left[\beta H+\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\tilde{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\tilde{C}}\right]U_{\tau,\tilde{C}}^{\dagger}U_{\tau,\tilde{A}}^{\dagger}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq e^{\Theta(\tau\beta^{D})\log\left(\beta|AC|R\tau\right)-\kappa_{\beta}R/6},

(S.611)

where, in the third inequality, we use
e^{\beta H_{0,\tilde{A}\sqcup\tilde{C}}}=e^{\beta H_{0,\tilde{A}}}\otimes e^{\beta H_{0,\tilde{C}}}
, which reduces Eq. (
S.606
) to

\displaystyle\log\left(e^{-\tau({\mathcal{Q}}_{A}+{\mathcal{Q}}_{C})}e^{\beta H_{0,\tilde{A}\sqcup\tilde{C}}}e^{-\tau({\mathcal{Q}}_{A}+{\mathcal{Q}}_{C})}\right)=\log\left(e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{A}}e^{\beta H_{0,\tilde{A}}}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{A}}\right)+\log\left(e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{C}}e^{\beta H_{0,\tilde{C}}}e^{-\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{C}}\right).

(S.612)

Therefore, by defining
H^{\prime\prime}_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)

\displaystyle H^{\prime\prime}_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B):=

\displaystyle-U_{\tau,\tilde{A}}\left[\beta H+\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\tilde{A}}\right]U_{\tau,\tilde{A}}^{\dagger}-U_{\tau,\tilde{C}}\left[\beta H+\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\tilde{C}}\right]U_{\tau,\tilde{C}}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle+\beta H+U_{\tau,\tilde{A}}U_{\tau,\tilde{C}}\left[\beta H+\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\tilde{A}}+\tau{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\tilde{C}}\right]U_{\tau,\tilde{C}}^{\dagger}U_{\tau,\tilde{A}}^{\dagger},

(S.613)

we have

\displaystyle\left\|H^{\prime}_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)-H^{\prime\prime}_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)\right\|\leq e^{\Theta(\tau\beta^{D})\log\left(\beta|AC|R\tau\right)-\kappa_{\beta}R/6}.

(S.614)

We finally estimate the norm of
\left\|H^{\prime\prime}_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)\right\|
. If there are no interactions between the regions
\tilde{A}
and
\tilde{C}
, i.e.,
H=H_{\tilde{A},\tilde{B}}+H_{\tilde{B},\tilde{C}}
[
\tilde{B}=\Lambda\setminus(\tilde{A}\sqcap\tilde{C})
], we can immediately obtain
\left\|H^{\prime\prime}_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)\right\|=0
. Therefore, by letting
h_{\tilde{A},\tilde{C}}
be the interaction term as
\sum_{Z:Z\cap\tilde{A}=\emptyset,Z\cap\tilde{C}=\emptyset}h_{Z}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|H^{\prime\prime}_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)\right\|\leq 4\beta\left\|h_{\tilde{A},\tilde{C}}\right\|\leq 4\beta\bar{J}_{0}|\tilde{A}|\cdot|\tilde{C}|e^{-\mu R/3}\leq\Theta(|AC|,R^{2D},\beta)e^{-4\kappa_{\beta}R/3},

(S.615)

where we use the inequality (
S.16
) in Lemma
1
and
\mu\geq 4\kappa_{\beta}
from Eq. (
S.121
).

By combining the inequalities (
S.604
), (
S.614
) and (
S.615
),

\displaystyle\left\|H_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|H^{\prime}_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)-H_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)\right\|+\left\|H^{\prime}_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)-H^{\prime\prime}_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)\right\|+\left\|H^{\prime\prime}_{\rho_{\beta},\tau}(A:C|B)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 6e^{-\tau}+e^{\Theta(\tau\beta^{D})\log\left(\beta|AC|R\tau\right)-\kappa_{\beta}R/6}+\Theta(|AC|,R^{2D},\beta)e^{-4\kappa_{\beta}R/3}.

(S.616)

We thus prove the main inequality. This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
39
]

By applying the inequalities (
S.600
) and (
S.601
) to (
S.592
), we finally obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:C|B)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{D}_{AC}e^{-\tau/2+\Theta(1)\log(\beta|AC|)}+e^{\Theta(\tau\beta^{D})\log\left(\beta|AC|R\tau\right)-\kappa_{\beta}R/6}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{D}_{AC}e^{-\tau/2+\Theta(1)\log(\tau R)}+e^{\Theta(\tau\beta^{D})\log\left(\tau R\right)-\kappa_{\beta}R/6},

(S.617)

which holds for an arbitrary positive
\tau>0
, where we use the fact that the inequality is meaningful only for
\tau\geq\Theta(|AC|)
and
R\geq\Theta(\beta)
.
Therefore, by choosing
\tau
such that

\displaystyle\Theta(\tau\beta^{D})\log\left(\tau R\right)-\frac{\kappa_{\beta}R}{6}=-\frac{\kappa_{\beta}R}{12}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\tau=\frac{R}{\Theta\left(\beta^{D+1}\right)\log\left(R\right)}\geq\frac{R}{\Theta\left(\beta^{D+1}\right)\log\left(R\right)},

(S.618)

which reduces the inequality (
S.617
) to

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:C|B)\leq\mathcal{D}_{AC}e^{-R/\Theta\left[\beta^{D+1}\log\left(R\right)\right]+\Theta(1)\log(R)}+e^{-\kappa_{\beta}R/12}.

(S.619)

Because the first term is more dominant than the second term, we prove the main inequality (
S.589
).
This completes the proof of Theorem
4
.
\square

S.VIII.2
One-dimensional case: improved bound

We now consider an improved bound for the one-dimensional cases.
The result in the previous section can be applied to one-dimensional systems, but it is meaningless when
A
or
C
is macroscopically large, i.e.,
|A|=|C|=\mathcal{O}(|\Lambda|)
.
In one-dimensional systems, we can resolve this drawback by using the BP formalism as in Sec.
S.III.2
.
We now aim to prove the following theorem:

Theorem 5
.

Let us define
A\sqcup B\sqcup C=\Lambda
and let
A_{0}
and
C_{0}
be subsystems such that
A_{0}\subseteq A
and
C_{0}\subseteq C
.
Then, for an arbitrary quantum Gibbs state on one dimension, the conditional mutual information
{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A_{0}:C_{0}|B)
is upper-bounded by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A_{0}:C_{0}|B)\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/10},

(S.620)

where
R=d_{A,C}
.
Note that
\kappa_{\beta}
has been defined as in Eq. (
S.121
).

Remark.
The inequality gives the exponential decay of the conditional mutual information as
e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(R)-R/\Theta(\beta)}
because of
\kappa_{\beta}=\mathcal{O}(1/\beta)
. Hence, the conditional mutual information has a correlation length of
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\beta^{2})
.
As has been mentioned, this scaling is the same as the length scale of the quantum belief propagation as was given in Corollary
11
.

Also, in 1D cases, the clustering of the CMI can be derived for arbitrary contiguous subsystems, i.e.,
A_{0}\sqcup B\sqcup C_{0}\subset\Lambda
.
However, when the conditional region
B
is disconnected (non-contiguous), there exists a counterexample where the decay of the CMI no longer holds.
For example, let us consider the 1D cluster Hamiltonian as
[
168
,
169
]

\displaystyle H=-\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\sigma_{i-1}^{z}\otimes\sigma_{i}^{x}\otimes\sigma_{i+1}^{z}

(S.621)

with
\{\sigma^{x},\sigma^{y},\sigma^{z}\}
the Pauli matrices, where the periodic boundary condition is adopted.
Here the quantum Gibbs state
e^{\beta H}
is close to the cluster state up to an error of
1/{\rm poly}(|\Lambda|)
for
\beta=\Omega[\log(|\Lambda|)]
.
On the other hand, if we decompose the total system into
\Lambda_{\rm odd}=\{1,3,5,\ldots\}
and
\Lambda_{\rm even}=\{2,4,6,\ldots\}
, the CMI
{\mathcal{I}}(A_{0}:C_{0}|B_{0})
(
A_{0}\sqcup B_{0}\sqcup C_{0}=\Lambda_{\rm odd}
) has an infinite correlation length for the CMI.
Thus, the subsystem states
\rho_{\beta,\Lambda_{\rm odd}}
have a long-range CMI.

In comparison with the previous bound in Ref.
[
56
]
, the present upper bound is better in the following sense.
First, the decay is given by the exponential function instead of the subexponential function
e^{-\Theta(\sqrt{R})}
.
Second, the correlation length is exponentially improved from
e^{\mathcal{O}(\beta)}
to
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\beta^{2})
.
The primary reason for this improvement is that we do not rely on the clustering theorem for the bipartite correlation function, which should have a correlation length of
e^{\mathcal{O}(\beta)}
.
Third, from our derivation, we can ensure the quasi-locality of the entanglement Hamiltonian for an approximate quantum Gibbs state [see Eq. (
S.628
) below] after the partial trace operation.
However, to derive the quasi-locality for the exact quantum Gibbs state,
we need a significant leap from the current analyses (see Sec.
S.X
).

Finally, we also mention the recent works
[
74
,
75
]
that prove the exponential decay of the conditional mutual information for the matrix product states (or the matrix product density matrices) under the two assumptions: i) the translation invariance, ii) existence of a constant gap in the transfer matrix
*4
*4

*4

In addition, there is a coefficient that is proportional to the minimum eigenvalue of the target density matrix
\rho
; specifically, the coefficient
Q
in Ref.
[
75
, Theorem 1]
is proportional to
\lambda_{\min}^{-3/2}
, where
\lambda_{\min}
is the minimum eigenvalue of
\rho
. Hence, it is only applicable to quantum states with low purity.
.
While proving these conditions is still challenging, it is of further interest to investigate the decay of conditional mutual information within the class of tensor network states, as opposed to quantum Gibbs states.In general, the gap of the transfer matrix is related to the decay rate of the bipartite correlation function
[
170
]
, and hence the gap is expected to be as small as
e^{-\Omega(\beta)}
. From this aspect, the CMI decay in the Gibbs state may have a qualitatively different property from the matrix product states.

S.VIII.2.1
Proof of Theorem
5

Using the monotonicity of the conditional mutual information (see Refs.
[
29
, Proposition 3 therein]
and
[
30
, Ineq. (1.1)]
), we prove

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A_{0}:C_{0}|B)\leq{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:C|B)\quad\textrm{for}\quad\forall A_{0}\subseteq A,\quad C_{0}\subseteq C.

(S.622)

Hence, we only have to estimate the upper bound of
{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:C|B)
.

For this purpose, we consider the following partition of the total system (see Fig.
14
):
