\displaystyle\Lambda=A\sqcup B\sqcup C=A\sqcup B_{1}\sqcup B_{2}\sqcup B_{3}\sqcup B_{4}\sqcup B_{5}\sqcup C,

(S.623)

where
|B_{1}|=|B_{2}|=|B_{3}|=|B_{4}|=|B_{5}|=R/5
.
In Eqs (
S.167
) and (
S.168
), we choose
L=AC
,
X=B_{1}B_{4}
, and
Y=B_{2}B_{3}
,
and the following decomposition holds from the belief propagation:

\displaystyle e^{\beta H}=\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}e^{\beta\left(H_{AB_{1}}+H_{B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+H_{B_{5}C}\right)}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{\dagger},

(S.624)

where the belief propagation operators
\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}
and
\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle e^{\beta\left(H_{AB_{1}B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+H_{B_{5}C}\right)}=\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}e^{\beta\left(H_{AB_{1}}+H_{B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+H_{B_{5}C}\right)}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{\dagger},

\displaystyle e^{\beta H}=\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}e^{\beta\left(H_{AB_{1}B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+H_{B_{5}C}\right)}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{\dagger}.

(S.625)

Using Lemma
10
and Corollary
11
, we approximate
\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}
and
\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}
by

\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}
and
\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}
, respectively, where they are given by

\displaystyle\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}=\mathcal{T}\exp\left(\int_{0}^{1}\tilde{\phi}_{B_{1}B_{2},\tau}d\tau\right),\quad\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}=\mathcal{T}\exp\left(\int_{0}^{1}\tilde{\phi}_{B_{4}B_{5},\tau}d\tau\right),

(S.626)

such that

\displaystyle\left\|\phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}},\tau}-\tilde{\phi}_{B_{1}B_{2},\tau}\right\|\leq\bar{\phi}_{\beta,1}e^{-\kappa_{\beta}R/5},\quad\left\|\phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C},\tau}-\tilde{\phi}_{B_{4}B_{5},\tau}\right\|\leq\bar{\phi}_{\beta,1}e^{-\kappa_{\beta}R/5},

(S.627)

where we use
|\partial(AB_{1})|=|\partial(B_{5}C)|=1
in the definition (
S.120
) for
\bar{\phi}_{\beta,|\partial L|}
.
Using the truncated belief propagation operators in (
S.626
), we approximate
e^{\beta H}
by
e^{\beta\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}}
as in

\displaystyle e^{\beta\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}}:=\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta\left(H_{AB_{1}}+H_{B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+H_{B_{5}C}\right)}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger},\quad\tilde{\rho}_{\beta}=\frac{e^{\beta\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}}}{\tilde{Z}_{\beta}},

(S.628)

where we define
\tilde{Z}_{\beta}:={\rm tr}\left(e^{\beta\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}}\right)
.
Note that the operator
\tilde{\phi}_{B_{1}B_{2},\tau}
(resp.
\tilde{\phi}_{B_{4}B_{5},\tau}
) is quasi-local around the joint between
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
(resp.
B_{3}
and
B_{4}
).

Figure 14:
Proof of the clustering theorem for the conditional mutual information in one dimension.
To apply the BP formalism (see Sec.
S.III.2
), we decompose the subsystem
B
into 5 pieces as
\{B_{1},B_{2},B_{3},B_{4},B_{5}\}
and approximate the effects of the partial traces for
A
and
C
onto
B_{1}B_{2}
and
B_{3}B_{4}
, respectively [see also (
S.638
) and (
S.639
)].
Also, to upper-bound the conditional mutual information, we rely on Proposition
20
.
As a drawback of the proposition, it includes the dependence on
\log(\mathcal{D}_{AC})=\mathcal{O}(|A|+|C|)
.
To obtain meaningful bound in the limit of
|A|,|C|\to\infty
, we utilize the decomposition of (
S.644
), which allows us to use Proposition
20
for a smaller system
\Lambda_{0}=A_{0}\sqcup B\sqcup C_{0}
, where
A_{0}
and
C_{0}
are subsets of
A
and
C
, respectively, and have the length of
R^{2}
.
By combining all the techniques together, we prove Theorem
5
.

We now utilize Lemma
14
for the approximate Gibbs state
\tilde{\rho}_{\beta}
, where the original definition for
\tilde{\rho}_{\beta}
in Eq. (
S.175
) is now replaced by the one in Eq. (
S.628
).
For the convenience of readers, we show it again so that the statement meets our current purpose:

Lemma
14
(restatement).

Let us define
H_{\rho}(A:C|B)
as

\displaystyle H_{\rho}(A:C|B)=-\log(\rho_{AB})-\log(\rho_{BC})+\log(\rho_{ABC})+\log(\rho_{B})

(S.629)

for an arbitrary quantum state
\rho
.
Then, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:C|B)\leq

\displaystyle\left\|H_{\tilde{\rho}_{\beta}}(A:C|B)\right\|+4\beta\left\|H-\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}\right\|+4\delta_{\beta,R/5,AB_{1}}+4\delta_{\beta,R/5,B_{5}C},

(S.630)

where we use the form of
\tilde{H}_{\lambda}
in Eq. (
S.628
) and define
\delta_{\beta,\ell,L}
by (
S.169
) as follows:

\displaystyle\delta_{\beta,\ell,L}:=13\bar{\phi}_{\beta,|\partial L[\ell]|}\left\|\partial h_{L[\ell]}\right\|e^{2\beta\left\|\partial h_{L[\ell]}\right\|-\kappa_{\beta}(\ell-1)}.

(S.631)

First of all, we can obtain for an arbitrary concatenated region
L\subset\Lambda

\displaystyle\delta_{\beta,R/5,L}\leq\Theta(\beta^{2})e^{\Theta(\beta)-\kappa_{\beta}R/5}

(S.632)

for an arbitrary connected region
L\subset\Lambda
,
where we use for
\bar{\phi}_{\beta,2}
from Eq. (
S.120
)

\displaystyle\bar{\phi}_{\beta,2}:=8\beta\gamma\tilde{J}_{0}e^{\mu/2}\left[1+\frac{4\beta\gamma Cv}{7}\left(\frac{4D}{e\mu}\right)^{D}+\frac{8}{\pi^{2}}\log\left(e+\frac{e}{\kappa_{\beta}}\right)\frac{e^{\kappa_{\beta}}}{e^{\kappa_{\beta}}-1}\right]\leq\Theta\left(\beta^{2}\right).

(S.633)

Therefore, we obtain the upper bound for
4\delta_{\beta,R/5,AB_{1}}+4\delta_{\beta,R/5,B_{5}C}
in the inequality (
S.630
).

In the following, we aim to estimate the norms of
\left\|H_{\tilde{\rho}_{\beta}}(A:C|B)\right\|
and the norm

\displaystyle\varepsilon(R):=\beta\left\|H-\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}\right\|=\left\|\beta H-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta\left(H_{AB_{1}}+H_{B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+H_{B_{5}C}\right)}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|,

(S.634)

separately.
We first prove the following lemma for
\left\|H_{\tilde{\rho}_{\beta}}(A:C|B)\right\|
:

Lemma 40
.

Under the definition of
\tilde{\rho}_{\beta}
as in Eq. (
S.628
), we upper-bound

\displaystyle\left\|H_{\tilde{\rho}_{\beta}}(A:C|B)\right\|\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/10}+4\beta\bar{J}_{0}|B_{2}|\cdot|B_{4}|e^{-\mu R/5}.

(S.635)

Note that the second term in the RHS of the above inequality can be absorbed to the first term since
\mu\geq 4\kappa_{\beta}
from Eq. (
S.121
) and
4\beta\bar{J}_{0}|B_{2}|\cdot|B_{4}|\leq e^{\Theta(1)\log(\beta R)}
.

Proof of Lemma
40
.

Throughout the proof, we let
\tilde{Z}_{\beta}=1
in Eq. (
S.628
) for simplicity.
For the proof, we need to consider the entanglement Hamiltonian
\log(\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,AB})
,
\log(\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,BC})
,
\log(\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,ABC})
and
\log(\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,B})
which is given by using Eq. (
S.171
):

\displaystyle\log(\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,AB})=\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta\left(H_{AB_{1}}+H_{B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{B_{5}}\right)}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right),

\displaystyle\log(\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,BC})=\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta\left(\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{B_{1}}+H_{B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+H_{B_{5}C}\right)}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right),

\displaystyle\log(\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,ABC})=\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta\left(H_{AB_{1}}+H_{B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+H_{B_{5}C}\right)}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right),

\displaystyle\log(\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,B})=\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta\left(\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{B_{1}}+H_{B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{B_{5}}\right)}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right).

(S.636)

By adopting the expression of Eq. (
S.555
),
we here define the unitary operators
U_{\tau,B_{1}B_{2}}
,
U_{\tau,B_{4}B_{5}}
,
U^{\prime}_{\tau,B_{1}B_{2}}
,
U^{\prime}_{\tau,B_{4}B_{5}}
and
\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\tau,B_{1}B_{2}}
,
\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\tau,B_{1}B_{2}}^{\prime}
,
\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\tau,B_{4}B_{5}}
,
\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\tau,B_{4}B_{5}}^{\prime}
as follows:

\displaystyle\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta\left(H_{B_{1}}+H_{B_{2}}\right)}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\right)=U_{B_{1}B_{2}}\left[\beta\left(H_{B_{1}}+H_{B_{2}}\right)+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}_{B_{1}B_{2}}\right]U_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger},

\displaystyle\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}e^{\beta\left(H_{B_{4}}+H_{B_{5}}\right)}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)=U_{B_{4}B_{5}}\left[\beta\left(H_{B_{4}}+H_{B_{5}}\right)+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\right]U_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger},

\displaystyle\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta\left(H^{\ast}_{B_{1}}+H_{B_{2}}\right)}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\right)=U^{\prime}_{B_{1}B_{2}}\left[\beta\left(H^{\ast}_{B_{1}}+H_{B_{2}}\right)+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}^{\prime}_{B_{1}B_{2}}\right]U_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{{}^{\prime}\dagger},

\displaystyle\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}e^{\beta\left(H_{B_{4}}+H^{\ast}_{B_{5}}\right)}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)=U^{\prime}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\left[\beta\left(H_{B_{4}}+H^{\ast}_{B_{5}}\right)+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}^{\prime}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\right]U_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{{}^{\prime}\dagger}.

(S.637)

where we omit the index
\tau=1
and simply denote
U_{\tau=1,B_{1}B_{2}}
by
U_{B_{1}B_{2}}
for example.
Then, from the inequality (
S.554
) in Theorem
3
, we approximate
\log(\rho_{AB})
in Eq. (
S.636
) by

\displaystyle\left\|\log(\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,AB})-U_{B_{1}B_{2}}U^{\prime}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\left[\beta\left(H_{AB_{1}}+H_{B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{B_{5}}\right)+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}_{B_{1}B_{2}}+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}^{\prime}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\right]U_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{{}^{\prime}\dagger}U_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/10},

(S.638)

where we choose
\mathfrak{L}=(\partial B_{1})\cup(\partial B_{4})
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}=B_{1}B_{2}B_{4}B_{5}
, and the length
r
in (
S.554
) is chosen as
R/5
.
The same upper bounds as (
S.638
) hold for the norm differences of

\displaystyle\left\|\log(\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,BC})-U^{\prime}_{B_{1}B_{2}}U_{B_{4}B_{5}}\left[\beta\left(\tilde{H}_{B_{1}}^{\ast}+H_{B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+\tilde{H}_{B_{5}C}\right)+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}^{\prime}_{B_{1}B_{2}}+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\right]U_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}U_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{{}^{\prime}\dagger}\right\|

\displaystyle\left\|\log(\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,ABC})-U_{B_{1}B_{2}}U_{B_{4}B_{5}}\left[\beta\left(H_{AB_{1}}+H_{B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+H_{B_{5}C}\right)+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}_{B_{1}B_{2}}+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\right]U_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}U_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\right\|

\displaystyle\left\|\log(\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,B})-U^{\prime}_{B_{1}B_{2}}U^{\prime}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\left[\beta\left(\tilde{H}_{B_{1}}^{\ast}+H_{B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{B_{5}}\right)+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}^{\prime}_{B_{1}B_{2}}+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}^{\prime}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\right]U_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{{}^{\prime}\dagger}U_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{{}^{\prime}\dagger}\right\|.

(S.639)

Relying on similar analyses to (
S.613
), (
S.614
) and (
S.615
), we can obtain

\displaystyle\left\|H_{\tilde{\rho}_{\beta}}(A:C|B)\right\|\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/10}+4\beta\bar{J}_{0}|B_{2}|\cdot|B_{4}|e^{-\mu R/5},

(S.640)

which proves the main inequality (
S.635
).
This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
40
]

We next estimate the norm (
S.634
) in
\varepsilon(R)
by utilizing Proposition
20
.
We prove the following statement:

Lemma 41
.

Under the definition of Eq. (
S.634
) for
\varepsilon(R)
, we obtain the upper bound of

\displaystyle\varepsilon(R)=\beta\left\|H-\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}\right\|\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/10}.

(S.641)

Proof of Lemma
41
.

Here, we adopt the notation of
H_{0}
as

\displaystyle H_{0}=H_{AB_{1}}+H_{B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+H_{B_{5}C}.

(S.642)

Using the notation, we obtain from Eq. (
S.624
)

\displaystyle e^{\beta H}=\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}e^{\beta H_{0}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}

(S.643)

Then, we start with applying the decomposition of (
S.253
) to Eq. (
S.634
):

\displaystyle\left\|\beta H-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta\left(H_{AB_{1}}+H_{B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+H_{B_{5}C}\right)}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\log\left(\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}e^{\beta H_{0}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta H_{0}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\log\left(\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\biggl\|\log\left(\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}e^{\beta H_{0}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta H_{0}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\left[\log\left(\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)\right]\biggr\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\log\left(\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\beta\widehat{H_{0,\Lambda_{0}^{\rm c}}}+\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta H_{0}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\beta\widehat{H_{0,\Lambda_{0}^{\rm c}}}+\log\left(\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}e^{\beta H_{0}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|,

(S.644)

where we define the subsets
A_{0}
,
C_{0}
(see also Fig.
14
) as follows:

\displaystyle A_{0}:=\{i\in A|d_{i,B}\leq R^{2}\},\quad C_{0}:=\{i\in C|d_{i,B}\leq R^{2}\},\quad\Lambda_{0}:=A_{0}\sqcup B\sqcup C_{0}\subset\Lambda.

(S.645)

Also, we use the notation (
S.9
) for
\widehat{H_{0,\Lambda_{0}^{\rm c}}}
, which gives

H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}+\widehat{H_{0,\Lambda_{0}^{\rm c}}}=H_{0}
.

For the first term in the RHS of (
S.644
), we apply Proposition
20
or the inequality (
S.252
).
For this purpose, we define
\Phi_{\partial h_{A_{0}B_{1}}}
and
\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C_{0}}}
in similar ways to Eq. (
S.625
):

\displaystyle e^{\beta\left(H_{A_{0}B_{1}B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+H_{B_{5}C_{0}}\right)}=\Phi_{\partial h_{A_{0}B_{1}}}e^{\beta\left(H_{A_{0}B_{1}}+H_{B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+H_{B_{5}C}\right)}\Phi_{\partial h_{A_{0}B_{1}}}^{\dagger},

\displaystyle e^{\beta H}=\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C_{0}}}e^{\beta\left(H_{A_{0}B_{1}B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+H_{B_{5}C_{0}}\right)}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C_{0}}}^{\dagger},

(S.646)

and

\displaystyle\Phi_{\partial h_{A_{0}B_{1}}}=\mathcal{T}\exp\left(\int_{0}^{1}\phi_{\partial h_{A_{0}B_{1}},\tau}d\tau\right),\quad\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C_{0}}}=\mathcal{T}\exp\left(\int_{0}^{1}\phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C_{0}},\tau}d\tau\right),

(S.647)

which also yields

\displaystyle e^{\beta H_{\Lambda_{0}}}=\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C_{0}}}\Phi_{\partial h_{A_{0}B_{1}}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\Phi_{\partial h_{A_{0}B_{1}}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C_{0}}}^{\dagger}.

(S.648)

We then obtain a similar inequality to (
S.255
) as

\displaystyle\left\|\log\left(\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\log\left(\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C_{0}}}\Phi_{\partial h_{A_{0}B_{1}}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\Phi_{\partial h_{A_{0}B_{1}}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C_{0}}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle+\left\|\log\left(\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C_{0}}}\Phi_{\partial h_{A_{0}B_{1}}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\Phi_{\partial h_{A_{0}B_{1}}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C_{0}}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|

(S.649)

We note that because of
|A_{0}|=|C_{0}|=R^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}},\tau}-\phi_{\partial h_{A_{0}B_{1}},\tau}\right\|\leq e^{-\Theta(R^{2}/\beta)},\quad\left\|\phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C},\tau}-\partial h_{B_{5}C_{0},\tau}\right\|\leq e^{-\Theta(R^{2}/\beta)},

(S.650)

by employing similar calculations to Lemma
10
.

We now choose as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}\to H_{0,\Lambda_{0}},\quad\Phi_{{\mathcal{B}}}\to\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C_{0}}}\Phi_{\partial h_{A_{0}B_{1}}},\quad\tilde{\Phi}_{{\mathcal{B}}}\to\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}\quad{\rm or}\quad\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}\to\partial h_{A_{0}B_{1}}+\partial h_{B_{5}C_{0}},\quad\delta\to 2\bar{\phi}_{\beta,1}e^{-\kappa_{\beta}R/5}+e^{-\Theta(R^{2}/\beta)},

(S.651)

where we use the upper bounds (
S.627
) and (
S.650
) for
the parameter
\delta
.
Using
\left\|\partial h_{A_{0}B_{1}}\right\|+\left\|\partial h_{B_{5}C_{0}}\right\|=\mathcal{O}(1)
, the inequality (
S.252
) now reduces to

\displaystyle\left\|\log\left(\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log\left(\beta\left\|H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}\right\|\right)}\bar{\phi}_{\beta,1}e^{-\kappa_{\beta}R/5}=e^{\Theta(\beta)\log\left(\beta R\right)}e^{-\kappa_{\beta}R/5},

(S.652)

where we use
\bar{\phi}_{\beta,1}\leq\Theta(\beta^{2})
in Eq. (
S.633
) and

\displaystyle\left\|H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}\right\|\leq\sum_{i\in\Lambda_{0}}\sum_{Z:Z\ni i}\left\|h_{Z}\right\|\leq\bar{J}_{0}|\Lambda_{0}|=\Theta(R^{2}).

(S.653)

For the second and the third terms in the RHS of (
S.644
), we apply
Theorem
3
or the inequality (
S.554
).
We consider the second term, and the third term can be treated in the same way.
Using the definition of
U_{B_{1},B_{2}}
and
U_{B_{4},B_{5}}
as in Eq. (
S.637
),
we can derive the second inequality in (
S.639
):

\displaystyle\left\|\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta H_{0}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)-U_{B_{1}B_{2}}U_{B_{4}B_{5}}\left[\beta H_{0}+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}_{B_{1}B_{2}}+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\right]U_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}U_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/10},

\displaystyle\left\|\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)-U_{B_{1}B_{2}}U_{B_{4}B_{5}}\left[\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}_{B_{1}B_{2}}+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\right]U_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}U_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/10}.

(S.654)

By using the above inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\beta\widehat{H_{0,\Lambda_{0}^{\rm c}}}+\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta H_{0}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/10}+\left\|\beta\widehat{H_{0,\Lambda_{0}^{\rm c}}}+\beta U_{B_{1}B_{2}}U_{B_{4}B_{5}}\left(H_{0}-H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}\right)U_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}U_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/10}+\beta\left\|\left[\widehat{H_{0,\Lambda_{0}^{\rm c}}},U_{B_{1}B_{2}}U_{B_{4}B_{5}}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/10}+2\beta\tilde{J}_{0}e^{-\mu R^{2}/2},

(S.655)

where we use
H_{0}-H_{0,\Lambda_{0}}=\widehat{H_{0,\Lambda_{0}^{\rm c}}}
from Eq. (
S.8
), and the norm of the commutator

\left\|\left[\widehat{H_{0,\Lambda_{0}^{\rm c}}},U_{B_{1}B_{2}}U_{B_{4}B_{5}}\right]\right\|
is upper-bounded by using the inequality (
S.17
) in Lemma
1
:

\displaystyle\left\|\left[\widehat{H_{0,\Lambda_{0}^{\rm c}}},U_{B_{1}B_{2}}U_{B_{4}B_{5}}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{Z:Z\cap\Lambda_{0}^{\rm c}\neq\emptyset,\ Z\cap(B_{1}B_{2}B_{4}B_{5})\neq\emptyset}\left\|h_{Z}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{Z:Z\cap B[R^{2}]\neq\emptyset,\ Z\cap B\neq\emptyset}\left\|h_{Z}\right\|\leq|\partial B|\tilde{J}_{0}e^{-\mu R^{2}/2}=2\tilde{J}_{0}e^{-\mu R^{2}/2}.

(S.656)

Note that
d_{\Lambda_{0}^{\rm c},B}=R^{2}
from the definition (
S.645
).
We can obtain the same inequality for the third term in the RHS of (
S.644
).

By applying the inequalities (
S.652
) and (
S.655
) to (
S.644
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\log\left(\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}e^{\beta H_{0}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{\dagger}\right)-\log\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta H_{0}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log\left(\beta R\right)}e^{-\kappa_{\beta}R/5}+4e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/10}+4\beta\tilde{J}_{0}e^{-\mu R^{2}/2}=e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/10},

(S.657)

which yields the main inequality (
S.641
).
This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
41
]

Finally by applying the inequalities in Lemmas
40
and
41
to (
S.630
), we finally upper-bound the conditional mutual information as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:C|B)\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/10}+4\beta\bar{J}_{0}|B_{2}|\cdot|B_{4}|e^{-\mu R/5}+e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/10}+\Theta(\beta^{2})e^{\Theta(\beta)-\kappa_{\beta}R/5},

which reduces to the desired inequality (
S.620
), where we use
R\geq\Theta(\beta)
[or
\log(\beta R)\leq\Theta(1)\log(R)
] since the inequality is meaningless for
R\leq\Theta(\beta)
.
This completes the proof of Theorem
5
.
\square

S.IX
Relation to other information measures

S.IX.1
Clustering of Entanglement of Formation (EoF) at arbitrary temperatures

As one application of the clustering theorem for the CMI, we show the clustering of quantum entanglement.
In the previous results
[
26
]
, the exponential clustering of the positive-partial-transpose (PPT) relative entanglement
[
83
,
84
,
85
,
86
]
has been proved.
However, the PPT class cannot capture the existence of the bound entanglement
[
81
,
82
]
.
To analyze the genuine bipartite entanglement, we consider the Entanglement of Formation (EoF) since it gives the upper bound for other entanglement measures
[
90
]
such as the relative entanglement
[
91
]
, the entanglement cost
[
92
]
, the squashed entanglement
[
29
]
, etc.
The EoF for an arbitrary bipartite quantum state
\rho_{AB}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle E_{F}(\rho_{AB})

\displaystyle:=\inf_{\{p_{s},\ket{\psi_{s,AB}}\}}\sum_{s}p_{s}S_{\ket{\psi_{s,AB}}}(A:B),

(S.658)

where
S_{\ket{\psi_{s,AB}}}(A)
is the von Neumann entropy for the reduced density matrix on the subset
A
, respectively.
The convex roof
\inf_{\{p_{s},\ket{\psi_{s,AB}}\}}
is taken for an arbitrary decomposition
\rho=\sum_{s}p_{s}\ket{\psi_{s,AB}}\bra{\psi_{s,AB}}
with
p_{s}>0
.

In high-dimensional cases, we can prove the following corollary as the direct consequence of Theorem
4
:

Corollary 42

(High dimensional cases)
.

Let
A
and
B
be subsystems that are separated by a distance
R
.
Then, for the reduced density matrix
\rho_{\beta,AB}
, the EoF obeys the following clustering theorem:

\displaystyle E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,AB})\leq\mathcal{D}_{AB}e^{-R/\Theta\left[\beta^{D+1}\log\left(R\right)\right]+\Theta(1)\log(R)}.

(S.659)

Remark.

The upper bound is meaningful only when the subsystem sizes of
A
and
B
do not depend on the system size
|\Lambda|
.
The same constraints were also imposed in the previous result
[
26
]
.
On the other hand, the temperature dependence of the entanglement length becomes worse in comparison with the previous one,
where correlation length for the PPT relative entanglement was given by
\mathcal{O}(\beta)
.
From this point, we still have room to further improve the inequality (
S.659
).

Proof of Corollary
42
.

We first introduce the continuity inequality for the EoF
[
171
, Corollary 4 therein]
.
Given two bipartite states
\rho_{AB}
and
\sigma_{AB}
such that
\delta=\left\|\rho_{AB}-\sigma_{AB}\right\|_{1}/2
, the EoF for
\rho_{AB}
is upper-bounded by

\displaystyle E_{F}(\rho_{AB})\leq E_{F}(\sigma_{AB})+\bar{\delta}\log\left[\max(\mathcal{D}_{A},\mathcal{D}_{B})\right]+(1+\bar{\delta})\left(\frac{\bar{\delta}}{1+\bar{\delta}}\right),

(S.660)

where
\bar{\delta}:=\sqrt{(\delta/2)(2-\delta/2)}\leq\delta^{1/2}
and
h(x)=-x\log(x)-(1-x)\log(1-x)
(
0<x<1
).
By using
\delta\leq 1
and
h(x)\leq x\log(3/x)
, we simplify the inequality (
S.660
) to

\displaystyle E_{F}(\rho_{AB})\leq E_{F}(\sigma_{AB})+\delta^{1/2}\left\{\log\left[\max(\mathcal{D}_{A},\mathcal{D}_{B})\right]+2\log\left(3\delta^{-1/2}\right)\right\}.

(S.661)

Using the minimum distance
\delta_{\rho_{\beta,AB}}
in Eq. (
S.33
) between the reduced density matrix
\rho_{\beta,AB}
and separable (i.e., non-entangled) states,
we prove the upper bound for the EoF
E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,AB})
as follows:

\displaystyle E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,AB})\leq\delta_{\rho_{\beta,AB}}^{1/2}\left\{\log\left[\max(\mathcal{D}_{A},\mathcal{D}_{B})\right]+2\log\left(3\delta_{\rho_{\beta,AB}}^{-1/2}\right)\right\}.

(S.662)

Note that the separable state
\sigma_{AB}
satisfy
E_{F}(\sigma_{AB})=0
.
For the quantity
\delta_{\rho_{\beta,AB}}
, by applying Theorem
4
to the inequality (
S.34
), we obtain

\displaystyle\delta_{\rho_{\beta,AB}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\min(\mathcal{D}_{A},\mathcal{D}_{B})\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{AB}}e^{-R/\Theta\left[\beta^{D+1}\log\left(R\right)\right]+\Theta(1)\log(R)},

(S.663)

which reduces the inequality (
S.662
) to the main inequality (
S.659
).
This completes the proof of Corollary
42
.
\square

In the one-dimensional case, as in the previous work
[
26
]
, we can remove the dependence on the Hilbert space dimension
\mathcal{D}_{AB}
.
We prove the following proposition:

Proposition 43

(One dimensional cases)
.

Let us adopt the same setup as in Corollary
42
for one-dimensional systems.
We then obtain

\displaystyle E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,AB})\leq e^{\Theta(\beta\log(\beta))-\kappa_{\beta}^{2}R/[81\log(d_{0})]},

(S.664)

where
d_{0}
is the dimension of the local Hilbert space.

Remark.

In the previous bound
[
26
, Theorem 12]
for the PPT relative entanglement, there exists an additional coefficient of
\mathcal{O}(|\Lambda|)
, which spoils the bound in the thermodynamic limit.
A prominent advantage of the current clustering theorem (
S.664
) is that we can remove the
|\Lambda|
dependence.
To achieve this, we cannot rely solely on the continuity inequality (
S.660
), which yields the additional coefficient of
\log\left[\max(\mathcal{D}_{A},\mathcal{D}_{B})\right]=\mathcal{O}(|\Lambda|)
.
An essential technique here is the iterative use of the approximate recovery map to reconstruct the total quantum Gibbs state (see Lemma
44
below).
The approximation error is controlled by the CMI decay in Theorem
5
with the combination of the Fawzi-Renner theorem
[
47
]
.

S.IX.1.1
Proof of Proposition
43

For the proof, we rely on a similar proof technique to
[
26
, Proof of Theorem 12]
.
We first decompose the subsystems
A
and
B
into three pieces, respectively (see Fig.
15
).
We let the decomposition of
A
be

\displaystyle A=A_{0}\sqcup A_{1}\sqcup A_{2},\quad|A_{1}|=|A_{2}|=\ell,

(S.665)

where we adopt the same decomposition for
B
.

We then use the belief propagation operator to obtain a similar decomposition to Eq. (
S.624
) as

\displaystyle e^{\beta H}=\Phi_{\partial h_{A_{0}A_{1}}}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{1}B_{0}}}e^{\beta(H_{A_{0}A_{1}}+H_{A_{2}CB_{2}}+H_{B_{1}B_{0}})}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{1}B_{0}}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{\partial h_{A_{0}A_{1}}}^{\dagger}.

(S.666)

By approximating the belief propagation operators
\Phi_{\partial h_{A_{0}A_{1}}}
and
\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{1}B_{0}}}
onto
A_{1}A_{2}
and
B_{1}B_{2}
, respectively, we have a similar approximation to Eq. (
S.628
) as follows:

\displaystyle e^{\beta\tilde{H}^{\prime}}:=\tilde{\Phi}_{A_{1}A_{2}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta(H_{A_{0}A_{1}}+H_{A_{2}CB_{2}}+H_{B_{1}B_{0}})}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}\tilde{\Phi}_{A_{1}A_{2}}^{\dagger},

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}^{\prime}_{\beta}:=\frac{e^{\beta\tilde{H}^{\prime}}}{\tilde{Z}^{\prime}_{\beta}},\quad\tilde{Z}^{\prime}_{\beta}:={\rm tr}\left(e^{\beta\tilde{H}^{\prime}}\right).

(S.667)

Using Corollary
11
, the approximation error between
e^{\beta H}
and
e^{\beta\tilde{H}^{\prime}}
is upper-bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Z_{\beta}}\left\|e^{\beta H}-e^{\beta\tilde{H}^{\prime}}\right\|_{1}\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)-\kappa_{\beta}\ell}\longrightarrow\left\|\rho_{\beta,AB}-\tilde{\rho}^{\prime}_{\beta,AB}\right\|_{1}\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)-\kappa_{\beta}\ell},

(S.668)

where we use
\left\|\rho_{\beta,AB}-\tilde{\rho}^{\prime}_{\beta,AB}\right\|_{1}\leq\frac{1}{Z_{\beta}}\left\|e^{\beta H}-e^{\beta\tilde{H}^{\prime}}\right\|_{1}+\left|\frac{\tilde{Z}^{\prime}_{\beta}}{Z_{\beta}}-1\right|
and

\displaystyle\tilde{Z}^{\prime}_{\beta}={\rm tr}\left(e^{\beta\tilde{H}^{\prime}}\right)=Z_{\beta}+{\rm tr}\left(e^{\beta\tilde{H}^{\prime}}-e^{\beta H}\right)\leq Z_{\beta}\left(1+\frac{1}{Z_{\beta}}\left\|e^{\beta\tilde{H}^{\prime}}-e^{\beta H}\right\|\right)\leq Z_{\beta}\left(1+e^{\Theta(\beta)-\kappa_{\beta}\ell}\right).

(S.669)

Figure 15:
Setup of the proof of Proposition
51
.
We decompose the subsets
A
and
B
into three pieces as in Eq. (
S.665
), i.e.,
A=A_{0}\sqcup A_{1}\sqcup A_{2}
and
B=B_{0}\sqcup B_{1}\sqcup B_{2}
respectively. Here, the sizes of the subsets
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
(or
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
) are set to be equal to
\ell
, which will be chosen afterward such that it minimizes the RHS of (
S.677
).
Then, the closeness
\delta_{\rho_{\beta,AB}}
between the separable state and
\rho_{AB}
is connected to that of
e^{\beta H_{A_{2}CB_{2}}}
as in Eq. (
S.671
).
In the inequality (
S.677
), we can prove
\delta_{\rho_{\beta,AB}}\lesssim e^{-R/\Theta(\beta)}
.
Unfortunately, only the bound on
\delta_{\rho_{\beta,AB}}
is not enough to derive the main inequality because of the
\log\left[\max(\mathcal{D}_{A},\mathcal{D}_{B})\right]
dependence in the continuity inequality (
S.661
).
This dependence cannot be ignored in the thermodynamic limit of
|\Lambda|\to\infty
.
To address this point, we extend the left and the right end regions little by little as in Eq. (
S.680
), where the subsets
\{\Lambda_{s}\}_{s}
extend with
s
so that
\Lambda_{s}\to\Lambda
in the limit of
s\to\infty
.
Using the recovery map (see Lemma
44
), we take into account the influences coming from the edges, which finally leads to the inequality (
S.696
) without
|\Lambda|
dependence.

We then consider the quantum state
\sigma_{\beta}
defined by

\displaystyle\sigma_{\beta}=\frac{e^{\beta(H_{A_{0}A_{1}}+H_{A_{2}CB_{2}}+H_{B_{1}B_{0}})}}{\tilde{Z}^{\prime\prime}_{\beta}},\quad\tilde{Z}^{\prime\prime}_{\beta}={\rm tr}\left(e^{\beta(H_{A_{0}A_{1}}+H_{A_{2}CB_{2}}+H_{B_{1}B_{0}})}\right).

(S.670)

Note that from Eq. (
S.667
) we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}^{\prime}_{\beta}=\frac{\tilde{Z}^{\prime\prime}_{\beta}}{\tilde{Z}^{\prime}_{\beta}}\tilde{\Phi}_{A_{1}A_{2}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}\sigma_{\beta}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}\tilde{\Phi}_{A_{1}A_{2}}^{\dagger}.

(S.671)

Then, by writing the reduced density matrix of
\sigma_{\beta,AB}=\sigma_{\beta,A_{0}A_{1}}\otimes\sigma_{\beta,A_{2}B_{2}}\otimes\sigma_{\beta,B_{1}B_{0}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sigma_{\beta,A_{2}B_{2}}=\sigma^{({\rm SEP})}_{\beta,A_{2}B_{2}}+\Delta\sigma_{\beta,A_{2}B_{2}},\quad\sigma_{\beta,AB}=\sigma^{({\rm SEP})}_{\beta,AB}+\Delta\sigma_{\beta,AB},

\displaystyle{\rm with}\quad\sigma^{({\rm SEP})}_{\beta,AB}=\sigma_{\beta,A_{0}A_{1}}\otimes\sigma^{({\rm SEP})}_{\beta,A_{2}B_{2}}\otimes\sigma_{\beta,B_{1}B_{0}}\and\Delta\sigma_{\beta,AB}=\sigma_{\beta,A_{0}A_{1}}\otimes\Delta\sigma_{\beta,A_{2}B_{2}}\otimes\sigma_{\beta,B_{1}B_{0}}.

(S.672)

Note that
\sigma_{\beta,A_{0}A_{1}}\otimes\sigma^{({\rm SEP})}_{\beta,A_{2}B_{2}}\otimes\sigma_{\beta,B_{1}B_{0}}
is also the separable state. For the norm of
\Delta\sigma_{\beta,AB}
, we can utilize the inequality (
S.34
) and Theorem
5
to derive

\displaystyle\delta_{\sigma_{\beta,AB}}=\left\|\Delta\sigma_{\beta,AB}\right\|_{1}=\left\|\Delta\sigma_{\beta,A_{2}B_{2}}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 4\min(\mathcal{D}_{A_{2}},\mathcal{D}_{B_{2}})e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/20}

\displaystyle\leq 4e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/20+\ell\log(d_{0})},

(S.673)

where we use
d_{A_{2},B_{2}}=R
and
\mathcal{D}_{A_{2}}=\mathcal{D}_{B_{2}}\leq d_{0}^{\ell}
.

In a similar way to Eq. (
S.672
), we decompose
\tilde{\rho}^{\prime}_{\beta,AB}
as

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}^{\prime}_{\beta,AB}=\tilde{\rho}^{{}^{\prime}({\rm SEP})}_{\beta,AB}+\Delta\tilde{\rho}^{\prime}_{\beta,AB}.

(S.674)

By applying the inequality (
S.673
) to Eq. (
S.671
), we obtain

\displaystyle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}_{\beta,AB}}=\left\|\Delta\tilde{\rho}^{\prime}_{\beta,AB}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tilde{Z}^{\prime\prime}_{\beta}}{\tilde{Z}^{\prime}_{\beta}}\left\|\tilde{\Phi}_{A_{1}A_{2}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}\Delta\sigma_{\beta,AB}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}\tilde{\Phi}_{A_{1}A_{2}}^{\dagger}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\tilde{\Phi}_{A_{1}A_{2}}^{-1}\right\|^{2}\left\|\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{-1}\right\|^{2}\left\|\tilde{\Phi}_{A_{1}A_{2}}\right\|^{2}\left\|\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}\right\|^{2}\cdot 4e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/20+\ell\log(d_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/20+\ell\log(d_{0})}

(S.675)

where we use the inequality (
S.113
), which yields the inequality as
\left\|\tilde{\Phi}_{A_{1}A_{2}}\right\|\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)}
, and

\displaystyle\tilde{Z}^{\prime\prime}_{\beta}={\rm tr}\left(e^{\beta(H_{A_{0}A_{1}}+H_{A_{2}CB_{2}}+H_{B_{1}B_{0}})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\tilde{\Phi}_{A_{1}A_{2}}^{-1}\right\|^{2}\left\|\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{-1}\right\|^{2}\tilde{Z}^{\prime}_{\beta}.

(S.676)

By applying the bound (
S.675
) to (
S.668
), we reach the upper bound of

\displaystyle\delta_{\rho_{\beta,AB}}=\left\|\Delta\rho_{\beta,AB}\right\|_{1}\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/20+\ell\log(d_{0})}+e^{\Theta(\beta)-\kappa_{\beta}\ell}

(S.677)

for the decomposition of

\rho_{\beta,AB}=\rho^{({\rm SEP})}_{\beta,AB}+\Delta\rho_{\beta,AB}.

By choosing
\ell
as
\ell=\kappa_{\beta}R/[40\log(d_{0})]
, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\rho_{\beta,AB}}\leq e^{\Theta(\beta\log(\beta))-\kappa_{\beta}^{2}R/[40\log(d_{0})]}.

(S.678)

From the upper bound (
S.662
), we can immediately derive an upper bound as

\displaystyle E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,AB})\lesssim\log\left[\max(\mathcal{D}_{A},\mathcal{D}_{B})\right]\delta_{\rho_{\beta,AB}}^{1/2},

(S.679)

but it is infinitely large in the limit of
|A|,|B|\to\infty
.
To obtain a better bound, we define the contiguous subsystems in
A_{0}
and
B_{0}
as
\{A_{0,\ell_{s}}\}_{s}
and
\{B_{0,\ell_{s}}\}_{s}
, respectively, where

|A_{0,\ell_{s}}|=|B_{0,\ell_{s}}|=\ell_{s}
.
We also denote

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}_{s}=A_{0,\ell_{s}}\sqcup A_{1}\sqcup A_{2},\quad\mathfrak{B}_{s}=B_{2}\sqcup B_{1}\sqcup B_{0,\ell_{s}},

\displaystyle\Delta\mathfrak{A}_{s}=\mathfrak{A}_{s}\setminus\mathfrak{A}_{s-1},\quad\Delta\mathfrak{B}_{s}=\mathfrak{B}_{s}\setminus\mathfrak{B}_{s-1},

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\prime}_{s}=\mathfrak{A}_{s}\sqcup C\sqcup\mathfrak{B}_{s-1},\quad\Lambda_{s}=\mathfrak{A}_{s}\sqcup C\sqcup\mathfrak{B}_{s}.

(S.680)

We choose the length
\ell_{s}
as

\displaystyle\ell_{s}=R^{s+1}\longrightarrow\max\left[\log(\mathcal{D}_{\mathfrak{A}_{s}}),\log(\mathcal{D}_{\mathfrak{B}_{s}})\right]=(R^{s+1}+2\ell)\log(d_{0}).

(S.681)

To derive the upper bound for
E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,AB})
, we need to estimate
E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{\infty}\mathfrak{B}_{\infty}})
.

We start from considering the EoF of
\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{1}\mathfrak{B}_{1}}
, which also satisfies

\displaystyle\delta_{\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{1}\mathfrak{B}_{1}}}=\left\|\Delta\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{1}\mathfrak{B}_{1}}\right\|\leq\left\|\Delta\rho_{\beta,AB}\right\|_{1}\leq e^{\Theta(\beta\log(\beta))-\kappa_{\beta}^{2}R/[40\log(d_{0})]}

(S.682)

for the decomposition of

\displaystyle\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{1}\mathfrak{B}_{1}}={\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{A}_{1}^{\rm c}}{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{B}_{1}^{\rm c}}\left(\rho_{\beta,AB}\right)=\rho^{({\rm SEP})}_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{1}\mathfrak{B}_{1}}+\Delta\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{1}\mathfrak{B}_{1}}.

(S.683)

Note that the partial trace does not increase the norm of operators.
By applying the continuity inequality (
S.662
) with
\delta_{\rho_{\beta,AB}}\to\left\|\Delta\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{1}\mathfrak{B}_{1}}\right\|
and (
S.681
) to (
S.682
), we have

\displaystyle E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{1}\mathfrak{B}_{1}})

\displaystyle\leq e^{\Theta(\beta\log(\beta))+\Theta(1)\log(R\log(d_{0}))-\kappa_{\beta}^{2}R/[80\log(d_{0})]}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\Theta(\beta\log(\beta))-\kappa_{\beta}^{2}R/[81\log(d_{0})]},

(S.684)

where we use
\Theta(1)\log[R\log(d_{0})]-c\kappa_{\beta}^{2}R\leq\Theta(\log(\beta))
for an arbitrary
c>0
.

In the next step, we consider
E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{2}\mathfrak{B}_{2}})
.
For this purpose, we prove the following lemma:

Lemma 44
.

Let us consider a recovery map that transforms
\rho_{\beta,\Lambda_{s}}
to
\rho_{\beta,\Lambda_{s+1}}
.
Then, there exists local completely-positive-trace-preserving (CPTP) maps
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{A}_{s}\to\mathfrak{A}_{s+1}}
and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{s}\to\mathfrak{B}_{s+1}}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\rho_{\beta,\Lambda_{s+1}}-\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{s}\to\mathfrak{B}_{s+1}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{A}_{s}\to\mathfrak{A}_{s+1}}(\rho_{\beta,\Lambda_{s}})\right\|_{1}\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\ell_{s})-\kappa_{\beta}\ell_{s}/10},

(S.685)

where
\ell_{s}
is defined in Eqs. (
S.680
) and (
S.681
), which gives
|\mathfrak{A}_{s}|\geq\ell_{s}
and
|\mathfrak{B}_{s}|\geq\ell_{s}
.

Figure 16:
Recovery from
\rho_{\beta,\Lambda_{s}}
to
\rho_{\beta,\Lambda_{s+1}}
in Lemma
44
.
We take the two steps: i) recovery from
\rho_{\beta,\Lambda_{s}}
to
\rho_{\beta,\Lambda^{\prime}_{s+1}}
, ii) recovery from
\rho_{\beta,\Lambda^{\prime}_{s+1}}
to
\rho_{\beta,\Lambda_{s+1}}
, where
\Lambda^{\prime}_{s+1}=\mathfrak{A}_{s+1}\sqcup\Lambda_{s}
.
We approximate the recovery maps so that they only act on
\mathfrak{A}_{s}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{s}
, respectively.
Under these approximated local maps, i.e.,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{A}_{s}\to\mathfrak{A}_{s+1}}
and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{s}\to\mathfrak{B}_{s+1}}
, the entanglement cannot increase due to the monotonicity.

Proof of Lemma
44
.

We construct the recovery map in two steps: i)
\rho_{\beta,\Lambda_{s}}
to
\rho_{\beta,\Lambda^{\prime}_{s+1}}
and
(ii)
\rho_{\beta,\Lambda^{\prime}_{s+1}}
to
\rho_{\beta,\Lambda_{s+1}}
(see Fig.
16
).
We first consider the recovery map (i).
From Theorem
5
, we can ensure

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(\Delta\mathfrak{A}_{s+1},C\mathfrak{B}_{s}|\mathfrak{A}_{s})\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(|\mathfrak{A}_{s}|)-\kappa_{\beta}|\mathfrak{A}_{s}|/10}.

(S.686)

By using the Fawzi-Renner theorem
[
47
, Ineq. (6) therein]
(see also
[
48
,
172
,
50
]
),
we can construct a recovery map
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{A}_{s}\to\mathfrak{A}_{s+1}}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{A}_{s}\to\mathfrak{A}_{s+1}}(\rho_{\beta,\Lambda_{s}})-\rho_{\beta,\Lambda^{\prime}_{s+1}}\right\|^{2}_{1}\leq\log(2){\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(\Delta\mathfrak{A}_{s+1},C\mathfrak{B}_{s}|\mathfrak{A}_{s})\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\ell_{s})-\kappa_{\beta}\ell_{s}/10},

(S.687)

where we use
\ell_{s}\leq|\mathfrak{A}_{s}|\leq\Theta(1)\ell_{s}
from
\mathfrak{A}_{s}\supset A_{0,\ell_{s}}
as in Eq. (
S.680
).
In the same way, we consider the recovery map (ii). Because of

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(\Delta\mathfrak{B}_{s+1},\mathfrak{A}_{s+1}C|\mathfrak{B}_{s})\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\ell_{s})-\kappa_{\beta}\ell_{s}/10},

(S.688)

we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{s}\to\mathfrak{B}_{s+1}}(\rho_{\beta,\Lambda^{\prime}_{s+1}})-\rho_{\beta,\Lambda_{s+1}}\right\|^{2}_{1}\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\ell_{s})-\kappa_{\beta}\ell_{s}/10}.

(S.689)

By combining the inequality (
S.687
) and (
S.689
), we prove the main inequality (
S.685
), where we use the fact that the CPTP map
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{A}_{s}\to\mathfrak{A}_{s+1}}
and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{s}\to\mathfrak{B}_{s+1}}
does not increase the norm. This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
44
]

Using the Lemma
44
with
s=1
, we can derive

\displaystyle\left\|\rho_{\beta,\Lambda_{2}}-\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{1}\to\mathfrak{B}_{2}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{A}_{1}\to\mathfrak{A}_{2}}(\rho_{\beta,\Lambda_{1}})\right\|_{1}\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\ell_{1})-\kappa_{\beta}\ell_{1}/10},

(S.690)

which yields by taking the partial trace with respect to the subset
C

\displaystyle\left\|\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{2}\mathfrak{B}_{2}}-\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{1}\to\mathfrak{B}_{2}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{A}_{1}\to\mathfrak{A}_{2}}(\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{1}\mathfrak{B}_{1}})\right\|_{1}\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\ell_{1})-\kappa_{\beta}\ell_{1}/10}.

(S.691)

By using the continuity inequality (
S.661
) and the monotonicity of the entanglement, i.e.,

\displaystyle E_{F}\left(\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{1}\to\mathfrak{B}_{2}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{A}_{1}\to\mathfrak{A}_{2}}(\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{1}\mathfrak{B}_{1}})\right)\leq E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{1}\mathfrak{B}_{1}}),

(S.692)

we obtain

\displaystyle E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{2}\mathfrak{B}_{2}})

\displaystyle\leq E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{1}\mathfrak{B}_{1}})+e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\ell_{1})-\kappa_{\beta}\ell_{1}/20}\log\left(\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{1}\mathfrak{B}_{1}})+\Theta(\ell_{2})e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\ell_{1})-\kappa_{\beta}\ell_{1}/20}.

(S.693)

In the same way, we can derive the same inequality as (
S.691
) for
\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{3}\mathfrak{B}_{3}}
:

\displaystyle\left\|\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{3}\mathfrak{B}_{3}}-\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}\to\mathfrak{B}_{3}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{A}_{2}\to\mathfrak{A}_{3}}(\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{2}\mathfrak{B}_{2}})\right\|_{1}\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\ell_{2})-\kappa_{\beta}\ell_{2}/20},

(S.694)

and hence

\displaystyle E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{3}\mathfrak{B}_{3}})

\displaystyle\leq E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{2}\mathfrak{B}_{2}})+\Theta(\ell_{3})e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\ell_{2})-\kappa_{\beta}\ell_{2}/20}.

(S.695)

By repeating the same process, we finally obtain

\displaystyle E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{\infty}\mathfrak{B}_{\infty}})=E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,AB})

\displaystyle\leq E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{1}\mathfrak{B}_{1}})+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\Theta(\ell_{j+1})e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\ell_{j})-\kappa_{\beta}\ell_{j}/20}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\Theta(\beta\log(\beta))-\kappa_{\beta}^{2}R/[81\log(d_{0})]}+e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(R)-\kappa_{\beta}\Theta(R^{2})},

(S.696)

where we use the inequality (
S.684
) for
E_{F}(\rho_{\beta,\mathfrak{A}_{1}\mathfrak{B}_{1}})

and the definition of
\ell_{s}
in Eq. (
S.681
), i.e.,
\ell_{s}=R^{s+1}
.
We thus prove the main inequality (
S.664
).
This completes the proof of Proposition
43
.
\square

S.IX.2
Clustering of information distribution: strong 1D thermal area law

As a relevant information measure, we consider the mutual information as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:B):=S(\rho_{\beta,A})+S(\rho_{\beta,B})-S(\rho_{\beta,AB})=S(\rho_{\beta,AB}|\rho_{\beta,A}\otimes\rho_{\beta,B}).

(S.697)

When
A
and
B
are connected as
A\sqcup B=\Lambda
, the mutual information is known to obey the area law as follows
[
18
,
19
]
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:B)=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\beta^{2/3}\right),

(S.698)

where the exponent
2/3
may be further improved
[
19
]
.
The area law implies that the total amount of the shared information between
A
and
B
is proportional to the surface region
\partial A
.
Here, we aim to prove that almost all the information localizes around the surface region between
A
and
B
.
In detail, we decompose
A=A_{1}\sqcup A_{2}
and
B=B_{2}\sqcup B_{1}
with
A_{2}
and
B_{2}
neighboring to each other and prove

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:B)\approx{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A_{2}:B_{2}),

(S.699)

where the approximation error depends on the size of
A_{2}
and
B_{2}
and approaches to zero for
|A_{2}|\to\infty
and
|B_{2}|\to\infty
(i.e.,
A_{1}=B_{1}=\emptyset
).
We, in general, prove the following lemma:

Corollary 45
.

Let us consider arbitrary subsets
A
and
B
and decompose
A=A_{1}\sqcup A_{2}
and
B=B_{2}\sqcup B_{1}
such that
d_{A,B}=d_{A_{2},B_{2}}
(see Fig.
17
). Also, we define
C
be the intermediate region between
A
and
B
, i.e.,
|C|=d_{A,B}
. Then, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:B)

\displaystyle\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(r)-\kappa_{\beta}r/10}+{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A_{2}:B_{2}),

(S.700)

where we set
\min(|A_{2}|,|B_{2}|)=r
. Note that the above bound does not depend on the size of
|C|
.

Figure 17:
Setup of Corollary
45
.
When we consider the mutual information between the subsets
A
and
B
, the shared information between
A
and
B
is approximately localized around the boundary regions
A_{2}
and
B_{2}
with an exponential tails as
e^{-r/\Theta(\beta)}
, where
r=\min(|A_{2}|,|B_{2}|)
.
This implies a strong version of the area law; that is, the total amount of information is not only proportional to the surface region of
A
(or
B
) but also exponentially localized around the boundary.

Proof of Corollary
45
.

From the definition of the CMI as
{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:B|C)={\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:BC)-{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:C)
,
we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:B)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A_{1}A_{2}:B_{2}B_{1})

\displaystyle={\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A_{1}A_{2}:B_{1}|B_{2})+{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A_{1}:B_{2}|A_{2})+{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A_{2}:B_{2})

\displaystyle\leq{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(AC:B_{1}|B_{2})+{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A_{1}:BC|A_{2})+{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A_{2}:B_{2}),

(S.701)

where we use the inequality (
S.622
).
By applying Theorem
5
to the above inequality, we prove the main inequality (
S.700
).
This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Corollary
45
]

Furthermore, Corollary
45
immediately leads to the exponential clustering of the mutual information.

Corollary 46
.

Let us assume that the correlation function is given by

\displaystyle{\rm Cor}_{\rho_{\beta}}(O_{A},O_{B})\leq\left\|O_{A}\right\|\cdot\left\|O_{B}\right\|{\rm Cor}(R),\quad d_{A,B}=R,

(S.702)

for arbitrary operator
O_{A},O_{B}
,
where
{\rm Cor}_{\rho_{\beta}}(O_{A},O_{B})={\rm tr}(\rho_{\beta}O_{A}O_{B})-{\rm tr}(\rho_{\beta}O_{A}){\rm tr}(\rho_{\beta}O_{B})
.
Then, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:B)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\log[1/{\rm Cor}(R)]}{\log(d_{0})}\right)^{\Theta(\beta)}[{\rm Cor}(R)]^{\frac{\kappa_{\beta}}{100\log(d_{0})}}

\displaystyle\leq[{\rm Cor}(R)]^{1/\Theta(\beta)},

(S.703)

where we assume
d_{0}=\mathcal{O}(1)
.

Remark.
Using Araki’s result
[
1
]
or its generalization
[
108
]
, we have
{\rm Cor}(R)=e^{-\Omega(R)}
for translation invariant infinite systems, where the temperature dependence is ignored.
In a more general setup, a recent result
[
77
]
has proven
{\rm Cor}(R)=e^{-e^{-\Theta(\beta)}\Omega(\sqrt{R})}
including the
\beta
dependence.
By using it, we prove in general

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:B)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left[-e^{-\Theta(\beta)}\Omega\left(\sqrt{R}\right)\right]

(S.704)

for arbitrary short-range interacting systems.
A similar clustering theorem for the mutual information has also been derived in Ref.
[
173
]
.
An advantage of our approach is its ability to extend to more general classes of interactions, e.g., long-range interactions.

The result implies the closeness between the reduced density matrix
\rho_{\beta,AB}
and a product state, i.e.,

\displaystyle\left\|\rho_{\beta,AB}-\rho_{\beta,A}\otimes\rho_{\beta,B}\right\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{2{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:B)},

(S.705)

where we use the Pinsker’s inequality for Eq. (
S.697
) as
\left\|\rho-\sigma\right\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{2S(\rho|\sigma)}
.
Hence, the clustering of the mutual information imposes a stronger constraint than the entanglement clustering.
On the other hand, we emphasize that the correlation length of the mutual information is as large as
e^{\Theta(\beta)}
, while the correlation length of the entanglement is at most
\mathcal{O}(\beta^{2})
as in Proposition
43
.

Proof of Corollary
46
.

We first use Corollary
45
to obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:B)

\displaystyle\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(r)-\kappa_{\beta}r/10}+{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A_{2}:B_{2}),

(S.706)

where we let
|A_{2}|=|B_{2}|=r
.
We then upper-bound the mutual information
{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A_{2}:B_{2})
using the correlation function (
S.702
).
By expanding the reduced density matrix
\rho_{\beta,A_{2}B_{2}}
using the operator bases
\{P_{A_{2},s}\}_{s=1}^{\mathcal{D}_{A_{2}}^{2}}
and
\{P_{B_{2},s}\}_{s=1}^{\mathcal{D}_{B_{2}}^{2}}
, we decompose

\displaystyle\rho_{\beta,A_{2}B_{2}}=\sum_{s,s^{\prime}}\lambda_{s,s^{\prime}}P_{A_{2},s}\otimes P_{B_{2},s^{\prime}},

(S.707)

where we choose
P_{A_{2},s}
and
P_{B_{2},s^{\prime}}
such that
\left\|P_{A_{2},s}\right\|_{1}\leq 2
and
\left\|P_{B_{2},s^{\prime}}\right\|_{1}\leq 2
*5
*5

*5

For example, it is possible by choosing
P_{A_{2},s}=\ket{x}\bra{x^{\prime}}+{\rm h.c.}
with
\{\ket{x}\}
orthonormal bases on the Hilbert space for
A_{2}
.
.
By using the assumption (
S.702
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\lambda_{s,s^{\prime}}-\bar{\lambda}_{s}\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}_{s^{\prime}}\right|\leq{\rm Cor}(R),

(S.708)

where
\bar{\lambda}_{s}:={\rm tr}\left(P_{A_{2},s}\rho_{\beta,A_{2}B_{2}}\right)
and
\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}_{s^{\prime}}:={\rm tr}\left(P_{B_{2},s^{\prime}}\rho_{\beta,A_{2}B_{2}}\right)
.
We therefore obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\rho_{\beta,A_{2}B_{2}}-\sum_{s}\bar{\lambda}_{s}P_{A_{2},s}\otimes\sum_{s^{\prime}}\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}_{s^{\prime}}P_{B_{2},s^{\prime}}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s,s^{\prime}}\left|\lambda_{s,s^{\prime}}-\bar{\lambda}_{s}\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}_{s^{\prime}}\right|\cdot\left\|P_{A_{2},s}\otimes P_{B_{2},s^{\prime}}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 4\mathcal{D}_{A_{2}}^{2}\mathcal{D}_{B_{2}}^{2}{\rm Cor}(R)\leq 4d_{0}^{4r}{\rm Cor}(R),

(S.709)

where we use the local Hilbert dimension
d_{0}
and the inequality
\left\|P_{A_{2},s}\otimes P_{B_{2},s^{\prime}}\right\|_{1}\leq\left\|P_{A_{2},s}\right\|_{1}\left\|P_{B_{2},s^{\prime}}\right\|_{1}\leq 4
.
By using the continuity inequality for the mutual information as in Ref.
[
174
, Remark 1 therein]
, we prove

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A_{2}:B_{2})\leq\Theta(1)d_{0}^{5r}{\rm Cor}(R).

(S.710)

Here, the parameter
r
can be adjusted to achieve any desired power of
{\rm Cor}(R)
in the right-hand side of the above inequality; for example,
[{\rm Cor}(R)]^{\eta}
with
0<\eta<1
. For simplicity, we choose
r=\log[1/{\rm Cor}(R)]/[10\log(d_{0})]
so that the right-hand side is proportional to
\sqrt{{\rm Cor}(R)}
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A_{2}:B_{2})\leq\Theta(1)\sqrt{{\rm Cor}(R)}.

(S.711)

By combining the inequality (
S.706
) and (
S.711
), we arrive at the inequality of

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rho_{\beta}}(A:B)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\log[1/{\rm Cor}(R)]}{10\log(d_{0})}\right)^{\Theta(\beta)}[{\rm Cor}(R)]^{\frac{\kappa_{\beta}}{100\log(d_{0})}}+\Theta(1)\sqrt{{\rm Cor}(R)},

(S.712)

which reduces to the main inequality (
S.703
).
This completes the proof of Corollary
46
.
\square

S.X
Quasi-locality of the true entanglement Hamiltonian

In Sec.
S.VIII.2
for the 1D CMI, we have shown that the approximate quantum Gibbs state
\tilde{\rho}_{\beta}

[see Eq. (
S.628
)]
satisfies the quasi-locality of entanglement Hamiltonians on the subsets
AB
,
BC
and
B
as in Eq. (
S.636
).
The inequality implies that the effective interactions of
\log(\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,L})
for
\forall L\subset\Lambda
is localized around the boundary
\partial L
with an exponential tail of
e^{-\Theta(R/\beta)}
.

Our fundamental question is whether the quasi-locality of the effective interactions is proved for the true entanglement Hamiltonian of
\rho_{\beta,B}
instead of
\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,B}
, or
{\rm tr}_{AC}\left(e^{\beta\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}}\right)
, that is,

\displaystyle\log\left(\rho_{\beta,B}\right)\overset{?}{\approx}\log\left(\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,B}\right).

(S.713)

We note that in general for given
\rho
and
\tilde{\rho}
, we cannot ensure
\log(\rho)\approx\log(\tilde{\rho})
only from the norm error of
\left\|\rho-\tilde{\rho}\right\|_{1}
even in the classical cases [see the inequality (
S.751
) below].
The quasi-locality of the true entanglement Hamiltonian is a stronger concept than the decay of the CMI and has several applications, e.g., in Hamiltonian learning of 1D quantum systems.

Using the inequality (
S.641
) in Lemma
41
, we can ensure

\displaystyle\beta\left\|H-\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}\right\|\lesssim e^{-\Theta(R/\beta)}.

(S.714)

That is, the approximate density matrix
\tilde{\rho}_{\beta}
has a similar global Hamiltonian.
We then have the following fundamental question:

“Can we prove closeness of the entanglement Hamiltonians between
e^{\beta H}
and
\tilde{\rho}_{\beta}
?
”

We address the problem in the 1D cases where the interactions are of a finite range.
Precisely speaking, we can prove the following theorem:

Theorem 6
.

Let us assume that the one-dimensional Hamiltonian only has finite-range interactions as follows:

\displaystyle H=\sum_{Z:{\rm diam}(Z)\leq k}h_{Z},\quad\sum_{Z:Z\ni i}\left\|h_{Z}\right\|\leq J_{0},

(S.715)

where
{\rm diam}(Z)
is defined as
{\rm diam}(Z)=\max_{i,i^{\prime}\in Z}(d_{i,i^{\prime}})
.
Then, for an arbitrary concatenate subset
B
(
\subset\Lambda
) such that
|B|=R
, we can prove

\displaystyle\left\|\log(\rho_{\beta,B})-\log(\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,B})\right\|\leq e^{\xi_{\beta}-R/\Theta(\beta)},

(S.716)

where we define
\xi_{\beta}
as a doubly exponential function of
\beta
, i.e.,
\xi_{\beta}=e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}
.

The proof is deferred to Subsections
S.X.2
,
S.X.3
and
S.X.5
.
The proof relies on the following two steps:

1.

We first prove the closeness of two density matrices
\rho
and
\sigma
under the condition of the small relative error (see Definition
1
). The statement is summarized in Theorem
7
in Sec.
S.X.2
.

2.

We then prove an upper bound for the relative error between
\rho_{\beta,B}
and
\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,B}
, which will be given in Subtheorem
2
, which will be proven in Sec.
S.X.5
.
Theorem
6
is straightforwardly derived by a simple combination of Theorem
7
and Subtheorem
2
as in Sec.
S.X.3
.
Here, the quasi-locality of the imaginary-time evolution plays a crucial role (see Lemma
50
and Proposition
51
).
To ensure the quasi-locality by the imaginary time evolution (Lemma
53
), we need the condition of the finite interaction length in the Hamiltonian
*6
*6

*6

In general, the quasi-locality of the imaginary time evolution breaks down
[
175
,
108
]
even when the interaction decay is exponential.
.

From the inequality (
S.638
) we have

\displaystyle\left\|\log(\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,B})-\beta(\tilde{H}_{B_{1}B_{2}}+H_{B_{3}}+\tilde{H}_{B_{4}B_{5}})\right\|\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/10},

(S.717)

where we denote
\tilde{H}_{B_{1}B_{2}}+H_{B_{3}}+\tilde{H}_{B_{4}B_{5}}
as follows [see also (
S.639
)]:

\displaystyle\beta(\tilde{H}_{B_{1}B_{2}}+H_{B_{3}}+\tilde{H}_{B_{4}B_{5}})=U^{\prime}_{B_{1}B_{2}}U^{\prime}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\left[\beta\left(\tilde{H}_{B_{1}}^{\ast}+H_{B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{B_{5}}\right)+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}^{\prime}_{B_{1}B_{2}}+\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}^{\prime}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\right]U_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{{}^{\prime}\dagger}U_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{{}^{\prime}\dagger}.

(S.718)

By combining the inequalities (
S.716
) and (
S.717
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\log(\rho_{\beta,B})-\beta(\tilde{H}_{B_{1}B_{2}}+H_{B_{3}}+\tilde{H}_{B_{4}B_{5}})\right\|\leq e^{\xi_{\beta}-R/\Theta(\beta)}+e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/10}.

(S.719)

From the above bound, we can ensure that the true effective interaction terms are localized around the boundary up to the distance of
\xi_{\beta}
.
Although mathematical tools to access the true entanglement Hamiltonian are scarce so far, we believe that a refined analytical technique may improve the temperature dependence to a similar form to the CMI decay, i.e.,
e^{\Theta(\beta)\log(\beta R)-\kappa_{\beta}R/10}
.

From Theorem
6
, the approximation of the effective interaction terms up to a distance
R/5
from the boundary
\partial B
is considered. As a more convenient statement, we prove the following corollary, which argues the quasi-locality of the effective interaction around the boundary of
B

Figure 18:
Setup of Corollary
47
.
We construct effective interactions
\{\tilde{V}_{B,s}\}_{s=0}^{\bar{s}+1}
, each which has an interaction length at most
2s+k
as in Eq. (
S.723
). By expanding
V_{B}^{\ast}
as in Eq. (
S.724
), we can prove the exponential decay of the effective interaction
V_{B}^{\ast}
.

Corollary 47
.

Let
V_{B}^{\ast}
be the effective interaction defined as the difference between the entanglement Hamiltonian
H_{B}^{\ast}
and the subset Hamiltonian
H_{B}
:

\displaystyle V_{B}^{\ast}:=H_{B}^{\ast}-H_{B}=\frac{1}{\beta}\log\left[{\rm tr}_{AC}\left(e^{\beta H}\right)\right]-H_{B},

(S.720)

where
B^{\rm c}=AC
.
Then, the effective interaction
V_{B}^{\ast}
is quasi-local around the boundary in the sense that

\displaystyle\left\|\left[V_{B}^{\ast},u_{i}\right]\right\|\leq\Theta(\beta)e^{\xi_{\beta}-d_{i,AC}/\Theta(\beta)}.

(S.721)

The inequality means that the effective interactions decay exponentially with the distance from the boundary.

Proof of Corollary
47
.

For the proof, we adopt a similar decomposition to Fig.
14
and
decompose the subsystem
B
as
B_{1,s}
,
B_{2,s}
,
B_{3,s}
,
B_{4,s}
and
B_{5,s}
such that (see Fig.
18
)

\displaystyle B_{1,s}=B_{2,s}=B_{4,s}=B_{5,s}=s,\quad B_{3,s}=|B|-4s=R-4s,\quad s\leq\bar{s}:=R/4.

(S.722)

Then, using the notation of Eq. (
S.718
),]
we define the approximate effective interaction
\tilde{V}_{B,s}
as

\displaystyle\tilde{V}_{B,s}

\displaystyle:=\left(\tilde{H}_{B_{1,s}B_{2,s}}+H_{B_{3,s}}+\tilde{H}_{B_{4,s}B_{5,s}}\right)-H_{B}

\displaystyle=\left(\tilde{H}_{B_{1,s}B_{2,s}}-H_{B_{1,s}B_{2,s}}\right)+\left(\tilde{H}_{B_{4,s}B_{5,s}}-H_{B_{4,s}B_{5,s}}\right)+h_{\partial B_{3,s}},

(S.723)

where we use the notation of Eq. (
S.9
), and
h_{\partial B_{3,s}}
is localized around the boundary of
B_{3,s}
up to the distance
k
.
We also let
\tilde{V}_{B,\bar{s}+1}=V_{B}^{\ast}
.
Note that the operator
\tilde{V}_{B,s}
is supported on
(B_{1,s}B_{2,s})[k]\cup(B_{4,s}B_{5,s})[k]
, where the boundary interaction between
B_{3,s}
and
B_{1,s}B_{2,s}B_{4,s}B_{5,s}
is taken into account.

Using the notation of
\tilde{V}_{B,s}
, we rewrite the effective interaction
V_{B}^{\ast}
as

\displaystyle V_{B}^{\ast}=\tilde{V}_{B,0}+\sum_{s=0}^{\bar{s}}\left(\tilde{V}_{B,s+1}-\tilde{V}_{B,s}\right).

(S.724)

Note that
\left(\tilde{V}_{B,s+1}-\tilde{V}_{B,s}\right)
is supported to the boundary region of
B
up to an distance
2(s+1)+k
(see Fig.
18
).
This means

\displaystyle\left[\tilde{V}_{B,s+1}-\tilde{V}_{B,s},u_{i}\right]=0\quad\textrm{for}\quad 2(s+1)<d_{i,AC}-k,

(S.725)

where the condition is satisfied for
s<d_{i,AC}/2-1-k/2
.
We therefore prove

\displaystyle\left[V_{B}^{\ast},u_{i}\right]=\sum_{s\geq d_{i,AC}/2-1-k/2}^{\bar{s}}\left[\tilde{V}_{B,s+1}-\tilde{V}_{B,s},u_{i}\right].

(S.726)

From the inequality (
S.719
), we can derive

\displaystyle\left\|V_{B}^{\ast}-\tilde{V}_{B,s}\right\|\leq e^{\xi_{\beta}-s/\Theta(\beta)},

(S.727)

which also yields the upper bound of

\displaystyle\left\|\left[\tilde{V}_{B,s+1}-\tilde{V}_{B,s},u_{i}\right]\right\|\leq 4e^{\xi_{\beta}-s/\Theta(\beta)}.

(S.728)

By applying the inequality (
S.728
) to (
S.726
), we obtain the main inequality as follows:

\displaystyle\left\|\left[V_{B}^{\ast},u_{i}\right]\right\|\leq\sum_{s\geq d_{i,AC}/2-1-k/2}^{\bar{s}}4e^{\xi_{\beta}-s/\Theta(\beta)}\leq\Theta(\beta)e^{\xi_{\beta}-d_{i,AC}/\Theta(\beta)}.

(S.729)

This completes the proof of Corollary
47
.
\square

S.X.1
1D Hamiltonian learning with log sample complexity

Here, we show an efficient Hamiltonian learning in one-dimensional systems by applying the quasi-locality of the true effective interactions.
By combining Theorem
6
and Proposition
12
with the method in Ref.
[
66
]
, we prove the following corollary:

Corollary 48
.

Let us adopt the same setup as in Theorem
6
.
Then, the sufficient number of copies of the quantum Gibbs state to learn the Hamiltonian
H
up to an error
\epsilon
is given by

\displaystyle N=e^{\xi_{\beta}}(1/\epsilon)^{\Theta(\beta^{2})}\log(|\Lambda|)

(S.730)

with
\xi_{\beta}=e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}
, where the success probability is larger than
0.99
as in Corollary
13
, and the error is measured by

\displaystyle\epsilon=\max_{Z\subset\Lambda}\left\|h_{Z}-\tilde{h}_{Z}\right\|,

(S.731)

with
\{\tilde{h}_{Z}\}_{Z\subset\Lambda}
the reconstructed interactions.
The time complexity for the learning is
|\Lambda|e^{\beta\xi_{\beta}}(1/\epsilon)^{\Theta(\beta)}
.

Remark.

The corollary provides not only sample-efficient but also time-efficient Hamiltonian learning.
On the time efficiency, the recent result achieved the polynomial time complexity in arbitrary dimensions, including infinite-dimensional graphs
[
183
,
111
]
.
Although achieving the logarithmic sample complexity is still an active open research area, we believe that the logarithmic sample complexity should be derived without relying on the effective Hamiltonian theory
*7
*7

*7

Private communication with Quynh The Nguyen.
.

Proof of Corollary
48
.

We adopt the same setup as in Ref.
[
65
]
, and the proof wholly relies on Ref.
[
66
]
, which treats the learning of commuting Hamiltonians.
First, we consider a set of concatenated regions
\{L_{s}\}_{s=1}^{n}
such that
|L_{s}|=R
and
n=\Theta(|\Lambda|)
.
For each of
\{L_{s}\}_{s=1}^{n}
, we define the center region with the length
R/5
as
\tilde{L}_{s}
.

Let us define the operator bases on
L_{s}
as
\{\hat{E}_{L_{s},j}\}_{j=1}^{\mathcal{D}_{L_{s}}^{2}}
for
s\in[n]
.
Then, the set of
\{e_{L_{s},j}\}_{j=1}^{\mathcal{D}_{L_{s}}^{2}}
, defined by

\displaystyle e_{L_{s},j}={\rm tr}\left(\hat{E}_{L_{s},j}\rho_{\beta,L_{s}}\right),

(S.732)

characterizes the reduced density matrices
\{\rho_{\beta,L_{s}}\}_{s=1}^{n}
.
That is, the total number of parameters is given by
n\mathcal{D}_{L_{s}}^{2}
.
We now denote
e^{\prime}_{L_{s},j}
by the measurement average of
\hat{E}_{L_{s},j}
for the copies of the quantum Gibbs state.
Using Refs.
[
176
,
177
,
178
]
, the sufficient number of samples
N
to ensure

\displaystyle\left\|e_{L_{s},j}-e^{\prime}_{L_{s},j}\right\|\leq\epsilon_{0}\quad\textrm{for}\quad\forall s,j

(S.733)

is given by

\displaystyle N=\frac{\Theta(1)}{\epsilon_{0}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n\mathcal{D}_{L_{s}}^{2}}{\delta}\right),

(S.734)

where the success probability is at least
1-\delta
.

Under the condition (
S.733
), the reconstructed quantum states
\{\rho^{\prime}_{\beta,L_{s}}\}_{s=1}^{n}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|\rho_{\beta,L_{s}}-\rho^{\prime}_{\beta,L_{s}}\right\|_{1}\leq\mathcal{D}_{L_{s}}^{2}\epsilon_{0}\leq e^{\Theta(R)}\epsilon_{0}.

(S.735)

Hence, using the inequality (
S.156
) with
\lambda_{\min}=e^{-\Theta(\beta)R}
from Proposition
12
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\log\left(\rho_{\beta,L_{s}}\right)-\log\left(\rho^{\prime}_{\beta,L_{s}}\right)\right\|\leq e^{\Theta(\beta)R}\epsilon_{0}.

(S.736)

Therefore, from Theorem
6
, the subset Hamiltonian on the center region
\tilde{L}_{s}
can be estimated up to the error of

\displaystyle e^{\Theta(\beta)R}\epsilon_{0}+e^{\xi_{\beta}-R/\Theta(\beta)},

(S.737)

where the second term comes from the effective interaction terms due to the partial trace.
To make it smaller than the desired error
\epsilon
, we have to let

\displaystyle R=\xi_{\beta}+\Theta(\beta)\log(1/\epsilon),\quad\epsilon_{0}=e^{-\Theta(\beta)R}\epsilon=e^{-\Theta(\beta)\xi_{\beta}}(1/\epsilon)^{-\Theta(\beta^{2})}\epsilon=e^{-\xi_{\beta}}\epsilon^{\Theta(\beta^{2})},

(S.738)

where we let
\beta\xi_{\beta}=\beta e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}=e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)+\log\log(\beta)}}=e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}
.
By applying the above choice and
\delta=0.01
to Eq. (
S.734
), we prove the statement (
S.730
).
Also, the necessary time to construct the Hamiltonian is at most
n\cdot{\rm poly}(\mathcal{D}_{L_{s}})=ne^{\Theta(R)}\leq|\Lambda|e^{\xi_{\beta}}(1/\epsilon)^{\Theta(\beta)}
.
This completes the proof of Corollary
48
.
\square

S.X.2
Continuity inequality for logarithmic operators

In this section, we aim to prove the following statements to relate the relative error to the continuity inequality of the operator logarithm.
We first define the relative error as follows:

Definition 1

(Relative error)
.

We define the relative error
\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)
between the density matrices
\rho
and
\sigma
as

\displaystyle\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma):=\sup_{\ket{\psi}}\frac{|\bra{\psi}\rho-\sigma\ket{\psi}|}{\bra{\psi}\rho\ket{\psi}},

(S.739)

where the
\sup_{\ket{\psi}}
is taken for all the set of quantum states
\ket{\psi}
.

Remark.
When we simply consider
\sup_{\ket{\psi}}|\bra{\psi}\rho-\sigma\ket{\psi}|
, it reduces to the standard operator norm as
\left\|\rho-\sigma\right\|
.
By using the minimum eigenvalue of
\rho
as
\lambda_{\min}(\rho)
, we can upper bound

\displaystyle\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)\leq\frac{1}{\lambda_{\min}(\rho)}\sup_{\ket{\psi}}|\bra{\psi}\rho-\sigma\ket{\psi}|=\frac{\left\|\rho-\sigma\right\|}{\lambda_{\min}(\rho)}.

(S.740)

We note that we usually obtain
\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)\neq\delta_{\rm R}(\sigma,\rho)
.
However, the two quantities are related to each other.
To see it, we use the definition (
S.739
) to provide

\displaystyle\left[1-\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)\right]\bra{\psi}\rho\ket{\psi}\leq\bra{\psi}\sigma\ket{\psi}\leq\left[1+\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)\right]\bra{\psi}\rho\ket{\psi},

(S.741)

and hence

\displaystyle\frac{\bra{\psi}\sigma\ket{\psi}}{1+\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)}\leq\bra{\psi}\rho\ket{\psi}\leq\frac{\bra{\psi}\sigma\ket{\psi}}{1-\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)}.

(S.742)

Then, for
\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)\leq 1/2
, we have

\displaystyle\bra{\psi}\sigma\ket{\psi}[1-\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)]\leq\bra{\psi}\rho\ket{\psi}\leq\bra{\psi}\sigma\ket{\psi}[1+2\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)],

(S.743)

which yields for
\delta_{\rm R}(\sigma,\rho)

\displaystyle\delta_{\rm R}(\sigma,\rho)\leq 2\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)\quad\textrm{for}\quad\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)\leq\frac{1}{2}.

(S.744)

On the relative error, one can immediately prove the following lemma:

Lemma 49
.

A small relative error between
\rho
and
\sigma
also implies a small relative error between their reduced density matrices:

\displaystyle\delta_{\rm R}(\rho_{L},\sigma_{L})\leq\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma),

(S.745)

where
\rho_{L}
and
\sigma_{L}
are reduced density matrices on a subset
L\subset\Lambda
.

Proof of Lemma
49
.

For the proof, we let
\ket{\psi_{L}}
be an arbitrary quantum state on the subset
L
and
\{\ket{x_{L^{\rm c}}}\}_{x}
be the orthonormal bases on the subset
L^{\rm c}
. We then obtain

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{x}\bra{\psi_{L},x_{L^{\rm c}}}\rho-\sigma\ket{\psi_{L},x_{L^{\rm c}}}\right|=\left|\bra{\psi_{L}}\rho_{L}-\sigma_{L}\ket{\psi_{L}}\right|,

(S.746)

where we use
\sum_{x}\bra{x_{L^{\rm c}}}\rho-\sigma\ket{x_{L^{\rm c}}}={\rm tr}_{L^{\rm c}}(\rho-\sigma)
.
Also, by using the global relative error
\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{x}\bra{\psi_{L},x_{L^{\rm c}}}\rho-\sigma\ket{\psi_{L},x_{L^{\rm c}}}\right|\leq\sum_{x}\left|\bra{\psi_{L},x_{L^{\rm c}}}\rho-\sigma\ket{\psi_{L},x_{L^{\rm c}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)\sum_{x}\bra{\psi_{L},x_{L^{\rm c}}}\rho\ket{\psi_{L},x_{L^{\rm c}}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)\bra{\psi_{L}}\rho\ket{\psi_{L}}.

(S.747)

By applying the inequality (
S.747
) to Eq. (
S.746
), we arrive at the inequality of

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\bra{\psi_{L}}\rho_{L}-\sigma_{L}\ket{\psi_{L}}\right|}{\bra{\psi_{L}}\rho\ket{\psi_{L}}}\leq\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)

(S.748)

for
\forall\psi_{L}
, which yields the desired inequality (
S.745
).
This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
49
]

Under the assumption of the relative error, one can prove the following continuity inequality for logarithmic operators (see Appendix
C.2
for the proof):

Theorem 7
.

Under the condition that

\displaystyle\varepsilon=\max\left[\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma),\delta_{\rm R}(\sigma,\rho)\right]\leq\frac{1}{2},

(S.749)

we obtain the upper bound as

\displaystyle\left\|\log(\sigma)-\log(\rho)\right\|\leq\varepsilon\left[\frac{4\log[2\lambda^{-1}_{\min}(\rho)]}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{e\log[2\lambda^{-1}_{\min}(\rho)]}{2\pi}\right)+23\right],

(S.750)

where
\lambda^{-1}_{\min}(\rho)
is the minimum eigenvalue of
\rho
.

Remark.

To satisfy the condition (
S.749
), it is enough to ensure

\min\left(\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma),\delta_{\rm R}(\sigma,\rho)\right)\leq\frac{1}{4}

from the inequality (
S.744
).
In practically important cases, the inverse minimum eigenvalue
\lambda^{-1}_{\min}(\rho)
is at most of
{\rm Poly}(\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda})
, where
\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}
is the Hilbert space dimension. Then, the upper bound is roughly given by
\left\|\log(\sigma)-\log(\rho)\right\|\lesssim\varepsilon\log(\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda})\log\log(\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda})
.

In the classical case or the commuting case of
[\rho,\sigma]=0
, the inequality is rather trivial because
\sigma
and
\rho
have simultaneous eigenstates. By letting the eigenvalues of
\rho
and
\sigma
be
\{\rho_{m}\}_{m}
and
\{\sigma_{m}\}_{m}
, respectively, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\log(\sigma)-\log(\rho)\right\|=\max_{m}\left|\log(\rho_{m})-\log(\sigma_{m})\right|=\max_{m}\left|\log\left(\frac{\sigma_{m}}{\rho_{m}}\right)\right|\leq\log(1+\varepsilon)\leq\varepsilon,

(S.751)

where we use the condition (
S.749
) in the second inequality.
Therefore, the dependence on the minimum eigenvalue
\lambda_{\min}(\rho)
only appears in the quantum setups.

If we use only the norm distance
\left\|\rho-\sigma\right\|
, we obtain from (
S.740
):

\displaystyle\left\|\log(\sigma)-\log(\rho)\right\|\lesssim\frac{{\rm Polylog}[\lambda^{-1}_{\min}(\rho)]}{\lambda_{\min}(\rho)}\left\|\rho-\sigma\right\|,

(S.752)

which requires
\left\|\rho-\sigma\right\|\ll\lambda_{\min}(\rho)
for a good approximation.

Finally, we emphasize that the converse of Theorem
7
is not true
*8
*8

*8

In the commuting cases, from the relations in (
S.751
), the small relative error and the closeness of the operator logarithms are equivalent.
.
That is, the closeness of the logarithmic operators does not imply a small relative error, i.e.,

\displaystyle\left\|\log(\sigma)-\log(\rho)\right\|\ll 1\xrightarrow{\textrm{Not imply}}\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)\ll 1.

(S.753)

The counterexample is given by the following case in a
1
qubit system:

\displaystyle\log(\sigma)=J\sigma_{z}+\epsilon\sigma_{x},\quad\log(\rho)=J\sigma_{z}\quad(J>0),

(S.754)

where
\sigma_{x}
and
\sigma_{z}
are the Pauli matrix with
\sigma_{z}\ket{0}=\ket{0}
and
\sigma_{z}\ket{1}=-\ket{1}
.
Then, we have
\left\|\log(\sigma)-\log(\rho)\right\|=\epsilon
, whereas we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)\geq\frac{\bra{1}\sigma-\rho\ket{1}}{\bra{1}\rho\ket{1}}

\displaystyle=e^{J}\left|\cosh(J)-\cosh\left(\sqrt{J^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\right)-\sinh(J)+\frac{J\sinh\left(\sqrt{J^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\right)}{\sqrt{J^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}}\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{\left|e^{J}\sinh(J)-J\right|}{2J^{2}}\epsilon^{2}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{4}),

(S.755)

which can be arbitrarily large in the limit of
J\to\infty
.
From this point, it is an important mathematical open problem to identify the necessary and efficient condition to get the small logarithmic error
\left\|\log(\sigma)-\log(\rho)\right\|\ll 1
.

In applying Theorem
7
to quantum Gibbs states, a technical challenge is to estimate the relative error between
e^{\beta H}
and
e^{\beta\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}}
.
We prove the following subtheorem:

Subtheorem 2
.

Let us adopt the same setup as in Theorem
6
.
Then, we obtain the upper bound for
\delta_{\rm R}(e^{\beta H},e^{\beta\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}})
as follows:

\displaystyle\delta_{\rm R}\left(e^{\beta H},e^{\beta\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}}\right)\leq\exp\left[\xi_{\beta}-R/\Theta(\beta)\right],

(S.756)

where
\xi_{\beta}=e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}
, and
R
is defined as the size of
B
[see the definition (
S.762
) below].

S.X.3
Proof of Theorem
6

Here, we prove Theorem
6
based on Theorem
7
and Subtheorem
2
.
We first apply Theorem
7
to the operators
\rho_{\beta,B}
and
\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,B}
.
To utilize the inequality (
S.750
), we need to estimate the minimum eigenvalue of
\rho_{\beta,B}
, which we denote by
\lambda_{\min}
.
By using the inequality (
S.146
) in Proposition
12
, we can immediately obtain

\displaystyle\log(1/\lambda_{\min})=\mathcal{O}(\beta|B|),

(S.757)

where we use the fact that the Hamiltonian is assumed to be
k
-local as in (
S.715
).
Also, from Lemma
49
, we can ensure that the relative error is upper-bounded as

\displaystyle\delta_{\rm R}\left[{\rm tr}_{AC}\left(e^{\beta H}\right),{\rm tr}_{AC}\left(e^{\beta\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}}\right)\right]\leq\delta_{\rm R}\left(e^{\beta H},e^{\beta\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}}\right).

(S.758)

Then, by using Theorem
7
, we prove

\displaystyle\left\|\log(\rho_{\beta,B})-\log(\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,B})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\Theta(1)\delta_{\rm R}\left(e^{\beta H},e^{\beta\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}}\right)(\beta|B|)^{2},

(S.759)

where we use
\beta|B|\log(\beta|B|)\leq(\beta|B|)^{2}
from
x\log(x)\leq x^{2}
for
x>0
.
By applying the upper bound (
S.756
) for
\delta_{\rm R}\left(e^{\beta H},e^{\beta\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}}\right)
to the above inequality,
we prove

\displaystyle\left\|\log(\rho_{\beta,B})-\log(\tilde{\rho}_{\beta,B})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq e^{\xi_{\beta}-R/\Theta(\beta)+\Theta(1)\log(\beta R)},

(S.760)

where we use
|B|=R
.
For
R\geq\xi_{\beta}
, we have
-R/\Theta(\beta)+\Theta(1)\log(\beta R)\leq-R/\Theta(\beta)
, which gives the main inequality (
S.716
).
This completes the proof of Theorem
6
.
\square

S.X.4
Proof of Subtheorem
2
: estimation of the relative error

For simplicity of the notations, we write

\displaystyle H_{0}=H_{AB_{1}}+H_{B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}+H_{B_{5}C},

\displaystyle v_{1}=\partial h_{AB_{1}},\quad v_{2}=\partial h_{B_{5}C},

(S.761)

where
H=H_{0}+v_{1}+v_{2}
.
For the convenience of readers, we show the definition (
S.628
) of
e^{\beta\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}}
again with the decomposition of
e^{\beta H}
in Eq. (
S.624
):

\displaystyle e^{\beta H}=\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}e^{\beta H_{0}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{\dagger},

\displaystyle e^{\beta\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}}=\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta H_{0}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}^{\dagger},

(S.762)

with

\displaystyle e^{\beta\left(H_{0}+v_{1}\right)}=\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}e^{\beta H_{0}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{\dagger},

\displaystyle e^{\beta\left(H_{0}+v_{1}+v_{2}\right)}=\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}e^{\beta\left(H_{0}+v_{1}\right)}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{\dagger}.

(S.763)

Here, the approximation
\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}
and
\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}
are defined by the local approximation of the belief propagation operators
\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}
and
\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}
, respectively:

\displaystyle\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}=\mathcal{T}\exp\left(\int_{0}^{1}\tilde{\phi}_{B_{1}B_{2},\tau}d\tau\right),\quad\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}=\mathcal{T}\exp\left(\int_{0}^{1}\tilde{\phi}_{B_{4}B_{5},\tau}d\tau\right),

(S.764)

where
\tilde{\phi}_{B_{1}B_{2},\tau}
and
\tilde{\phi}_{B_{4}B_{5},\tau}
are explicitly given by

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{B_{1}B_{2},\tau}=\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}f_{\beta}(t)\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{(B_{1}B_{2})^{\rm c}}\left[v_{1}(H_{0}+\tau v_{1},t)\right]dt,

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{B_{4}B_{5},\tau}=\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}f_{\beta}(t)\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{(B_{4}B_{5})^{\rm c}}\left[v_{2}(H_{0}+v_{1}+\tau v_{2},t)\right]dt.

(S.765)

Note that after the partial traces
\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{(B_{1}B_{2})^{\rm c}}
and
\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{(B_{4}B_{5})^{\rm c}}
, the operators are supported on
B_{1}B_{2}
and
B_{4}B_{5}
, respectively.

To derive the relative error between
e^{\beta H}
and
e^{\beta\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}}
in Eq. (
S.762
), we use the following inequality which holds for an arbitrary quantum state
\ket{\psi}
:

\displaystyle|\bra{\psi}e^{\beta H}-e^{\beta\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}}\ket{\psi}|

\displaystyle=|\bra{\psi}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}e^{\beta H_{0}/2}\left(1-e^{-\beta H_{0}/2}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{-1}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{-1}e^{\beta\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{-1}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{-1}e^{-\beta H_{0}/2}\right)e^{\beta H_{0}/2}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{\dagger}\ket{\psi}|

\displaystyle\leq\bra{\psi}e^{\beta H}\ket{\psi}\left\|1-\mathcal{W}\mathcal{W}^{\dagger}\right\|,

(S.766)

where we define
\mathcal{W}
as follows:
