\displaystyle\mathcal{W}:=e^{-\beta H_{0}/2}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{-1}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{-1}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta H_{0}/2}.

(S.767)

By decomposing
1-\mathcal{W}\mathcal{W}^{\dagger}=1-\mathcal{W}+\mathcal{W}(1-\mathcal{W}^{\dagger})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\delta_{\rm R}\left(e^{\beta H},e^{\beta\tilde{H}_{\Lambda}}\right)\leq\left\|1-\mathcal{W}\mathcal{W}^{\dagger}\right\|\leq\left\|1-\mathcal{W}\right\|\left(1+\left\|\mathcal{W}\right\|\right).

(S.768)

To estimate the norm of
\left\|1-\mathcal{W}\right\|
, we use the following lemma:

Lemma 50
.

Let
A_{x}
and
B_{x}
be arbitrary operators that depend on
x
with
0\leq x\leq x_{0}
.
We then define an arbitrary parameterization of the Hamiltonian as
H_{x}
(
0\leq x\leq x_{0}
) such that
H_{x=0}=H_{0}
and
\left\|dH_{x}/dx\right\|<\infty
.
We then obtain

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\beta H_{0}/2}\left(\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{x_{0}}B_{x}dx}\right)^{-1}\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{x_{0}}A_{x}dx}e^{\beta H_{0}/2}-1\right\|

\displaystyle\leq e^{\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\left\|e^{-\beta H_{x}/2}A_{x}e^{\beta H_{x}/2}\right\|+\left\|e^{-\beta H_{x}/2}B_{x}e^{\beta H_{x}/2}\right\|+\left\|\Delta H_{x}\right\|dx}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\left\|e^{-\beta H_{x}/2}(A_{x}-B_{x})e^{\beta H_{x}/2}\right\|dx,

(S.769)

where we define
\Delta H_{x}
as

\displaystyle\Delta H_{x}:=\beta\int_{0}^{\beta}e^{-zH_{x}/2}\frac{dH_{x}}{dx}e^{zH_{x}/2}dz.

(S.770)

Proof of Lemma
50
.

We first write

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{x_{0}}A_{x}dx}e^{\beta H_{0}/2}=e^{A_{x_{M}}/N}\cdots e^{A_{x_{2}}/N}e^{A_{x_{1}}/N}e^{\beta H_{0}/2}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-2}),

(S.771)

with
x_{m}=m/N
for
1\leq m\leq Nx_{0}=:M
. We then discretize
H_{x}
by
\{H_{x_{1}},H_{x_{2}},\ldots,H_{x_{M}}\}
and obtain

\displaystyle e^{A_{x_{M}}/N}\cdots e^{A_{x_{2}}/N}e^{A_{x_{1}}/N}e^{\beta H_{0}/2}

\displaystyle=e^{\beta H_{x_{M}}/2}\left(e^{-\beta H_{x_{M}}/2}e^{A_{x_{M}}/N}e^{\beta H_{x_{M}}/2}\right)\left(e^{-\beta H_{x_{M}}/2}e^{\beta H_{x_{M-1}}/2}\right)

\displaystyle\cdots\left(e^{-\beta H_{x_{2}}/2}e^{A_{x_{2}}/N}e^{\beta H_{x_{2}}/2}\right)\left(e^{-\beta H_{x_{2}}/2}e^{\beta H_{x_{1}}/2}\right)\left(e^{-\beta H_{x_{1}}/2}e^{A_{x_{1}}/N}e^{\beta H_{x_{1}}/2}\right)\left(e^{-\beta H_{x_{1}}/2}e^{\beta H_{0}/2}\right).

(S.772)

For general
m
, we let
H_{x_{m}}-H_{x_{m-1}}=(1/N)(dH_{x}/dx)_{x=x_{m-1}}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-2})
and reduce

\displaystyle\left(e^{-\beta H_{x_{m}}/2}e^{A_{x_{m}}/N}e^{\beta H_{x_{m}}/2}\right)\left(e^{-\beta H_{x_{m}}/2}e^{\beta H_{x_{m-1}}/2}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(1+\frac{1}{N}e^{-\beta H_{x_{m}}/2}A_{x_{m}}e^{\beta H_{x_{m}}/2}\right)\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2N}\int_{0}^{\beta}e^{-zH_{x_{m-1}}/2}\left(\frac{dH_{x}}{dx}\right)_{x=x_{m-1}}e^{zH_{x_{m-1}}/2}dz\right)+\mathcal{O}(N^{-2})

\displaystyle=\exp\left[\frac{1}{N}\left(e^{-\beta H_{x_{m}}/2}A_{x_{m}}e^{\beta H_{x_{m}}/2}-\frac{1}{2}\Delta H_{x_{m-1}}\right)\right]+\mathcal{O}(N^{-2}),

(S.773)

where
\Delta H_{x}
has been defined in Eq. (
S.770
).

By combining the above calculations, we prove

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{x_{0}}A_{x}dx}e^{\beta H_{0}/2}=e^{\beta H_{x_{0}}/2}\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\left(e^{-\beta H_{x}/2}A_{x}e^{\beta H_{x}/2}-\Delta H_{x}/2\right)}dx

(S.774)

In the same way, we obtain

\displaystyle e^{-\beta H_{0}/2}\left(\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{x_{0}}B_{x}dx}\right)^{-1}=\tilde{\mathcal{T}}e^{-\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\left(e^{-\beta H_{x}/2}B_{x}e^{\beta H_{x}/2}+\Delta H_{x}/2\right)dx}e^{-\beta H_{x_{0}}/2},

(S.775)

where we let the time-ordering operator
\tilde{\mathcal{T}}
be

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{x_{0}}B_{x}dx}\right)^{-1}=\tilde{\mathcal{T}}e^{-\int_{0}^{x_{0}}B_{x}dx}=e^{-B_{x_{1}}/N}e^{-B_{x_{2}}/N}\cdots e^{-B_{x_{M}}/N}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-2})

(S.776)

with
N\to\infty
.

From Eqs. (
S.774
) and (
S.775
), we can derive

\displaystyle e^{-\beta H_{0}/2}\left(\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{x_{0}}B_{x}dx}\right)^{-1}\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{x_{0}}A_{x}dx}e^{\beta H_{0}/2}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{T}}e^{-\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\left(e^{-\beta H_{x}/2}B_{x}e^{\beta H_{x}/2}+\Delta H_{x}/2\right)dx}\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\left(e^{-\beta H_{x}/2}A_{x}e^{\beta H_{x}/2}-\Delta H_{x}/2\right)dx}.

(S.777)

To estimate the norm of the above operator, we define the operator
G_{z}
as

\displaystyle G_{z}:=\tilde{\mathcal{T}}e^{-\int_{0}^{z}\left(e^{-\beta H_{x}/2}B_{x}e^{\beta H_{x}/2}+\Delta H_{x}/2\right)dx}\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{z}\left(e^{-\beta H_{x}/2}A_{x}e^{\beta H_{x}/2}-\Delta H_{x}/2\right)dx}-1,

(S.778)

which depends on the parameter
z
.
Here, we aim to derive the upper bound for
\left\|G_{x_{0}}\right\|
.
We then calculate the derivative of

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dz}G_{z}=\tilde{\mathcal{T}}e^{-\int_{0}^{z}\left(e^{-\beta H_{x}/2}B_{x}e^{\beta H_{x}/2}+\Delta H_{x}/2\right)dx}\left[e^{-\beta H_{z}/2}\left(A_{z}-B_{z}\right)e^{\beta H_{z}/2}\right]\mathcal{T}e^{\int_{0}^{z}\left(e^{-\beta H_{x}/2}A_{x}e^{\beta H_{x}/2}-\Delta H_{x}/2\right)dx},

(S.779)

and obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{d}{dz}G_{z}\right\|\leq e^{\int_{0}^{z}\left\|e^{-\beta H_{x}/2}A_{x}e^{\beta H_{x}/2}\right\|+\left\|e^{-\beta H_{x}/2}B_{x}e^{\beta H_{x}/2}\right\|+\left\|\Delta H_{x}\right\|dx}\cdot\left\|e^{-\beta H_{z}/2}(A_{z}-B_{z})e^{\beta H_{z}/2}\right\|.

(S.780)

By applying the above inequality to

\displaystyle\left\|G_{x_{0}}\right\|\leq\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\left\|\frac{d}{dx}G_{x}\right\|dx,

(S.781)

we prove the main inequality (
S.769
).
This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
50
]

Here, we adopt the Hamiltonian
H_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}
as

\displaystyle H_{0,\tau}:=H_{0}+\tau v_{1},\quad H_{1,\tau}:=H_{0}+v_{1}+\tau v_{2},

(S.782)

which reduce Eq. (
S.765
) to

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{B_{1}B_{2},\tau}=\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}f_{\beta}(t)\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{(B_{1}B_{2})^{\rm c}}\left[v_{1}(H_{0,\tau},t)\right]dt,\quad\tilde{\phi}_{B_{4}B_{5},\tau}=\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}f_{\beta}(t)\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{(B_{4}B_{5})^{\rm c}}\left[v_{2}(H_{1,\tau},t)\right]dt.

(S.783)

Then, by applying Lemma
50
to

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}-1=e^{-\beta H_{0}/2}\Phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}}}^{-1}\Phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C}}^{-1}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{4}B_{5}}\tilde{\Phi}_{B_{1}B_{2}}e^{\beta H_{0}/2}-1,

(S.784)

we obtain the upper bound of

\displaystyle\left\|1-\mathcal{W}\right\|\leq

\displaystyle e^{\bar{w}_{B_{1}B_{2}}+\bar{w}_{B_{4}B_{5}}+\bar{w}_{0}}\int_{0}^{1}\left\|e^{-\beta H_{0,\tau}/2}\left(\phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}},\tau}-\tilde{\phi}_{B_{1}B_{2},\tau}\right)e^{\beta H_{0,\tau}/2}\right\|d\tau

\displaystyle+e^{\bar{w}_{B_{1}B_{2}}+\bar{w}_{B_{4}B_{5}}+\bar{w}_{0}}\int_{0}^{1}\left\|e^{-\beta H_{1,\tau}/2}\left(\phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C},\tau}-\tilde{\phi}_{B_{4}B_{5},\tau}\right)e^{\beta H_{1,\tau}/2}\right\|d\tau,

(S.785)

where
\bar{w}_{B_{1}B_{2}}
,
\bar{w}_{B_{4}B_{5}}
and
\bar{w}_{0}
, are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\bar{w}_{B_{1}B_{2}}:=\int_{0}^{1}\left(\left\|e^{-\beta H_{0,\tau}/2}\phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}},\tau}e^{\beta H_{0,\tau}/2}\right\|+\left\|e^{-\beta H_{0,\tau}/2}\tilde{\phi}_{B_{1}B_{2},\tau}e^{\beta H_{0,\tau}/2}\right\|\right)d\tau,

\displaystyle\bar{w}_{B_{4}B_{5}}:=\int_{0}^{1}\left(\left\|e^{-\beta H_{1,\tau}/2}\phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C},\tau}e^{\beta H_{1,\tau}/2}\right\|+\left\|e^{-\beta H_{1,\tau}/2}\tilde{\phi}_{B_{4}B_{5},\tau}e^{\beta H_{1,\tau}/2}\right\|\right)d\tau,

\displaystyle\bar{w}_{0}:=\beta\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\beta}\left(\left\|e^{-zH_{0,\tau}/2}v_{1}e^{zH_{0,\tau}/2}\right\|+\left\|e^{-zH_{1,\tau}/2}v_{2}e^{zH_{1,\tau}/2}\right\|\right)dzd\tau.

(S.786)

Using Eq. (
S.783
), we further obtain an upper bound of

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\beta H_{0,\tau}/2}\left(\phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}},\tau}-\tilde{\phi}_{B_{1}B_{2},\tau}\right)e^{\beta H_{0,\tau}/2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}f_{\beta}(t)\left\|e^{-\beta H_{0,\tau}/2}\left\{v_{1}(H_{0,\tau},t)-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{(B_{1}B_{2})^{\rm c}}\left[v_{1}(H_{0,\tau},t)\right]\right\}e^{\beta H_{0,\tau}/2}\right\|dt,

(S.787)

where we can derive a similar bound for
\left\|e^{-\beta H_{0}/2}\left(\phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C},\tau}-\tilde{\phi}_{B_{4}B_{5},\tau}\right)e^{\beta H_{0}/2}\right\|
.
We can estimate the integral in Eq. (
S.787
) using the following general proposition:

Proposition 51
.

Let
O_{X}
be an arbitrary operator on a subset
X
with
|X|\leq k
, where we assume
\left\|O_{X}\right\|=1
.
Then, for an arbitrary Hamiltonian with finite-range interactions as in Eq. (
S.715
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\beta H/2}\left\{O_{X}(H,t)-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left(O_{X}(H,t)\right)\right\}e^{\beta H/2}\right\|\leq\exp\left(e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}\right)\min\left(1,e^{-\mu r/3+vt}\right),

(S.788)

where
L=X[r]
(see Fig.
19
for the setup).
Moreover, as long as the RHS of (
S.788
) is smaller than
1
, i.e.,
r=e^{e^{\Omega(\beta)}}
*9
*9

*9

The condition is utilized in the inequality (
S.823
) for the proof below.
,
we also prove the upper bound of

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\beta H/2}O_{X}(H,t)e^{\beta H/2}\right\|\leq e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}},\quad\left\|e^{-\beta H/2}\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left(O_{X}(H,t)\right)e^{\beta H/2}\right\|\leq e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}.

(S.789)

We defer the proof to the subsequent subsection (Sec.
S.X.5
).

Figure 19:
Setup of Proposition
51
.
We consider a concatenated subset
X
such that
|X|\leq k
. We then consider the time evolution of
O_{X}(H,t)
and its local approximation onto the region
X[r]
, which is also denoted by
L
. The approximated operator is defined by
\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}[O_{X}(H,t)]
, whose approximation error has been estimated by Lemma
3
with the use of the Lieb–Robinson bound.
In Proposition
51
, we aim to estimate the amplification of the error by the imaginary time evolution (
S.788
).
The statement plays a key role in proving Subtheorem
2
through the estimation of
\left\|1-\mathcal{W}\right\|
in (
S.785
).

By applying the above proposition to (
S.787
) with

\displaystyle O_{X}\to v_{1}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z:{\rm diam}(Z)\leq k\\
Z\cap B_{1}\neq\emptyset,\ Z\cap B_{2}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}h_{Z},

\displaystyle H\to H_{0,\tau},\quad L\to B_{1}B_{2},\quad r\to R/5-k,

(S.790)

we reduce the inequality (
S.787
) to

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\beta H_{0}/2}\left(\phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}},\tau}-\tilde{\phi}_{B_{1}B_{2},\tau}\right)e^{\beta H_{0}/2}\right\|\leq\frac{\beta}{2}\exp\left(e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}\right)\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}f_{\beta}(t)\min\left(1,e^{-\mu R/15+\mu k/3+vt}\right)dt,

(S.791)

Note that the notations
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
were given in Eq. (
S.761
).
Using a similar decomposition to (
S.84
), we obtain the following inequality by choosing
t_{0}
such that
e^{-\mu R/15+vt}=e^{-\mu R/30}
, or
t_{0}=\mu R/(30v)
:

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}f_{\beta}(t)\min\left(1,e^{-\mu R/15+\mu k/3+vt}\right)dt\leq e^{-\mu R/30+\mu k/3}\int_{|t|\leq t_{0}}f_{\beta}(t)dt+\int_{|t|\geq t_{0}}f_{\beta}(t)dt\leq e^{-R/\Theta(\beta)},

(S.792)

which reduces the inequality (
S.791
) to

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\beta H_{0}/2}\left(\phi_{\partial h_{AB_{1}},\tau}-\tilde{\phi}_{B_{1}B_{2},\tau}\right)e^{\beta H_{0}/2}\right\|\leq\exp\left(e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}\right)e^{-R/\Theta(\beta)},

(S.793)

where we absorb the coefficient
(\beta/2)
into
\exp\left(e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}\right)
.
We obtain the same inequality for the norm of
\left\|e^{-\beta H_{0}/2}\left(\phi_{\partial h_{B_{5}C},\tau}-\tilde{\phi}_{B_{4}B_{5},\tau}\right)e^{\beta H_{0}/2}\right\|
.

Also, by using the inequality (
S.789
), we can upper-bound
\bar{w}_{B_{1}B_{2}}
,

and

) by
e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}
, which yields

\displaystyle e^{\bar{w}_{B_{1}B_{2}}+\bar{w}_{B_{4}B_{5}}+\bar{w}_{0}}\leq\exp\left(e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}\right).

(S.794)

By applying the inequalities (
S.793
) and (
S.794

), we prove

\displaystyle\left\|1-\mathcal{W}\right\|\leq\exp\left(e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}\right)e^{-R/\Theta(\beta)},

(S.795)

which reduces the inequality (
S.768
) to the desired one in (
S.756
).
This completes the proof of Subtheorem
2
.
\square

S.X.5
Proof of Proposition
51

S.X.5.1
Preliminaries

Here, we consider the one-dimensional Hamiltonian with finite-range interactions as follows:

\displaystyle H=\sum_{Z:{\rm diam}(Z)\leq k}h_{Z},\quad\sum_{Z:Z\ni i}\left\|h_{Z}\right\|\leq J_{0},

(S.796)

where
{\rm diam}(Z)
is defined as
{\rm diam}(Z)=\max_{i,j\in Z}(d_{i,j})
.
We consider the imaginary-time evolution for an arbitrary
O_{X}
with
{\rm diam}(X)\leq k
and estimate

\displaystyle e^{-\tau H}O_{X}e^{\tau H}.

(S.797)

We first obtain the following lemma, which is a direct consequence of Ref.
[
179
, Corollary 1 therein]
.

Lemma 52
.

For the norm of imaginary-time evolved operator
\left\|e^{-\tau H}O_{X}e^{\tau H}\right\|
, we can prove the following upper bound:

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\tau H}O_{X}e^{\tau H}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau^{m}}{m!}\left\|{\rm ad}^{m}_{H}(O_{X})\right\|\leq 2m_{0}e^{m_{0}}\left\|O_{X}\right\|\leq e^{e^{\Theta(\tau)}}\left\|O_{X}\right\|,

(S.798)

where
m_{0}=e^{4e^{2}kJ_{0}\tau}
.

Proof of Lemma
52
.

We start from the statement in Ref.
[
179
, Corollary 1 therein]
as follows:

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{H}^{m}(O_{X})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|O_{X}\right\|\left(6kJ_{0}\right)^{m}\left(\frac{m+\tilde{l}_{0}}{W[e(m+\tilde{l}_{0})]}\right)^{m+\tilde{l}_{0}-\frac{m+\tilde{l}_{0}}{W[e(m+\tilde{l}_{0})]}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|O_{X}\right\|\left(\frac{6kJ_{0}m}{1.602\log(m)}\right)^{m}\quad\textrm{for}\quad\forall X\subset\Lambda\quad(|X|\leq k),

(S.799)

where
\tilde{l}_{0}=|X|/k=1
and
m\geq 2
is assumed.
By using the above inequality, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\tau^{m}}{m!}\left\|{\rm ad}^{m}_{H}(O_{X})\right\|\leq\left(\frac{e\tau}{m}\right)^{m}\left\|{\rm ad}^{m}_{H}(O_{X})\right\|\leq\begin{cases}e^{m_{0}}&\quad\textrm{for}\quad m<m_{0}=e^{4e^{2}kJ_{0}\tau},\\
e^{-m}&\quad\textrm{for}\quad m\geq m_{0},\end{cases}

(S.800)

which yields

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\tau H}O_{X}e^{\tau H}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau^{m}}{m!}\left\|{\rm ad}^{m}_{H}(O_{X})\right\|\leq\left\|O_{X}\right\|\left(m_{0}e^{m_{0}}+\frac{e^{-m_{0}}}{1-e^{-1}}\right).

(S.801)

We thus prove the main inequality (
S.798
).
This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
52
]

Also, we can prove the imaginary Lieb–Robinson bound as follows (see Ref.
[
179
, Lemma 3 therein]
):

Lemma 53
.

We can approximate
e^{-\tau H}O_{X}e^{\tau H}
by
\tilde{O}^{(\tau)}_{X[r]}
with the error of

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\tau H}O_{X}e^{\tau H}-\tilde{O}^{(\tau)}_{X[r]}\right\|\leq\frac{\zeta_{r}^{\lceil r/k\rceil}}{1-\zeta_{r}}\leq e^{-\mu r+e^{\Theta(\tau)}},

(S.802)

where
\zeta_{r}=6ek\tau/\log(\lceil r/k\rceil)
, and
\tilde{O}^{(\tau)}_{X[r]}
is constructed from the truncation of the expansion of

e^{-\tau H}O_{X}e^{\tau H}\leq\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-\tau)^{m}}{m!}{\rm ad}^{m}_{H}(O_{X})
, and
\mu
can be arbitrarily chosen.

In the proof below, we fully utilize the above two lemmas.

S.X.5.2
Proof of Proposition
51

We begin with defining
\tilde{O}
as

\displaystyle\tilde{O}:=e^{-\beta H/2}O_{X}e^{\beta H/2},

(S.803)

which gives
e^{-\beta H/2}O_{X}(H,t)e^{\beta H/2}=\tilde{O}(H,t)
.
We aim to upper-bound the norm of

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\beta H/2}\left\{O_{X}(H,t)-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left(O_{X}(H,t)\right)\right\}e^{\beta H/2}\right\|

\displaystyle=\exp\left(e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}\right)\left\|\tilde{O}(H,t)-e^{-\beta H/2}\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[O_{X}(H,t)\right]e^{\beta H/2}\right\|.

(S.804)

A difficulty stems from the fact that
e^{-\beta H/2}\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[O_{X}(H,t)\right]e^{\beta H/2}\neq\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[\tilde{O}(H,t)\right]
because
e^{-\beta H/2}
cannot be inserted in
\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left(\cdots\right)
.

In the following, we aim to upper-bound

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\beta H/2}\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[O_{X}(H,t)\right]e^{\beta H/2}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[\tilde{O}(H,t)\right]\right\|.

(S.805)

We introduce the operator
W_{\beta}
as

\displaystyle W_{\beta}=e^{-\beta H/2}e^{\beta(H_{L}+H_{L^{\rm c}})/2},

(S.806)

where
H_{L}
and
H_{L^{\rm c}}
are subset Hamiltonian of
H
on
L
and
L^{\rm c}
, respectively.
Using the above notation, we have

\displaystyle e^{-\beta H/2}\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[O_{X}(H,t)\right]e^{\beta H/2}

\displaystyle=W_{\beta}\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left(e^{-\beta(H_{L}+H_{L^{\rm c}})/2}O_{X}(H,t)e^{\beta(H_{L}+H_{L^{\rm c}})/2}\right)W_{\beta}^{-1}.

(S.807)

Furthermore, we calculate as

\displaystyle\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[e^{-\beta(H_{L}+H_{L^{\rm c}})/2}O_{X}(H,t)e^{\beta(H_{L}+H_{L^{\rm c}})/2}\right]

\displaystyle=\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[W_{\beta}^{-1}e^{-\beta H/2}O_{X}(H,t)e^{\beta H/2}W_{\beta}\right]

\displaystyle=\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[W_{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{O}(H,t)W_{\beta}\right]

\displaystyle=\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left(W_{\beta}^{-1}\left[\tilde{O}(H,t),W_{\beta}\right]\right)+\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[\tilde{O}(H,t)\right].

(S.808)

By combining Eqs. (
S.807
) and (
S.808
), we have

\displaystyle e^{-\beta H/2}\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[O_{X}(H,t)\right]e^{\beta H/2}

\displaystyle=W_{\beta}\left\{\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left(W_{\beta}^{-1}\left[\tilde{O}(H,t),W_{\beta}\right]\right)+\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[\tilde{O}(H,t)\right]\right\}W_{\beta}^{-1},

(S.809)

which yields

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\beta H/2}\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[O_{X}(H,t)\right]e^{\beta H/2}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[\tilde{O}(H,t)\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|W_{\beta}\right\|\cdot\left\|W_{\beta}^{-1}\right\|^{2}\cdot\left\|\left[\tilde{O}(H,t),W_{\beta}\right]\right\|+\left\|W_{\beta}^{-1}\right\|\cdot\left\|\left[W_{\beta},\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left(\tilde{O}(H,t)\right)\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}\right)\left(\left\|\left[\tilde{O}(H,t),W_{\beta}\right]\right\|+\left\|\left[W_{\beta},\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left(\tilde{O}(H,t)\right)\right]\right\|\right),

(S.810)

where we use the following inequality to derive
\left\|W_{\beta}\right\|=\left\|e^{-\beta H/2}e^{\beta(H_{L}+H_{L^{\rm c}})/2}\right\|\leq\exp\left(e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}\right)
:

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\beta H/2}e^{\beta(H_{L}+H_{L^{\rm c}})/2}\right\|=\left\|e^{-\beta H/2}e^{\beta H/2}e^{-(\beta/2)\int_{0}^{1}e^{-\beta xH/2}\partial h_{L}e^{\beta xH/2}}dx\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\sum_{Z:Z\cap L\neq\emptyset,\ Z\cap L^{\rm c}\neq\emptyset}\left\|e^{-\beta xH/2}h_{Z}e^{\beta xH/2}\right\|dx\right)\leq\exp\left(e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}\right)

(S.811)

with
\partial h_{L}=H-(H_{L}+H_{L^{\rm c}})=\sum_{Z:Z\cap L\neq\emptyset,\ Z\cap L^{\rm c}\neq\emptyset}h_{Z}
.

In the following, by using the imaginary Lieb–Robinson bound in Lemma
53
, we approximate
\tilde{O}
in Eq. (
S.803
) onto the subsets
X[r/3]
:

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{O}-\tilde{O}_{X[r/3]}\right\|\leq e^{-\mu r/3+e^{\Theta(\beta)}}.

(S.812)

In the same way, we approximate
W_{\beta}
onto the subset
L^{\rm c}[r/3]=(X[2r/3])^{\rm c}
, where the approximation error is given by

\displaystyle\left\|W_{\beta}-W_{\beta,L^{\rm c}[r/3]}\right\|\leq\exp\left(e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}\right)e^{-\mu r/3+e^{\Theta(\beta)}}\leq\exp\left(e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}\right)e^{-\mu r/3},

(S.813)

where we apply Lemma
53
to
e^{-\beta xH/2}\partial h_{L}e^{\beta xH/2}
and use the inequality (
S.114
) for

\displaystyle W_{\beta}=e^{-\beta H/2}e^{\beta(H_{L}+H_{L^{\rm c}})/2}=\mathcal{T}e^{-(\beta/2)\int_{0}^{1}e^{-\beta xH/2}\partial h_{L}e^{\beta xH/2}dx},

\displaystyle W_{\beta,L^{\rm c}[r/3]}=\mathcal{T}e^{-(\beta/2)\int_{0}^{1}\left(\partial h_{L}\right)^{(\beta x/2)}_{L^{\rm c}[r/3]}dx}

(S.814)

to derive

\displaystyle\left\|W_{\beta}-W_{\beta,L^{\rm c}[r/3]}\right\|\leq e^{(\beta/2)\int_{0}^{1}\left\|e^{-\beta xH/2}\partial h_{L}e^{\beta xH/2}\right\|dx}\int_{0}^{1}\left\|e^{-\beta xH/2}\partial h_{L}e^{\beta xH/2}-\left(\partial h_{L}\right)^{(\beta x/2)}_{L^{\rm c}[r/3]}\right\|dx.

(S.815)

Here, the operator
\left(\partial h_{L}\right)^{(\beta x/2)}_{L^{\rm c}[r/3]}
is an approximation of
e^{-\beta xH/2}\partial h_{L}e^{\beta xH/2}
onto
L^{\rm c}[r/3]
as has been defined in Lemma
53
.
Note that
\left\|e^{-\beta xH/2}\partial h_{L}e^{\beta xH/2}\right\|\leq e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}
can be derived from Lemma
52
.

By using the above upper bounds to the RHS of (
S.810
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\beta H/2}\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left(O_{X}(H,t)\right)e^{\beta H/2}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left(\tilde{O}(H,t)\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}\right)\Bigl(\left\|\left[\tilde{O}_{X[r/3]}(H,t),W_{\beta,L^{\rm c}[r/3]}\right]\right\|+\left\|\left[W_{\beta,L^{\rm c}[r/3]},\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left(\tilde{O}_{X[r/3]}(H,t)\right)\right]\right\|+e^{-\mu r/3}\Bigr).

(S.816)

Because of
X[2r/3]\cap L^{\rm c}[r/3]=\emptyset
from
X[r]=:L
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\left[\tilde{O}_{X[r/3]}(H,t),W_{\beta,L^{\rm c}[r/3]}\right]\right\|+\left\|\left[W_{\beta,L^{\rm c}[r/3]},\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left(\tilde{O}_{X[r/3]}(H,t)\right)\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 4\left\|W_{\beta,L^{\rm c}[r/3]}\right\|\cdot\left\|\tilde{O}_{X[r/3]}(H,t)-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{X[2r/3]^{\rm c}}\left(\tilde{O}_{X[r/3]}(H,t)\right)\right\|,

(S.817)

where we use that
\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{X[2r/3]^{\rm c}}\left[\tilde{O}_{X[r/3]}(H,t)\right]
is supported on the subset
X[2r/3]
.
Finally, by using the Lieb–Robinson bound, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{O}_{X[r/3]}(H,t)-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{X[2r/3]^{\rm c}}\left(\tilde{O}_{X[r/3]}(H,t)\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\tilde{O}_{X[r/3]}\right\|\min\left(2,e^{-\mu r/3+vt+\Theta(1)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}\min\left(1,e^{-\mu r/3+vt}\right).

(S.818)

By combining the inequalities (
S.816
), (
S.817
) and (
S.818
), we arrive at the inequality of

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\beta H/2}\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[O_{X}(H,t)\right]e^{\beta H/2}-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[\tilde{O}(H,t)\right]\right\|\leq\exp\left(e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}\right)\min\left(1,e^{-\mu r/3+vt}\right),

(S.819)

which gives the upper bound for the target quantity (
S.805
).
It reduces the inequality (
S.804
) to

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\beta H/2}\left\{O_{X}(H,t)-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[O_{X}(H,t)\right]\right\}e^{\beta H/2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}\right)\left[\left\|\tilde{O}(H,t)-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left(\tilde{O}(H,t)\right)\right\|+\min\left(1,e^{-\mu r/3+vt}\right)\right].

(S.820)

We finally estimate the norm of
\left\|\tilde{O}(H,t)-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[\tilde{O}(H,t)\right]\right\|
.
From the inequalities (
S.812
) and (
S.818
), we can also derive

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{O}(H,t)-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[\tilde{O}(H,t)\right]\right\|\leq 2\left\|\tilde{O}-\tilde{O}_{X[r/3]}\right\|+\left\|\tilde{O}_{X[r/3]}(H,t)-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[\tilde{O}_{X[r/3]}(H,t)\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}-\mu r/3}+\left\|\tilde{O}_{X[r/3]}(H,t)-\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{X[2r/3]^{\rm c}}\left[\tilde{O}_{X[r/3]}(H,t)\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}\min\left(1,e^{-\mu r/3+vt}\right).

(S.821)

By combining the two inequalities (
S.820
) and (
S.821
),
we prove the first main inequality (
S.788
).

For the proof of the other main inequalities in (
S.789
), we immediately prove

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\beta H/2}O_{X}(H,t)e^{\beta H/2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|e^{-\beta H/2}O_{X}e^{\beta H/2}\right\|\leq e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}.

(S.822)

Moreover, using the inequality (
S.788
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\beta H/2}\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[O_{X}(H,t)\right]e^{\beta H/2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|e^{-\beta H/2}O_{X}(H,t)e^{\beta H/2}\right\|+\left\|e^{-\beta H/2}O_{X}(H,t)e^{\beta H/2}-e^{-\beta H/2}\tilde{{\rm tr}}_{L^{\rm c}}\left[O_{X}(H,t)\right]e^{\beta H/2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}}+1\leq e^{e^{\Theta(\beta)}},

(S.823)

where we use the assumption that the second term in the second line is smaller than
1
.
We thus prove the second main inequalities in (
S.789
).
This completes the proof of Proposition
51
.
\square

Appendix A
Another proof for Lemma
18

We show another proof for Lemma
18
, which plays a key role in our analyses and has been proved based on the quantum belief propagation technique in Sec.
S.II
.
Here, we utilize the derivation of the operator logarithm in Ref.
[
180
,
181
]
, which gives

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dx}\log\left(\rho+x\delta\rho\right)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\rho_{x}+z\hat{1}}\delta\rho\frac{1}{\rho_{x}+z\hat{1}}dz,

(S.824)

where we let
\rho_{x}:=\rho+x\delta\rho
.
The purpose here is that we cannot refine the effective Hamiltonian theory based on the above perturbation method.
For the convenience of readers, we show the statement of Lemma
18
again:

Restatement of Lemma
18
.

For arbitrary operators in the form of

\displaystyle e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}},

(S.825)

we obtain the logarithm as

\displaystyle\log(e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}e^{\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}})=\beta e^{-2i\epsilon\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{A}}e^{2i\epsilon\mathcal{C}}+2\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2}),

(S.826)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}:=\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{\beta}(t)e^{i{\mathcal{A}}t}{\mathcal{B}}e^{-i{\mathcal{A}}t}dt,

(S.827)

where
g_{\beta}(t)
has been defined in the context of the belief propagation (
S.92
).

For the proof, we let

\displaystyle\rho=e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}},\quad\delta\rho=\left\{e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}},{\mathcal{B}}\right\},\quad x=\epsilon,

(S.828)

which reduces Eq. (
S.824
) to

\displaystyle\log\left(e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}+\epsilon\left\{e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}},{\mathcal{B}}\right\}\right)=\log\left(e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}\right)+\epsilon\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}+z\hat{1}}\left\{e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}},{\mathcal{B}}\right\}\frac{1}{e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}+z\hat{1}}dz+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2}),

(S.829)

where
\left\{e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}},{\mathcal{B}}\right\}=e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}{\mathcal{B}}+{\mathcal{B}}e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}
.
Using the spectral decomposition of
{\mathcal{B}}
by the eigenspace of
{\mathcal{A}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}=\sum_{l,m}\bra{m}{\mathcal{B}}\ket{l}\ket{l}\bra{m},

(S.830)

where
{\mathcal{A}}\ket{m}=a_{m}\ket{m}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}+z\hat{1}}\left\{e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}},{\mathcal{B}}\right\}\frac{1}{e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}+z\hat{1}}dz

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,m}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{e^{\beta a_{l}}+z}\left(e^{\beta a_{l}}+e^{\beta a_{m}}\right)\bra{l}{\mathcal{B}}\ket{m}\ket{l}\bra{m}\frac{1}{e^{\beta a_{m}}+z}dz

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,m}\frac{\beta(a_{l}-a_{m})}{e^{\beta a_{l}}-e^{\beta a_{m}}}\left(e^{\beta a_{l}}+e^{\beta a_{m}}\right)\bra{l}{\mathcal{B}}\ket{m}\ket{l}\bra{m}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,m}\frac{\beta(a_{l}-a_{m})}{\tanh\left[\beta(a_{l}-a_{m})/2\right]}\bra{l}{\mathcal{B}}\ket{m}\ket{l}\bra{m}

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}{\tanh\left(\beta{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}/2\right)}\sum_{l,m}\bra{l}{\mathcal{B}}\ket{m}\ket{l}\bra{m}=\frac{\beta{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}{\tanh\left(\beta{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}/2\right)}{\mathcal{B}},

(S.831)

where we use
{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\ket{l}\bra{m})=a_{l}-a_{m}
.
Using
{\mathcal{B}}_{\omega}=\sum_{i,j}\bra{a_{i}}{\mathcal{B}}\ket{a_{j}}\delta(a_{i}-a_{j}-\omega)\ket{a_{i}}\bra{a_{j}}
and Eq. (
S.96
) as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{\omega}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{A}},t)e^{-i\omega t}dt,\quad{\mathcal{B}}=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\mathcal{B}}_{\omega}d\omega,

(S.832)

we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\beta{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}{\tanh\left[\beta{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}/2\right]}{\mathcal{B}}=2\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{\beta\omega/2}{\tanh\left(\beta\omega/2\right)}{\mathcal{B}}_{\omega}d\omega=2\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dt{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{A}},t)\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{\beta\omega/2}{\tanh\left(\beta\omega/2\right)}e^{-i\omega t}d\omega.

(S.833)

To perform the Fourier transform, we use the decomposition of

\displaystyle\frac{\beta\omega/2}{\tanh\left(\beta\omega/2\right)}=1+\left(\frac{\beta\omega/2}{\tanh\left(\beta\omega/2\right)}-1\right)=1+\frac{1}{\omega}\left(\frac{\beta\omega/2}{\tanh\left(\beta\omega/2\right)}-1\right)\omega.

(S.834)

Therefore, from
\omega{\mathcal{B}}_{\omega}={\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{B}}_{\omega})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{\beta\omega/2}{\tanh\left(\beta\omega/2\right)}{\mathcal{B}}_{\omega}d\omega

\displaystyle={\mathcal{B}}+{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\omega}\left(\frac{\beta\omega/2}{\tanh\left(\beta\omega/2\right)}-1\right){\mathcal{B}}_{\omega}d\omega

\displaystyle={\mathcal{B}}+{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dt{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{A}},t)\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\omega}\left(\frac{\beta\omega/2}{\tanh\left(\beta\omega/2\right)}-1\right)e^{-i\omega t}d\omega

\displaystyle={\mathcal{B}}+i{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{\beta}(t){\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{A}},t)dt,={\mathcal{B}}+i\beta{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\mathcal{C}),

(S.835)

where we use Eq. (
S.104
) for
g_{\beta}(t)
and the definition of
\mathcal{C}
in Eq. (
S.826
).

By combining Eqs (
S.829
), (
S.831
), (
S.833
), and (
S.835
), we obtain the main inequality (
S.826
) as follows:

\displaystyle\log\left(e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}}+\epsilon\left\{e^{\beta{\mathcal{A}}},{\mathcal{B}}\right\}\right)=\beta{\mathcal{A}}+2\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}+2i\beta\epsilon{\rm ad}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\mathcal{C})+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})=\beta e^{-2i\epsilon\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{A}}e^{2i\epsilon\mathcal{C}}+2\epsilon{\mathcal{B}}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2}).

(S.836)

This completes the proof of Lemma
18
.
\square

Appendix B
Exponential
\tau\left\|V\right\|
dependence

From our analyses, we observe that the non-locality grows with
e^{\tau\left\|V\right\|}
as has been shown in Subtheorem
1
.
This point is a critical obstacle to improving the current theorem from the pairwise Markov property to the global Markov property, where we choose the size of the subset as large as the system size.
In this case, the dependence
e^{\tau\left\|V\right\|}
yields the exponential factor as
e^{\mathcal{O}(|AC|)}
in Theorem
4
.

For this purpose, one might consider that proof techniques in Subtheorem
1
might be further refined.
However, we can provide an explicit example of an exponentially enhanced effective interaction term due to a connection of quasi-local exponential operators.
In detail, one can prove the following statement:

Claim 54
.

Let
H
be a one-dimensional Hamiltonian that has short-range interactions as in Eq. (
S.7
).
Then, under an appropriate choice of the local operator
V
, the effective interaction for

\log\left(e^{V}e^{H}e^{V}\right)

can be exponentially amplified as
e^{\mathcal{O}(\left\|V\right\|)}
. Here, we can include the
\tau
by simply replacing
V\to\tau V^{\prime}
.

To show an example of the statement, let
H_{0}
be a Hamiltonian that consists of only one interaction term as

\displaystyle H_{0}=-2v_{0}\sigma_{1,z}+4v_{0}\varepsilon\sigma_{1,x}\otimes\sigma_{\ell,x},\quad\varepsilon=e^{-\mathcal{O}(\ell)},

(S.837)

where
\{\sigma_{i,x},\sigma_{i,y},\sigma_{i,z}\}_{i\in\Lambda}
is the Pauli matrix on the site
i
.
We note that the Hamiltonian
H_{0}
satisfies the condition of short-range interaction (
S.7
) as long as
v_{0}={\rm Poly}(\ell)
.
We then choose
V
as

\displaystyle V=\left\|V\right\|\sigma_{1,z},

(S.838)

and define
H_{\rm eff}
as

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}=\log(e^{V}e^{H_{0}}e^{V}).

(S.839)

Then, the Hamiltonian
H
is essentially a two-qubit Hamiltonian; hence, we can analytically calculate its form.
The analytic form is quite complicated, but we can approximate it as

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}\approx\left(-2v_{0}+2\left\|V\right\|\right)\sigma_{1,z}+\varepsilon e^{2\left\|V\right\|}\sigma_{1,x}\otimes\sigma_{\ell,x}\quad\textrm{for}\quad\left\|V\right\|\lesssim v_{0}.

(S.840)

We thus obtain the exponential enhancement of the long-range interaction between the sites
1
and
\ell
.

The mechanism behind the above analysis is as follows: To make the point more transparent, let us choose
\left\|V\right\|=v_{0}
, where
v_{0}
appears in
H_{0}
.
First of all, we find that the Hamiltonian
H_{0}
is approximately given by

\displaystyle H_{0}\approx\log\left(e^{\varepsilon\sigma_{1,x}\otimes\sigma_{\ell,x}}e^{-2v_{0}\sigma_{1,z}}e^{\varepsilon\sigma_{1,x}\otimes\sigma_{\ell,x}}\right),

(S.841)

and hence the Hamiltonian
H_{\rm eff}
is given by

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle\approx\log\left(e^{v_{0}\sigma_{1,z}}e^{\varepsilon\sigma_{1,x}\otimes\sigma_{\ell,x}}e^{-2v_{0}\sigma_{1,z}}e^{\varepsilon\sigma_{1,x}\otimes\sigma_{\ell,x}}e^{v_{0}\sigma_{1,z}}\right)

\displaystyle=\log\left[\exp\left(\varepsilon e^{v_{0}\sigma_{1,z}}\sigma_{1,x}e^{-v_{0}\sigma_{1,z}}\otimes\sigma_{\ell,x}\right)\exp\left(\varepsilon e^{-v_{0}\sigma_{1,z}}\sigma_{1,x}e^{v_{0}\sigma_{1,z}}\otimes\sigma_{\ell,x}\right)\right],

(S.842)

where we use
e^{v_{0}\sigma_{1,z}}e^{\varepsilon\sigma_{1,x}\otimes\sigma_{\ell,x}}e^{-v_{0}\sigma_{1,z}}=\exp\left[\varepsilon e^{v_{0}\sigma_{1,z}}\sigma_{1,x}e^{-v_{0}\sigma_{1,z}}\otimes\sigma_{\ell,x}\right]
.
Now, we have

\displaystyle\left\|e^{\pm v_{0}\sigma_{1,z}}\sigma_{1,x}e^{\mp v_{0}\sigma_{1,z}}\right\|=\left\|\cosh(2v_{0})\sigma_{1,x}\pm i\sinh(2v_{0})\sigma_{1,y}\right\|,

(S.843)

which grows exponentially with
v_{0}
.
In this way, if
e^{V}e^{H_{0}}e^{V}
includes imaginary time evolutions for
V
, it induces the exponential growth of the amplitude of the effective interactions.

The model (
S.837
) itself is far from the practical many-body systems.
However, this mechanism may be feasible in more natural quantum many-body systems.
Therefore, to avoid exponential growth, we have to impose additional constraints on the form of the operator
V
*10
*10

*10

Indeed, if we choose
V
as the PTP operator, the imaginary time evolution as in Eq. (
S.842
) may not appear since the original Hamiltonian
H_{0}
does not act on the ancilla subspace and cannot be expressed in a form including
V
.
.

Appendix C
Continuity bound on the logarithm of operators based on relative error

C.1
Setup and Main result

For the readers’ convenience, we show the setup again so that the section can be closed independently.

Without loss of generality, we can let two operators
\rho
and
\sigma
be density matrices with
{\rm tr}(\rho)=1
and
{\rm tr}(\sigma)=1
.
In general, we get the difference between the logarithms of
c_{1}\rho
and
c_{2}\sigma
as

\displaystyle\log(c_{1}\rho)-\log(c_{2}\sigma)=\log(c_{1})-\log(c_{2})+\log(\rho)-\log(\sigma).

(S.844)

We restate the definition of the relative error as follows:

Definition
1
[Relative error].

We define the relative error
\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)
between
\rho
and
\sigma
as

\displaystyle\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma):=\sup_{\ket{\psi}}\frac{|\bra{\psi}\rho-\sigma\ket{\psi}|}{\bra{\psi}\rho\ket{\psi}},

(S.845)

where the
\sup_{\ket{\psi}}
is taken for all the set of quantum states
\ket{\psi}
.

Our purpose now is to derive a continuity inequality for
\left\|\log(\rho)-\log(\sigma)\right\|
based on the relative error of
\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma)
, which has been given as follows:

Theorem
7
.

Under the condition that

\displaystyle\varepsilon=\max\left[\delta_{\rm R}(\rho,\sigma),\delta_{\rm R}(\sigma,\rho)\right]\leq\frac{1}{2},

(S.846)

we obtain the upper bound as

\displaystyle\left\|\log(\sigma)-\log(\rho)\right\|\leq\varepsilon\left[\frac{4\log[2\lambda^{-1}_{\min}(\rho)]}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{e\log[2\lambda^{-1}_{\min}(\rho)]}{2\pi}\right)+23\right],

(S.847)

where
\lambda^{-1}_{\min}(\rho)
is the minimum eigenvalue of
\rho
.

C.2
Proof of Theorem
7

Throughout the proof, we denote
\sigma-\rho
by
\delta\rho
, i.e.,
\delta\rho:=\sigma-\rho
.
We first note that the condition (
S.846
) immediately gives

\displaystyle|\bra{\psi}\delta\rho\ket{\psi}|\leq\varepsilon\bra{\psi}\rho\ket{\psi},\quad|\bra{\psi}\delta\rho\ket{\psi}|\leq\varepsilon\bra{\psi}\sigma\ket{\psi}

(S.848)

from Definition
1
.
For the proof, we start with the equation of

\displaystyle\log(\sigma)-\log(\rho)=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{dx}\log\left(\rho+x\delta\rho\right)dx.

(S.849)

Using Ref.
[
180
]
*11
*11

*11

In the reference, there is a typo in the first equation (1) therein.
The second term in the RHS of the equation should be
t\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{A+zI}B\frac{1}{A+zI}dz

instead of
t\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{B+zI}A\frac{1}{B+zI}dz
.
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dx}\log\left(\rho+x\delta\rho\right)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\rho_{x}+z\hat{1}}\delta\rho\frac{1}{\rho_{x}+z\hat{1}}dz,\quad\rho_{x}:=\rho+x\delta\rho.

(S.850)

We note that the same inequality has been used in Eq. (
S.824
).

We now define

\displaystyle\rho_{x}=e^{H_{x}}=\sum_{m}e^{E_{m}}\ket{E_{m}}\bra{E_{m}},

(S.851)

and obtain the following bound:

\displaystyle\frac{|\bra{\psi}\delta\rho\ket{\psi}|}{\bra{\psi}\rho_{x}\ket{\psi}}\leq\varepsilon\longrightarrow|\bra{\psi}\delta\rho\ket{\psi}|\leq\varepsilon\bra{\psi}\rho_{x}\ket{\psi}

(S.852)

for an arbitrary quantum state
\ket{\psi}
.
It is immediately obtained from the condition (
S.848
) by using

\displaystyle|\bra{\psi}\delta\rho\ket{\psi}|\leq x\varepsilon\bra{\psi}\rho\ket{\psi}+(1-x)\varepsilon\bra{\psi}\sigma\ket{\psi}=\varepsilon\bra{\psi}\rho_{x}\ket{\psi}.

(S.853)

We then calculate

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\rho_{x}+z\hat{1}}\delta\rho\frac{1}{\rho_{x}+z\hat{1}}dz

\displaystyle=\sum_{m,n}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{e^{E_{m}}+z}\bra{E_{m}}\delta\rho\ket{E_{n}}\frac{1}{e^{E_{n}}+z}\ket{E_{m}}\bra{E_{n}}dz

\displaystyle=\sum_{m,n}\frac{E_{m}-E_{n}}{e^{E_{m}}-e^{E_{n}}}\bra{E_{m}}\delta\rho\ket{E_{n}}\ket{E_{m}}\bra{E_{n}}.

(S.854)

Note that for arbitrary quantum state
\ket{\psi}=\sum_{m}a_{m}\ket{E_{m}}
, we have from the inequality (
S.852
)

\displaystyle\left|\Braket{\psi|\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\rho_{x}+z\hat{1}}\delta\rho\frac{1}{\rho_{x}+z\hat{1}}dz|\psi}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m,n}\frac{E_{m}-E_{n}}{e^{E_{m}}-e^{E_{n}}}\left|\bra{E_{m}}\delta\rho\ket{E_{n}}\right|\cdot\left|a_{m}\right|\cdot\left|a_{n}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m,n}\frac{E_{m}-E_{n}}{e^{E_{m}}-e^{E_{n}}}\frac{\bra{E_{m}}\delta\rho\ket{E_{m}}+\bra{E_{n}}\delta\rho\ket{E_{n}}}{2}\cdot\left|a_{m}\right|\cdot\left|a_{n}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\sum_{m,n}\frac{E_{m}-E_{n}}{e^{E_{m}}-e^{E_{n}}}\frac{\bra{E_{m}}\rho_{x}\ket{E_{m}}+\bra{E_{n}}\rho_{x}\ket{E_{n}}}{2}\cdot\left|a_{m}\right|\cdot\left|a_{n}\right|

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\sum_{m,n}\frac{E_{m}-E_{n}}{e^{E_{m}}-e^{E_{n}}}\frac{e^{E_{m}}+e^{E_{n}}}{2}\cdot\left|a_{m}\right|\cdot\left|a_{n}\right|,

(S.855)

where we use
\frac{E_{m}-E_{n}}{e^{E_{m}}-e^{E_{n}}}\geq 0
for arbitrary
E_{m},E_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}
and the Cauthy–Schwarz inequality as

\displaystyle\left|\bra{E_{m}}\delta\rho\ket{E_{n}}\right|\leq\sqrt{\bra{E_{m}}\delta\rho\ket{E_{m}}\bra{E_{n}}\delta\rho\ket{E_{n}}}\leq\frac{\bra{E_{m}}\delta\rho\ket{E_{m}}+\bra{E_{n}}\delta\rho\ket{E_{n}}}{2}.

(S.856)

Therefore, by defining the operator
\bar{\mathcal{R}}
as

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{R}}:=\sum_{m,n}\frac{E_{m}-E_{n}}{e^{E_{m}}-e^{E_{n}}}\frac{e^{E_{m}}+e^{E_{n}}}{2}\ket{E_{m}}\bra{E_{n}},

(S.857)

we have

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\rho_{x}+z\hat{1}}\delta\rho\frac{1}{\rho_{x}+z\hat{1}}dz\right\|\leq\varepsilon\left\|\bar{\mathcal{R}}\right\|=\varepsilon\sup_{P}\left|{\rm tr}\left(P\bar{\mathcal{R}}\right)\right|,

(S.858)

where
P
is an arbitrary projector onto a quantum state.
For the trace of
{\rm tr}\left(P\bar{\mathcal{R}}\right)
, we aim to prove the following lemma:

Lemma 55
.

Under the definition of Eq. (
S.857
) for
\bar{\mathcal{R}}
, we obtain the upper bound of

\displaystyle\left|{\rm tr}\left(\bar{\mathcal{R}}P\right)\right|\leq\frac{4\left\|H_{x}\right\|}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{e\left\|H_{x}\right\|}{2\pi}\right)+23,

(S.859)

for an arbitrary projection
P
.

By applying Lemma
55
to the inequality (
S.858
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\rho_{x}+z\hat{1}}\delta\rho\frac{1}{\rho_{x}+z\hat{1}}dz\right\|\leq\varepsilon\left\|\bar{\mathcal{R}}\right\|=\varepsilon\left[\frac{4\left\|H_{x}\right\|}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{e\left\|H_{x}\right\|}{2\pi}\right)+23\right].

(S.860)

Here we have
\left\|H_{x}\right\|=\log[\lambda^{-1}_{\min}(\rho_{x})]
since all the eigenvalues of
\rho_{x}
are smaller than or equal to
1
.
We can also obtain the lower bound of

\displaystyle\inf_{\ket{\psi}}\bra{\psi}\rho_{x}\ket{\psi}

\displaystyle=\inf_{\ket{\psi}}\left(\bra{\psi}\rho\ket{\psi}+x\bra{\psi}\sigma-\rho\ket{\psi}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\ket{\psi}}\left(\bra{\psi}\rho\ket{\psi}-x|\bra{\psi}\sigma-\rho\ket{\psi}|\right)

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\ket{\psi}}\left(\bra{\psi}\rho\ket{\psi}-x\varepsilon\bra{\psi}\rho\ket{\psi}\right)\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\min}(\rho),

(S.861)

where we use
1-x\varepsilon\geq 1-\varepsilon\geq 1/2
from
\varepsilon\leq 1/2
.
We thus obtain
\left\|H_{x}\right\|\leq\log[2\lambda^{-1}_{\min}(\rho)]
for
0\leq x\leq 1
.

By combining Eqs. (
S.849
) and (
S.850
) with the inequality (
S.860
) and
\left\|H_{x}\right\|\leq\log[2\lambda^{-1}_{\min}(\rho)]
, we prove the main inequality (
S.847
).
This completes the proof of Theorem
7
.
\square

C.2.1
Proof of Lemma
55

We first rewrite
\bar{\mathcal{R}}
as

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{R}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m,n}\frac{E_{m}-E_{n}}{e^{E_{m}}-e^{E_{n}}}\frac{e^{E_{m}}+e^{E_{n}}}{2}\ket{E_{m}}\bra{E_{n}}=\frac{{\rm ad}_{H_{x}}\left(e^{{\rm ad}_{H_{x}}}+1\right)}{2\left(e^{{\rm ad}_{H_{x}}}-1\right)},

(S.862)

where we use
f(E_{m}-E_{n})\ket{E_{m}}\bra{E_{n}}=f({\rm ad}_{H_{x}})\ket{E_{m}}\bra{E_{n}}
because of
{\rm ad}_{H_{x}}\ket{E_{m}}\bra{E_{n}}=(E_{m}-E_{n})\ket{E_{m}}\bra{E_{n}}
.
We thus obtain

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{R}}P=\frac{{\rm ad}_{H_{x}}\left(e^{{\rm ad}_{H_{x}}}+1\right)}{2\left(e^{{\rm ad}_{H_{x}}}-1\right)}P=\frac{{\rm ad}_{H_{x}}/2}{\tanh\left({\rm ad}_{H_{x}}/2\right)}P.

(S.863)

We second adopt the spectral decomposition as in Ref.
[
26
, Appendix A therein]

\displaystyle P_{\omega}=\sum_{m,n}\bra{E_{m}}P\ket{E_{n}}\delta(E_{m}-E_{n}-\omega)\ket{E_{m}}\bra{E_{n}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow P_{\omega}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}P(H_{x},t)e^{-i\omega t}dt,\quad P=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}P_{\omega}d\omega.

(S.864)

By applying Eq. (
S.864
) to Eq. (
S.862
) with
{\rm ad}_{H_{x}}P_{\omega}=\omega P_{\omega}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{R}}P

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{\omega/2}{\tanh(\omega/2)}P_{\omega}d\omega=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left[\frac{1}{\omega}\left(\frac{\omega/2}{\tanh(\omega/2)}-1\right)\omega P_{\omega}+P_{\omega}\right]d\omega

\displaystyle=P+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm ad}_{H_{x}}[P(H_{x},t)]\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\omega}\left(\frac{\omega/2}{\tanh(\omega/2)}-1\right)e^{-i\omega t}d\omega dt

\displaystyle=P+i\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g(t){\rm ad}_{H_{x}}[P(H_{x},t)]dt,

(S.865)

where
g(t)
is defined by the Fourier transform of

\displaystyle g(t)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\omega}\left(\frac{\omega/2}{\tanh(\omega/2)}-1\right)e^{-i\omega t}d\omega={\rm sign}(t)\frac{e^{-2\pi|t|}}{1-e^{-2\pi|t|}}.

(S.866)

The remaining task is to estimate the upper bound of

\displaystyle\left|{\rm tr}\left(\bar{\mathcal{R}}P\right)\right|\leq 1+\left\|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g(t){\rm ad}_{H_{x}}[P(H_{x},t)]dt\right\|_{1},

(S.867)

where we use
{\rm tr}(P)=1
from the condition. We first note that the following simple upper bound as

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g(t){\rm ad}_{H_{x}}[P(H_{x},t)]dt\right\|_{1}\leq\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|g(t)|\cdot\left\|{\rm ad}_{H_{x}}(P)\right\|_{1}dt\leq 2\left\|H_{x}\right\|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|g(t)|dt

(S.868)

can NOT be used since the integral of
|g(t)|
is divergent because of
|g(t)|\propto 1/|t|
for
|t|\ll 1
.
Note that the Hölder inequality gives
\left\|{\rm ad}_{H_{x}}(P)\right\|_{1}\leq 2\left\|H_{x}\right\|\cdot\left\|P\right\|_{1}=2\left\|H_{x}\right\|
.

To avoid the divergence, we adopt the prescription in Ref.
[
26
, (D. 83) and (D.84)]
.
We first note that the following decomposition holds for an arbitrary operator
O
in general:

\displaystyle O(H_{x},t)=O+\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\lambda}O(H_{x},\lambda t)d\lambda=O+it\int_{0}^{1}{\rm ad}_{H_{x}}[O(H_{x},\lambda t)]d\lambda.

(S.869)

By applying it to
{\rm ad}_{H_{x}}[P(H_{x},t)]=[{\rm ad}_{H_{x}}(P)](H_{x},t)
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm ad}_{H_{x}}[P(H_{x},t)]={\rm ad}_{H_{x}}(P)+it\int_{0}^{1}{\rm ad}^{2}_{H_{x}}[P(H_{x},\lambda t)]d\lambda.

(S.870)

Here, we utilize the above decomposition for the integrals (
S.867
) in
|t|\leq\delta t
, which yields

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g(t){\rm ad}_{H_{x}}[P(H_{x},t)]dt=\int_{|t|>\delta t}g(t){\rm ad}_{H_{x}}[P(H_{x},t)]dt+\int_{|t|\leq\delta t}itg(t)\int_{0}^{1}{\rm ad}^{2}_{H_{x}}[P(H_{x},\lambda t)]d\lambda dt,

(S.871)

where we use
\int_{|t|\leq\delta t}g(t)=0
since
g(t)
in Eq. (
S.866
) is an odd function.
We thus prove

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g(t){\rm ad}_{H_{x}}[P(H_{x},t)]dt\right\|_{1}\leq 2\left\|H_{x}\right\|\int_{|t|>\delta t}|g(t)|dt+4\left\|H_{x}\right\|^{2}\int_{|t|\leq\delta t}|tg(t)|dt,

(S.872)

where we use
\left\|{\rm ad}_{H_{x}}(P)\right\|_{1}\leq 2\left\|H_{x}\right\|
and
\left\|{\rm ad}^{2}_{H_{x}}(P)\right\|_{1}\leq 4\left\|H_{x}\right\|
from
\left\|P\right\|_{1}={\rm tr}(P)=1
.

Using Lemma
26
with
\beta=1
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{|t|>\delta t}|g(t)|dt\leq\frac{2}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{1}{2\pi\delta t}\right),\quad\int_{|t|\leq\delta t}|tg(t)|dt\leq\frac{\delta t}{\pi},

(S.873)

where we assume
\delta t\leq 1/(4\pi)
.
By applying the above bound to the inequality (
S.872
), we derive

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g(t){\rm ad}_{H_{x}}[P(H_{x},t)]dt\right\|_{1}\leq\frac{4\left\|H_{x}\right\|}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{1}{2\pi\delta t}\right)+\frac{4\delta t}{\pi}\left\|H_{x}\right\|^{2}.

(S.874)

Finally, by choosing
\delta t
as
\min\left[1/(4\pi),1/\left\|H_{x}\right\|\right]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g(t){\rm ad}_{H_{x}}[P(H_{x},t)]dt\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}8\log(2)+16&\quad\textrm{for}\quad\left\|H_{x}\right\|\leq 4\pi\\
\frac{4\left\|H_{x}\right\|}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{e\left\|H_{x}\right\|}{2\pi}\right)&\quad\textrm{for}\quad\left\|H_{x}\right\|>4\pi\end{cases}

\displaystyle\leq\max\left[22,\frac{4\left\|H_{x}\right\|}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{e\left\|H_{x}\right\|}{2\pi}\right)\right].

(S.875)

Therefore, from the inequality (
S.867
), we prove the inequality of

\displaystyle\left|{\rm tr}\left(\bar{\mathcal{R}}P\right)\right|\leq 1+\max\left[22,\frac{4\left\|H_{x}\right\|}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{e\left\|H_{x}\right\|}{2\pi}\right)\right],

(S.876)

which reduces to the main inequality (
S.859
).
This completes the proof of Lemma
55
.
\square
